# scripts/ - Reference

> **Source of truth for every script in this folder.** This README MUST be
> updated whenever a script is added, renamed, removed, or materially changed.

Scripts are organized by lifecycle. Entry-point orchestrators stay at the root;
everything else lives in a subfolder:

```
scripts/
├── seed-env.sh       ← main data-seed entry point (stays at root)
├── test-timeout.ts   ← hard wall-clock guard wrapping every `node --test`,
│                       and the run READER (cancelled files + case floor)
├── test-report-logic.ts    ← pure TAP readers used by test-timeout.ts
├── test-case-floors.json   ← per-invocation executed-case floors
├── run-smoke.ts      ← SMOKE TIER runner (`yarn test:smoke`)
├── smoke-list.txt    ← smoke-tier manifest (workspace-relative globs)
├── tsconfig.json     ← tsx/ts config for all .ts scripts
├── seed/             ← bootstrap data (run once per environment)
├── migrations/       ← one-off data migrations (run once, then archive)
├── ops/              ← ongoing operational tools (reruns are safe)
├── data/             ← enrichment / fix utilities (ad-hoc)
└── quant/            ← PYTHON. bulk research reads (backtests)
```

Authoritative cross-references:
[`docs/reference/scripts-reference.md`](../docs/reference/scripts-reference.md)
has workflow recipes; this file is the per-script index.

## Root (entry points)

| Script                          | Purpose                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `seed-env.sh`                   | Full multi-env database seed (QA / Prod). Orchestrates 15 steps across `seed/` and `data/`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Fetches `DATABASE_URL_{ENV}` from GCP Secret Manager. Supports `--step N`, `--from N`, `--url`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `test-timeout.ts`               | Hard wall-clock guard wrapping every workspace `node --test` run. Spawns the test command in its own process group and SIGKILLs the whole group after a deadline so a wedged suite can never orphan/run for hours (root cause of an observed 11h run). **It also READS the run (MONEY-1489)**: it tees a second TAP reporter to a temp file and, after the child exits, (a) NAMES any file node CANCELLED — node already exits 1 on one, but `# fail 0` sitting beside `# cancelled 2`, plus an annotation carrying no filename, left three CI runs undiagnosed — and (b) enforces a per-invocation executed-case FLOOR. Every workspace routes through here, so this covers CI _and_ `yarn verify:push`; and it sits BELOW turbo, so the TAP it reads is unprefixed (a `^not ok` grep in the workflow is blind in the multi-task shard, where turbo prefixes every line).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Each workspace `"test"` script calls it: `node --import tsx ../../scripts/test-timeout.ts <node args>`. Cap via `TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 900000 = 15 min). Exit 124 on timeout. Prints one `[test-timeout] <key> — N cases (P passed, S skipped, C cancelled), floor F` line per run.                                                                             |
| `test-report-logic.ts`          | Pure TAP readers behind the above — `parseTapSummary` / `evaluateRun` / `floorKey`. Kept free of fs and spawn so the verdict ladder is testable from fixtures rather than only by a source scan. Tested by `packages/core/__tests__/ops/test-report-logic.test.ts`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Imported by `test-timeout.ts`. Also carries a default export, because `scripts/` is CJS and the test lives in an ESM package.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `test-case-floors.json`         | Per-invocation floors for the EXECUTED case count, keyed `"<workspace> <glob>"` — a workspace may invoke the wrapper twice (`@usemoney/core` runs `*.test.ts` then `*.spawntest.ts`) and one floor across both would be met by either. Catches the one class nothing else can: cases that were never REGISTERED, which exits **0** with every counter green (MONEY-1093 — 45 files / 3,093 cases).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Counts `pass + skipped` (so an env-gated suite that legitimately skips does not turn the gate red) and fails BELOW the floor; adding tests is always free. An unrecorded key reports and does not fail. Re-measure with `yarn turbo run test --concurrency=1` and read the `[test-timeout]` lines.                                                                  |
| `run-smoke.ts`                  | SMOKE-tier runner (`yarn test:smoke`). Reads `smoke-list.txt`, groups the globs by their leading workspace dir, expands them, and spawns one `node --import tsx --test` per workspace WITH THAT WORKSPACE AS CWD (so the `@/*` alias + `../../` imports resolve). Each run is wrapped in `test-timeout.ts`. Aggregates pass/fail + prints wall-clock time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `SMOKE_LIST=<path>` overrides the manifest. Tighter `TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 120000 = 2 min). Exit non-zero on any failure or empty glob. See "Test tiers" below.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `smoke-list.txt`                | The smoke-tier MANIFEST: one workspace-relative test glob per line (`#` comments / blank lines ignored). Edit this to add/remove a critical-path file from the smoke subset.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | When you change an entry, update the "Test tiers" section below in the same PR.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `gen-test-coverage.ts`          | TEST-COVERAGE.md freshness checker (the MONEY-838 "durable fix"): walks the yarn workspaces for `*.test.ts(x)` **and `*.spawntest.ts(x)`** files and compares the count against the doc's "Total Test Files" headline (MONEY-1316 added the second suffix: `packages/core` runs `node --test` in TWO lanes — 30 s for the normal files, 180 s for the process-spawning ones — and a file moves between them by being RENAMED, so a matcher that knew only `.test.ts` would have dropped those 4 files from the inventory in silence. `foo.spawntest.ts` does NOT end in `.test.ts`; one shared `TEST_FILE_SUFFIX_SRC` feeds the walker, the stem matcher and the phantom-row citation regex so a third suffix cannot be taught to one and forgotten in the others); flags phantom rows (the doc naming a file that does not exist); and **gates on unlisted files** — MONEY-1238 turned "N test file(s) never mentioned" from an ungated warning into a RATCHET against `UNMENTIONED_BASELINE` (149 pre-existing offenders frozen; only ADDITIONS fail, removals warn). Without it, bumping the headline without adding the row passed a BLOCKING CI step. Every gate now reports and the process exits ONCE at the end, so the count check can no longer hide the other two. Case counts stay runtime-derived — deliberately not guessed statically. | `yarn test:coverage-check` (`--check`: exit 1 on headline drift, phantom rows, or a NEW unlisted file; used by `.github/workflows/test.yml`, **BLOCKING as of MONEY-1157** — the headline was restamped to a measured 923 files / 24,320 cases, and the two contradictory stanzas it used to carry were collapsed to one). No args = report only.                   |
| `gen-claude-md-index.ts`        | Machine-readable index of every CLAUDE.md → `docs/reference/claude-md-index.json` (per-file path/title/headings/size/last-commit + env-var and command extractions from the root file). GENERATED artifact — the CLAUDE.md files stay the source of truth; output is timestamp-free so regeneration only diffs on real content change.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `yarn docs:claude-index`. Re-run after editing any CLAUDE.md. Not CI-gated.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `gen-chart-preload-manifest.ts` | MONEY-1383 (L2). Parses the bundle list out of `apps/web/public/charting_library/charting_library.standalone.js` (the loader carries it as a single-quoted JSON array it writes into the widget's iframe) and emits the `<link rel="preload">` hrefs to `apps/web/lib/charts/preload-manifest.json`, which the stock / ETF / paper-trading SERVER pages render through `components/charts/ChartPreloadLinks.tsx`. Those tags sit OUTSIDE `<PageGuard>`, so the 2.5 MB library starts downloading with the document instead of after the ~0.6s `/api/subscription/access` round trip. NOT hand-maintainable: `bundles/library.<contenthash>.js` gets a new hash on every library upgrade and a stale preload href **404s silently** — no error, no monitor, the page just quietly goes back to paying the ~1.5s cold download. Excludes the `__LANG__` chunk (locale-substituted at runtime) and the `.css` chunk (`as: "style"` in a document that never applies the sheet, for 158 KB).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Runs automatically as the last step of `yarn sync:chart`. Standalone: `npx tsx scripts/gen-chart-preload-manifest.ts`. `apps/web/__tests__/charts/chart-preload-manifest.test.ts` asserts every href EXISTS on disk and that the checked-in file matches a fresh parse — that is what turns a forgotten regeneration into a failing build rather than a silent 404. |
| `tsconfig.json`                 | Shared tsx config for all `.ts` scripts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `include: ["./**/*.ts"]` - covers all subfolders.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

Usage:

```bash
./scripts/seed-env.sh qa                    # full seed on QA
./scripts/seed-env.sh prod --step 3         # only sector enrichment on prod
./scripts/seed-env.sh --url "mongodb+srv://..." --from 5   # from step 5 onward
```

## Test tiers

The test suite runs in three tiers (MONEY-620). All three use the same runner
(`node:test` + `tsx`, wrapped by `test-timeout.ts`); they differ only in WHICH
files run and which env they need.

| Tier           | Command           | What it runs                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Env needed                                                  | Speed           |
| -------------- | ----------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- |
| **smoke**      | `yarn test:smoke` | The critical-path subset in `smoke-list.txt`: FIFO pairing, calc-suite, AI guardrails, webhook signature/idempotency + the `*-e2e` routes, demo routes, the `security/*` suite, subscription access, contract tests. | None (pure / mocked; contract tests use undici `MockAgent`) | < 60s (target)  |
| **regression** | `yarn test`       | The WHOLE suite — `turbo run test` over every workspace's `__tests__/**`. This is the CI merge gate.                                                                                                                 | None (mocked)                                               | minutes         |
| **full**       | `yarn test:full`  | Regression **+** the env-gated `tests/integration/**` (`RUN_INTEGRATION_TESTS=true`). The `*-e2e` route tests already run inside regression.                                                                         | QA DB + Redis (`DATABASE_URL`, `REDIS_URL`) for integration | minutes (+ I/O) |

How the smoke tier is wired (the chosen mechanism):

- `smoke-list.txt` is a plain-text manifest of WORKSPACE-RELATIVE globs (one per
  line; `#` comments + blank lines ignored). It is decoupled from code so the
  critical-path set is reviewable in one place.
- `run-smoke.ts` groups those globs by their leading workspace dir, expands them
  (tinyglobby), and runs ONE `node --import tsx --test` per workspace **with
  that workspace as CWD**. CWD-per-workspace is load-bearing — it is what makes
  the `@/*` tsconfig alias (and `../../` relative imports) resolve the same way
  they do under each workspace's own `yarn test`.
- Each spawned run is wrapped in `test-timeout.ts`, so the orphan/wedge
  SIGKILL guard still applies (smoke uses a tighter 2-min `TEST_TIMEOUT_MS`).

```bash
yarn test:smoke                  # fast critical-path signal (CI + local pre-flight)
yarn test                        # full regression (CI merge gate)
yarn test:full                   # regression + env-gated integration (needs QA DB/Redis)
SMOKE_LIST=path/to/list.txt yarn test:smoke   # run an alternate manifest
```

> **Keep `smoke-list.txt` fast and pure.** Only add files that have no live
> network / DB / Redis dependency and that run quickly. When you add or remove a
> smoke entry, update this section in the same PR.

**Coverage** (`yarn test:coverage`) is a separate, NON-BLOCKING c8 run over
`apps/web` that emits a `text-summary`. Real EXECUTED line coverage is ~14%
today — deliberately NOT enforced (see the TODO in `.github/workflows/test.yml`
and the note in `TEST-COVERAGE.md`).

## `seed/` - Bootstrap data (run once per environment)

These populate reference data tables. Run in numbered order when bootstrapping a
new environment (`seed-env.sh` does this automatically).

| #   | Script                     | Purpose                                                                                                                          |
| --- | -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| -   | `seed-all.ts`              | Legacy orchestrator (prefer `seed-env.sh`). Runs instruments → MF → sectors → prices sequentially.                               |
| 1   | `seed-instruments.ts`      | Zerodha master dump → `InstrumentMapping` table (~23K NSE/BSE symbols).                                                          |
| 2   | `seed-mf.ts`               | AMFI NAV feed → `MutualFundMaster` (~14K active funds).                                                                          |
| 3   | `seed-sectors.ts`          | Yahoo Finance → `InstrumentMapping.sector` enrichment. Skips instruments with existing sector.                                   |
| 4   | `seed-mf-metadata.ts`      | `MF_METADATA_PROVIDER` (AMFI) → full MF metadata enrichment (categories, returns, AUM).                                          |
| 5   | `seed-prices.ts`           | Yahoo Finance → `LatestQuote` for all active instruments.                                                                        |
| 6   | `seed-stock-ratios.ts`     | Tickertape → `StockRatios` (P/E, P/B, ROE, etc.).                                                                                |
| 7   | `seed-stock-financials.ts` | Tickertape → `StockFinancials` (income statement, balance sheet).                                                                |
| 8   | `seed-stock-profiles.ts`   | Yahoo Finance → `StockProfile` (company summary, employees, sector).                                                             |
| 9   | `seed-mf-tickertape.ts`    | Tickertape → MF overview + holdings enrichment.                                                                                  |
| 10  | `seed-etf-data.ts`         | Yahoo Finance → ETF profiles + key metrics.                                                                                      |
| 11  | `seed-stock-documents.ts`  | Multi-source document fetcher (Tickertape, NSE filings, NSE reports, Screener.in). Supports `--source` flag. Stores PDFs in GCS. |
| -   | `seed-glossary.ts`         | Seeds the `GlossaryTerm` table from a curated list. Separate from the numbered pipeline.                                         |

| `purge-parse-checkpoints.ts`         | **MONEY-1175** - delete `parse:<family>` rows from `feed_backfill_progress` so archived files re-parse. Needed because fixing a WRITER is not enough when the checkpoints recorded the loss as success: `writeCash` skipped every occupied `(instrumentId, date)` cell — Yahoo-owned ones included — then marked the day `DONE`, so the file was never read again. Evidence: `parse:nse-cm-udiff-bhavcopy` was checkpointed through 2026-08-11 while its rows stopped at 2026-03-12. Deleting a checkpoint destroys no data; the worst case is a re-parse the corrected writer treats as a no-op. **Families must be named explicitly — there is deliberately no `--all`**, since purging everything would re-parse the ~100M-row derivatives archive for a bug that never touched it. `--families=a,b`, `--from`, `--to`, `--apply`. 2026-08-12 (8,544 purged)                |
| `repair-daily-price-candles.ts`      | **MONEY-1175** - repair undrawable OHLC rows. Applies the repair matching the CAUSE, which is the whole point: Yahoo under-reports the RANGE (`o==h==l` with a real close) so low/high are widened to span observed prices, while NSE's 1995-96 files put **PREVCLOSE in the OPEN column** (verified against the archived bytes — `ACC,EQ,3338.35,…,3338.35`, and OPEN/PREVCLOSE diverge from 1997) so there `open` is rebuilt from `[low, high]` and the range is left alone. Widening the NSE case would assert a trade at yesterday's price; clamping the Yahoo case would discard a real one. A close-only row (`o=h=l=0`) collapses to a flat bar, the shape `udf/history` already synthesises for MF NAVs. Shares one `repairCandle` dispatch with the ingest path so a re-parse cannot undo it. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT.** `--apply`, `--source`, `--batch`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Ran on prod 2026-08-12 (7,875 repaired)              |
| `purge-phantom-yahoo-bars.ts`        | **MONEY-1175** - delete Yahoo bars for sessions that never happened. Rule 1: dates where NOT ONE instrument has an exchange-sourced row — a roll-call of market holidays (Republic Day, Independence Day, Gandhi Jayanti, Christmas, Good Friday) plus eleven **weekends**, confirmed as closures because _both_ NSE and BSE published nothing. Rule 2: pre-2024 low-authority rows for instruments that DO have exchange coverage — these turned out to be pre-listing fiction (TCS priced for 2002-2004 though it listed in Aug-2004; WAAREE for 2021 though it listed in 2024). Deleting leaves a one-day hole a chart does not draw; keeping leaves a bar at ~1/28th the raw price, which reads as a crash, because Yahoo is split-ADJUSTED and the exchange is raw. Never touches a row whose source outranks bhavcopy (uses the shared authority table), never touches an instrument whose ONLY coverage is third-party, and honours a grace window at the head of the series. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT.** `--apply`, `--grace-days`, `--residue-before`, `--no-residue`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Ran on prod 2026-08-12 (15,687 + 557)                |
| `backfill-user-isactive.ts`          | Stamp `User.isActive = true` on legacy documents PHYSICALLY missing the field (added after some users were created; `@default(true)` only applies to new writes). Fixes `where: { isActive: true }` queries silently dropping them — the content "Individual Emails" picker, `countMissingSubscriptions`, etc. Raw `$exists:false` match via `$runCommandRaw`. Idempotent. Dry-run by DEFAULT — pass `--apply` to write. Shell wrapper `ops/backfill-user-isactive.sh qa\|prod\|all` fetches `DATABASE_URL_{ENV}` from GCP. Run on prod AND qa. (MONEY-1050)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Pending (MONEY-1050)                                 |
| `backfill-edgar-row-timestamps.ts`   | **MONEY-1317** — fill `ingestedAt` / `updatedAt` / `createdAt` on EDGAR rows that lack them. Both are REQUIRED in `schema.prisma` with a CLIENT-SIDE default (`@default(now())`, `@updatedAt`) and `services/us-fundamentals` writes through pymongo, which applies neither — so the column is ABSENT and every Prisma read of the row throws **P2032**, not a null. Seven of ten EDGAR collections were unreadable. Value is `{ $toDate: "$_id" }`, the ObjectId's own generation time; `filedAt` is when the COMPANY filed, not when we stored, and two models have none. Fills, never overrides (`$ifNull` per field). Fields derived PER MODEL from the schema — see below. Dry-run by DEFAULT; `--apply`. **Re-run after the Python service deploys**, since until then the writer keeps producing broken rows.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Ran on prod 2026-09-01 (785,444 filled, 0 remaining) |
| `quarantine-html-stock-documents.ts` | **MONEY-1315 (B)** — clear `gcsPath` on `stock_documents` rows whose GCS object is an HTML page, not a document. Screener's Documents section links a DRHP to SEBI's HTML _landing page_ (`…/public-issues/mar-2018/advitiya-trade-india-limited_38237.html`) rather than the prospectus PDF; SEBI answers 200 with an honest `text/html`, so `initialProcessingStatus` correctly marked all 1,580 rows `UNSUPPORTED` — and the object was uploaded anyway, leaving every one of them claiming a document via `gcsPath`. No column distinguishes them, so every object is classified from a **ranged 2 KB head read** (never a download — ~99k objects, hundreds of GB) through `sniffDocumentBody`, which is `classifyArchivedObject` plus an envelope peel: **40 of 40 sampled rows are MONEY-1169 enveloped, none bare**, and the classifier checks the envelope first so it answers `envelope`, never `html`, for that whole population. Remedy is `gcsPath: ""` + `UNSUPPORTED` (the same constants the WRITER's refusal path imports), **keeping `sourceUrl` / `title` / `description`** on the BSE-retention precedent. Selection filters on NO possibly-absent field — it walks the collection with `$ifNull` projections through `$runCommandRaw` and decides in code — and the verify uses `$type`, which the selection's byte-level rule structurally cannot be. Deleting the objects is a SEPARATE `--delete-objects`, default OFF. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT**; `--apply`, `--env`, `--doc-type`, `--limit`. Idempotent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Pending (MONEY-1315)                                 | **MONEY-1321 NARROWED THE SELECTION.** This file described all 1,573 rows as ready to quarantine; opening the pages corrected it. **520** of them (CRISIL 442, Brickwork 69, Acuité 9 — measured prod 2026-09-02) are credit-rating RATIONALES published AS the page: median extracted text 17,177 / 13,201 / 18,288 characters, against 455 for a SEBI landing page and 86 for an India Ratings JS shell. CRISIL's own `sourceUrl` ends in `.html`; there was never a PDF to fetch instead, and `gcsPath: ""` is not reversible in practice because the enrichment pipeline never revisits a cleared row. They now classify **`htmlIsTheDocument`** and are EXCLUDED. The exclusion is STRUCTURAL, not a filter applied afterwards — ids are pushed only inside the `html` branch, so a row the classifier does not call `html` cannot reach the write at all, and a test asserts that against this file's source. The host list is `isHtmlTheDocumentHost`'s, the same one the recovery router reads, so the two cannot drift into the state where one deletes what the other is saving. **MONEY-1321 H2 — RUN THE RECOVERY FIRST, AND THIS FILE NOW ENFORCES IT.** This entry used to say running order does not matter "because that job reads `gcsPath` when it is set and re-fetches `sourceUrl` when this one has blanked it". **There was no such re-fetch branch.** `planRecovery` returned `noBytes` on an empty body and the recovery's only fetch derives its URL FROM THE PAGE — so quarantine-first would have blanked 604 SEBI + 186 ICRA `gcsPath`s, every one would have classified `noBytes`, the census would have read clean and the run would have exited 0 with the content gone. `--apply` is now REFUSED while any row it would clear is one the recovery could still save: each quarantine-bound row on a one-hop host has its FULL page read and put through `recoveryStillNeedsTheseBytes`, which is the recovery's own router over the row's own bytes. There is no override flag — the guard lifts itself, because a recovered row's bytes are a PDF and classify `ok`. A failed read counts as still-needed: on a one-way door, "we could not check" refuses. **MONEY-1321 M5 — `--delete-objects` no longer assumes a shared key has one verdict.** That held "by construction" only while the class came from the bytes; it now comes from the row's own `sourceUrl`, so two rows over identical bytes can split into `html` and `htmlIsTheDocument`. Any key a KEPT row still points at is WITHHELD from the delete set and reported, and the confirmation prompt quotes the number that will actually be deleted. Measured on prod 2026-09-02: 11 shared `gcsPath`s, none currently mixing a kept host with a cleared one — latent, and the old comment was already false. **Prod FULL dry run 2026-09-02, after the guards:** 90,213 rows / 123,131 objects → `html` **1,062**, `htmlIsTheDocument` **511 (KEPT)**, `ok` 81,558, `objectMissing` 0, `alreadyQuarantined` **7,082** — which equals the same run's own absent-field census (`gcsPath === "" 7,082`) EXACTLY, closing the 6,996-vs-7,234 gap this file used to flag as unreconciled (it was the short-page `break`). 0 distinct objects shared with a kept row. **The H2 guard reported 790 rows (SEBI 604 + ICRA 186) the recovery could still save, so `--apply` is currently REFUSED** — before the guard, that run would have destroyed all 790 and exited 0. **Earlier prod dry run 2026-09-02, `--doc-type credit_rating`:** 1,712 rows -> `htmlIsTheDocument` **511 (KEPT)**, `html` 321 (still quarantined, `gcsPath` blanked), `ok` 806, `alreadyQuarantined` 74. 511 + 321 = the **832** `credit_rating` rows this file's own pre-narrowing scan recorded, so the narrowing moved 511 rows OUT of the write set and left the other 321 exactly where they were. (The 9 Acuité rows land in `ok` rather than `htmlIsTheDocument` — this job's predicate is `classifyArchivedObject`, which does not call a page opening with a bare `<title>` HTML. They are kept either way, which is the property that matters.) |
| `recover-html-stock-documents.ts`    | **MONEY-1321** — recovers the documents behind the HTML-bodied rows the quarantine used to condemn. **DRY RUN BY DEFAULT** (`--apply` needs a TTY), **ADDITIVE ONLY** (it never clears `gcsPath`, never deletes an object, never moves a row to a terminal state — a test asserts all three against the source), and **IDEMPOTENT** (a `READY` row with text settles before its bytes are even read; a row a previous run fixed reads `notHtml` from the BYTES). **TWO LANES, and conflating them is the expensive mistake.** ONE-HOP: 790 rows (SEBI 604 + ICRA 186) whose page is chrome around a viewer — `recoverDocumentUrl` reads the viewer's own `file=` parameter and the document is fetched. TEXT-IS-THE-DOCUMENT: 520 rows (CRISIL 442, Brickwork 69, Acuité 9) where nothing is fetched at all and the stored page is extracted in place to `textContent` + `READY`; their `.pdf` links are METHODOLOGY BOILERPLATE, so a generic first-link parser swaps 442 company rating rationales for a generic criteria PDF. **Traps encoded rather than rediscovered:** SEBI's `file=` has TWO spellings (335 relative, 269 absolute — handling one loses 45% while looking like it works); ICRA pages carry four boilerplate attachments (`Annexure I_SEBI.pdf`) beside the real report, so the rationale is selected by ROLE; a recovered URL must be SAME-HOST as the page it came from, because the viewer's `file=` is absolutised and could otherwise name anything; and the HTML gate is the UNION of `sniffDocumentContainer` and the quarantine's `sniffDocumentBody` (**MONEY-1321 H4**). The old claim was that the two "disagree on exactly ten, all in one direction" — only one direction had ever been measured. Both were then run over EVERY object under `stocks/` on prod (123,131 objects, 2026-09-02, 0 read errors): both-html **1,573**, neither **121,548**, routing-only **10** (the 9 Acuité bare-`<title>` pages + 1 BOM'd knrcl.com page — SAFE, the quarantine keeps those), corruption-only **0**. That last column is a fact about today's corpus, not a property of the code: `archive-audit`'s rule matches `<?xml-stylesheet` (NSE's own filing-sidecar shape) and `doc-container`'s does not, so one bare XSL-styled object turns it non-zero — the quarantine would blank `gcsPath` while a container-only recovery answered `notHtml` and walked past. Neither predicate may be edited to serve the other, so the reconciliation is one-directional and lives here: **recovery-acts-on ⊇ quarantine-destroys**. **MONEY-1321 H2** — a row with no bytes is no longer a dead end: `recoverDocumentUrl` is asked with an EMPTY body first, which recovers ICRA from the `Id` in its own `sourceUrl` (SEBI cannot — its viewer link is in the page). **MONEY-1321 M13** — a GCS read failure is `readFailed`, a distinct outcome from `noBytes`, and the run exits non-zero; it used to be a bare `catch { return null }`, indistinguishable from a legitimately blanked path, so an auth or bucket failure printed a clean census and exited 0. **Prod dry run 2026-09-02, after all three:** 90,213 scanned / 1,402 candidates → **800 one-hop** (SEBI 604 + ICRA **196**, ten more than the pre-H2 186), 520 text, 64 `noBytes`, 17 `notHtml`, 1 `unrecoverable`, `readFailed` **0** — 1,320 rows recoverable. `--apply`, `--env`, `--lane one-hop | text                                                 | all`, `--host`, `--limit`, `--concurrency`. The whole decision is the pure, unit-tested `recover-html-stock-documents-logic.mts`. All migrations support a preview mode before writing (`--dry-run`, or dry-run
by default with `--apply` to commit).

---

## `ops/` - Ongoing operational tools (safe to rerun)

**`run-market-data-job.sh {aliases|derive|corp|fogap|reclass|resector|xbrl|xbrlreparse|xbrlrecon|xbrlratios|dbharvest|dbappend|dbprobe} {qa|prod|all} [--apply] [args…]`**
(MONEY-1184) — one wrapper for the repeatable MONEY-1175/1176/1177 data jobs, so
they are invoked the way every other data job here is: `DATABASE_URL_{ENV}` from
Secret Manager rather than pasted in by hand, which is one shell-history recall
away from pointing a prod backfill at QA. Echoes the masked target host BEFORE
any write, so "am I pointed at prod?" is answerable in advance rather than
inferred from the argument list afterwards. **The redactor fails CLOSED** — the
first version matched only `mongodb+srv://` and printed the string unchanged
when it missed, so its first real run against a plain-`mongodb://` QA cluster
echoed full credentials; anything unrecognised is now reduced to the scheme.
`aliases` and `corp` read the GCS archive, so the wrapper passes
`GCP_PROJECT_ID` / `GCP_STORAGE_BUCKET` / `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE=replay` explicitly
— those scripts deliberately do NOT load `.env` (they are Cloud Run jobs in
production), and `.env` carries the LOCAL bucket, so a runner that forgot this
would list an EMPTY bucket and report a confident zero. Dry-run by default;
unrecognised flags are forwarded verbatim to the underlying script.

**`xbrl` and `xbrlreparse` are the two cases that do NOT run here** (MONEY-1220 / MONEY-1252). Taking `xbrl` first: It dispatches to
the Cloud Run job `xbrl-backfill-{env}` — `gcloud run jobs execute`, not
`npx tsx`. Two reasons, both measured. (1) A shard is hours and the full walk is
over a day of paced downloads; two agents lost a run to laptop sleep on
2026-08-19. (2) A local run is **archive-blind**: the wrapper's GCS branch sets
`FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE=replay`, and MONEY-1204 routed the XBRL download through
`getFeedAsset`, so a replay walk fetches every byte from NSE and **drops it**
while the counters report a completed run. The job holds the right mode per env
instead (archive on prod, replay on QA).

The dry-run contract is unchanged, only translated: `--apply` is this wrapper's
word and is **consumed**, never forwarded (`xbrl-backfill.ts` does not know the
flag); without it the wrapper executes the job with the job's own `--dry-run`,
which prints the window plan and makes **zero** NSE requests. It preflights that
the job exists and names the script that creates it — on 2026-08-19 no `xbrl-*`
job existed in **any** env while this wrapper documented the walk as if it did.
An argument containing a comma is refused rather than mangled, because
`gcloud … --args` splits on commas.

```bash
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh xbrl prod                                  # window plan, no NSE traffic
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh xbrl prod --apply --from=2018-04 --to=2019-03
```

**`xbrlreparse` dispatches to `xbrl-reparse-{env}`** (MONEY-1252) and shares the
Cloud Run reasoning for a DIFFERENT reason: 96,056 stored filings at one GCS
read plus a parse plus two upserts apiece is hours, and it needs the bucket the
job spec pairs with the env. It re-parses each row from the XML **already in
GCS** so MONEY-1250's `bankBalanceOther` / `currentInvestments` reach rows
parsed before those concepts existed (`totalCash` 81.5% disagreement at the F3
gate; both lines were always in the filings, only our concept table was
missing). It calls `storeFiling` **and** `storeAnnualCompanion`, so both rows a
document produces are rewritten — ANNUAL is exactly what the reconcile gate
compares.

Three differences from `xbrl` worth knowing before reaching for it:

- **It makes ZERO exchange requests.** Bytes come from `FeedArchiveStore.get`,
  never `getFeedAsset`/`fetchXml` — both of which fall back to NSE on a miss,
  which is correct for the ingest and catastrophic here (~2,044 of 96,056 rows
  have no archived object). A miss is COUNTED and SKIPPED, warns with the
  remedy, and deliberately does **not** fail the run. A static test greps the
  job source for those two names.
- **Its dry run is a full REHEARSAL, not a plan.** The real write path runs
  against a no-op upsert, so it reports how many rows would actually CHANGE
  (`projectionReset`, observed at the Prisma seam) rather than how many exist.
  The job-outcome metric still reports `written: 0`; the would-have count lives
  in the reason string. If `changed` is ZERO, stop — the image predates
  MONEY-1250 (check `[build-provenance]`) or the rows are already done.
- **A bare `--apply` is legal here** and the wrapper's shard guard is scoped to
  `xbrl` only. There is no network risk, the default scope is deliberately EVERY
  stored filing (a hard-coded floor would silently exclude rows and still report
  DRAINED), and the run is bounded by a 20 h budget that prints a resume cursor.
  `--from`/`--to` are **PERIOD YEARS** here, the opposite of `xbrl`'s broadcast
  months; passing `--from=2024-05` is a hard argument error rather than a silent
  reinterpretation. `--limit` counts ROWS, and the table averages 1.34 rows per
  document.

```bash
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh xbrlreparse prod --limit=500               # rehearsal: what would CHANGE
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh xbrlreparse prod --apply --from=2024 --to=2024
```

Afterwards the corrected facts sit in `xbrl_filings` and are still invisible to
a reader: `storeFiling` cleared `projectedAt` on the rows that moved, so the
nightly `xbrl-projection` picks them up — or run `xbrl-projection-drain-{env}`
to move them sooner, then re-read the gate with `xbrlrecon`.

**`dbharvest` and `dbappend` are the third and fourth Cloud Run cases**
(MONEY-1373), and `dbprobe` is deliberately not one. The harvest submits a
Databento batch job, waits for the vendor to materialise it, downloads hundreds
of gigabytes of DBN and loads the loadable schemas — hours to days for one
manifest entry, which is a laptop lid this repo has already lost two walks to
(2026-08-19). The append polls one outstanding batch job to completion, which is
unbounded from our side by construction. Both dispatch to
`databento-{harvest,append}-{env}`, provisioned by
[`ops/setup-databento-jobs.sh`](ops/setup-databento-jobs.sh).

Three things differ from the `xbrl` pair and each of them has bitten something
in this repo before:

- **`--apply` on `dbharvest` REQUIRES `--entry=<id>`, and the wrapper refuses
  without it.** A bare `--apply` walks every entry in
  `data/databento-manifest.json` in ONE execution, and the seven entries differ
  by three orders of magnitude — `definition-baseline` is minutes,
  `ohlcv-1s-baseline` is TB-scale and cannot fit the 24 h task timeout at all.
  It would be killed part-way and surface as a FAILED task rather than as the
  "you asked for too much" it is, having already PAID for the entries before the
  wall (a Databento batch job bills on **submission**, not on completion). The
  job refuses too; the wrapper's message is the operator-facing one, and it
  prints the ids by reading the manifest rather than restating them, so it
  cannot go stale. Same guard, same shape, as `xbrl`'s shard refusal and
  `feed-backfill`'s "do not execute with no `--families`".
- **`APPLY_FLAG="--apply"` is declared for all three.** Both jobs are DRY-RUN BY
  DEFAULT and opt in with `--apply` — the `docqueue`/`xbrlreparse` contract, and
  the OPPOSITE of `xbrl-backfill`, which applies by default and opts out with
  `--dry-run`. `run_one_cloudrun` CONSUMES `--apply` and re-emits only
  `$APPLY_FLAG`, so omitting the declaration makes the flag reach the job as
  NEITHER flag: it quotes the manifest, prints a full summary of what it would
  buy, spends nothing and exits 0. That is what happened to `xbrl-reparse` for
  41,924 rows over 54 minutes. It fails SAFE, which is exactly why nobody sees
  it.
- **`FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE` is inert here**, unlike the `xbrl` case where it is the
  whole reason the job cannot run locally. The raw DBN is archived under
  `databento/`, deliberately OUTSIDE `feeds/` — `sync-prod-to-qa-feeds` rsyncs
  that entire prefix prod → QA twice daily, and a TB-scale second-bar archive
  under it would be mirrored in full for bytes QA has no reader for. These jobs
  never route through `getFeedAsset`. `NEEDS_GCS=1` is still set on all three,
  because the archive layer treats a missing `GCP_STORAGE_BUCKET` as a soft
  failure and reports success (59 rows with an empty `gcsPath` on the BSE job,
  MONEY-1258).

`dbprobe` stays LOCAL and costs nothing: `metadata.get_cost` and
`metadata.get_dataset_range` are FREE on the Databento historical API and it
never reaches `batch.submit_job`. Run it before arming anything.

```bash
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh dbprobe prod                                    # quote the manifest, spend nothing
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh dbharvest prod --entry=ohlcv-1m-baseline        # dry run on Cloud Run
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh dbharvest prod --apply --entry=ohlcv-1m-baseline
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh dbappend prod --apply
jq -r '.entries[].id' scripts/data/databento-manifest.json                           # the entry ids
```

| `run-doc-repair.sh`                                       | **MONEY-1319 / MONEY-1321 / MONEY-1333** — one wrapper for the three one-shot document repairs, invoked like every other data job: `run-doc-repair.sh JOB ENV [--apply]`, where JOB is one of recover / retype / quarantine / all and ENV is qa or prod. Both are POSITIONAL: they were matched by VALUE anywhere in argv until MONEY-1334, so `retype prod --doc-type all` silently ran the full destructive chain and `recover qa --host prod` retargeted a QA run at PRODUCTION. It is not secret plumbing (each script resolves its own DATABASE_URL and bucket from Secret Manager). It exists because **the ORDER is load-bearing and getting it wrong is unrecoverable**: quarantine blanks `gcsPath` on rows whose stored bytes are a web page, and recover reads those same bytes to find the real document link inside them. Run the quarantine first and the bytes are gone — 604 SEBI + 196 ICRA rows classify `noBytes`, the census reads clean, and the process exits 0 while the content is unrecoverable. Both underlying scripts CLAIMED the order did not matter (they said recover re-fetches `sourceUrl` when `gcsPath` is blank; there is no such code path), so quarantine now refuses `--apply` while any row it would clear is one recover could still save, and `all` encodes the same order. `all` accepts ONLY `--apply` — the three scripts have disjoint KNOWN_FLAGS and an unshared flag aborts the chain PART WAY THROUGH, after earlier writes have landed. Dry-run is the default; `--apply` on each underlying script is TTY-GATED, an anti-wrapper guard this wrapper does not defeat. Prod record 2026-09-01/02: 748 documents fetched, 520 pages extracted, 172 ZIPs retyped, 139 requeued, UNSUPPORTED 1,655 -> 411. ALWAYS read the dry run first — these populations move as the nightly syncs add rows, so a count from yesterday is not a count from today.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

Needs only gcloud auth. | `setup-infra.sh`                                          | Single-command infra setup for an env: DB push → Pub/Sub topics → Cloud Scheduler jobs → Cloud Build deploy → **EDGAR push subscriptions**. Supports `--step N`, `--from N`, `--skip-deploy`, `--only <area>`. **MONEY-979 — step 4 now deploys BOTH Cloud Run deployables**, `workers` and `us-fundamentals`, off `DEPLOY_TARGETS`. It knew about one, and steps 2 and 3 create seven Pub/Sub topics and five Scheduler entries that all target `us-fundamentals-{env}` (five entries over two of those topics — four publish to `edgar-detect` with a different `mode`) — so a run used to provision every one of them against a service it never built, i.e. a push subscription 404ing five times and dead-lettering once a minute, which reads as a failing job rather than a missing service. `--only` accepts a STEP name (`db`, `pubsub`, `scheduler`, `deploy`, `edgar-push`) **or one deploy target** (`--only us-fundamentals`), and an unrecognised value now **exits 1 with usage** instead of matching nothing, running nothing and still printing "Infrastructure Setup Complete!". `DEPLOY_SHA` is computed ONCE above the target loop, so one release carries one version string across both services (MONEY-839: with the empty default the tag is just `qa-`, the service spec is byte-identical and `gcloud run services replace` NO-OPS, so the freshly built image is never served). **Step 5 exists because the ladder could not bootstrap a cold environment.** The EDGAR push subscriptions can only be created once `us-fundamentals-{env}` is up, but step 2 runs before the step that builds it — so setup-pubsub used to exit 1 there, and `set -e` killed steps 3 and 4 outright: ~65 Scheduler entries and BOTH deploys never ran, on the file's own documented "full setup" invocation. setup-pubsub now distinguishes NOT-YET-BUILT (loud, exit 0) from BROKEN (exit 1), and step 5 re-runs it with `EDGAR_PUSH_REQUIRED=1` after the deploy. Skipping it leaves `edgar-detect-minutely-{env}` publishing 1,440 times a day into a topic with NO subscriber — which drops messages silently: no DLQ, no error, no metric. **Not covered, deliberately, and each printed in the closing banner:** the Cloud Build TRIGGERS (`setup-cloudbuild-triggers.sh`), the five EDGAR Cloud Run JOBS (`setup-us-fundamentals-jobs.sh`) and the `edgar/` bucket lifecycle (`setup-gcs-lifecycle.sh`). | `setup-cloudbuild-triggers.sh`                            | **MONEY-979** — idempotent create-or-update of the two `us-fundamentals` Cloud Build triggers: `us-fundamentals-qa` (`^main$` → `services/us-fundamentals/cloudbuild.qa.yaml`) and `us-fundamentals-prod` (`^release$` → the prod spec), both with `--included-files='services/us-fundamentals/**'`. Env is **positional and required** (`qa\|prod\|all`); `--dry-run` first-class. **A trigger with no `includedFiles` filter rebuilds on EVERY push to its branch** — that is why the new pair declares one, and why the existing pair arguably needs one. **The two EXISTING worker triggers are READ and REPORTED, never written**: adding a filter to a live trigger CHANGES WHEN IT FIRES, which is a behaviour change to the release path (and `workers/**` alone is almost certainly too narrow — that image bundles `packages/**`), so the script prints the command and does not run it. Measured 2026-08-29 and the constants are those measurements: `stockbook-worker-prod` and `stockbook-workers-qa`, 1st-gen GitHub App triggers on `UseMoney-AI/stockbook-monorepo` in region `global`, running as `stockbook-api@`, **neither carrying `includedFiles`**. One LIST per run, shape-validated; a listing that fails or is not a JSON array is FATAL on a live run, because read as "nothing exists" it would CREATE a duplicate of every trigger and every push would build twice (`apply-monitors.sh`'s fault (e), one product over). A `--dry-run` with no credentials still works and says it could not read the live state.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Once per environment, to provision. Again only when a branch pattern, build config or file filter changes — a release does not need it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **MONEY-1478 — it is no longer us-fundamentals-only.** The services it owns are a `declare -a SERVICES` table (`key
