                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | trigger-prefix                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | config-dir | included-files`), so a new deployable is a LINE rather than a second copy of the file, and the loop is services x envs. `market-data-{qa,prod}`joined it:`usemoney-market-data-\*`had **NO TRIGGER AT ALL** and every deploy had been a hand-run`gcloud builds submit`, which is not a theoretical drift — measured 2026-09-18, QA was serving `qa-20c00972`while main was three commits ahead, missing MONEY-1471/1474/1475, all of which touched`apps/market-data`or`packages/core/src/market-data`. Its filter includes `packages/**`deliberately: the service imports`@usemoney/core`for the tile logic, the UDF contract and the quote store, so a core-only change alters what it serves while leaving`apps/market-data/**`untouched — exactly the three commits QA had missed. The completion check is`services x envs`now; leaving it at`envs` would have made it fire on every healthy run the moment a second service was added. |
| `setup-gcs-lifecycle.sh` + `gcs-lifecycle.json`           | **MONEY-979 / MONEY-1309** — apply this repo's Object Lifecycle Management rules from `docs/reference/edgar-engine.md` §5 to `stockbook-content-{env}`: `edgar/bulk/**` DELETED at 30 days (superseded nightly; the two zips are 1.4 GB and 1.6 GB), `edgar/filings/**` → NEARLINE at 30 days (permanent, reparse-only reads). Env positional (`qa\|prod\|all`), `--dry-run` first-class with a before/after diff. **The hazard is that a lifecycle policy is set on a BUCKET, not a prefix**, and `--lifecycle-file` REPLACES the whole policy — the same bucket holds `feeds/`, `candles/`, learning content and the RAG corpus, so a naive apply would silently delete every other rule. It READS the live policy, MERGES (every rule not entirely scoped under `edgar/` is carried through untouched) and **refuses to apply if the read fails**, if the describe JSON does not name the bucket, if a lifecycle key is present in an unrecognised shape, or if the policy file declares a rule outside the ALLOW-LIST, or if a Delete rule's prefix would SWEEP a protected root. **MONEY-1309 generalized the scope and RENAMED the policy file** (`gcs-lifecycle-edgar.json` → `gcs-lifecycle.json`) because a second prefix moved in: `feeds/TIINGO/ws/**` DELETED at 30 days, the raw Tiingo websocket ticks. `ALLOW_PREFIXES` (`edgar/`, `feeds/TIINGO/ws/`) replaces the hard-coded literal; `PROTECTED_ROOTS` (`feeds/`, `candles/`, `stocks/`, `edgar/filings/`) is a SECOND, independent lock that does not consult the allow-list, so widening the allow-list cannot legalise a Delete on a shared root. The shorten test is `root.startsWith(prefix)` — true when the prefix IS the root or is SHORTER — which catches `feeds/`, `feeds`, `fe` and ``with no list of bad values, and which also closes a pre-existing hole:`Delete @ edgar/`was allow-listed and would have taken the permanent SEC filing archive. **Every refusal is EXECUTED in CI**, not grepped: the`node -e`guard is self-contained (env + a file in, a merged policy out, no gcloud call), so`packages/core/**tests**/ops/tiingo-realtime-infra.test.ts`lifts it out of the shell source and runs it against synthetic policies. The tick prefix is also asserted to AGREE with the`--exclude`regex in`sync-prod-to-qa.sh`— one rule, two files. **Measured 2026-08-29 (SDK 554.0.0) and it changes the code:**`--format="value(lifecycle)"`prints an EMPTY LINE for both "no policy" and "wrong field name", so a`value()`read is fail-open and would always clobber; the read is`--format=json`and parsed, where an ABSENT key and an EMPTY list are different values. Both buckets carry NO lifecycle policy today. Rules unchanged → no write at all (the comparison is order-insensitive, because GCS does not order-guarantee the list it returns). **The scratch file uses an explicit`${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/…XXXXXX`template**:`mktemp -t NAME`is BSD/macOS only — GNU coreutils reads`-t`'s operand as a template needing ≥3 trailing `X`s and exits `too few X's in template`, so the script aborted before its first bucket read on Linux and Cloud Shell. It failed SAFE, and only ever for an operator who is not the founder.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Once per environment. Again after editing `gcs-lifecycle.json`. **Lifecycle is asynchronous** — GCS evaluates roughly daily, so nothing being deleted an hour later is expected, not evidence the rule is wrong.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `setup-scheduler.sh`                                      | Create/update Cloud Scheduler jobs for QA / prod (deletes old jobs first). Two kinds: the per-env **Pub/Sub** jobs in `SCHEDULER_DEFS` (name + topic get `-{env}`; anything in `PROD_ONLY_JOBS` is skipped **and deleted** on QA so it never scrapes). **MONEY-1309** added `tiingo-news-poll` — `*/2 * * * *`, i.e. every 2 minutes on **ALL SEVEN DAYS**. That `*` day field is the ticket: the three US price rows carry `2-6` because a US session closes 16:00 ET and lands after midnight IST, which is a fact about the EXCHANGE CALENDAR — news has none, and an 8-K drops on a Sunday. It is in `PROD_ONLY_JOBS` (720 runs/day; a QA copy makes it 1,440 against ONE Tiingo quota, and `tiingo-pacer.ts` state is per-PROCESS), which is only defensible because `us_news_articles` is already in `sync-prod-to-qa.sh`'s `REFERENCE_COLLECTIONS`. It is deliberately NOT in `OLD_SCHEDULER_PREFIXES` — nothing is being renamed, and a name listed there is DELETED on every run in every env. Its topic is added to `workers/scripts/setup-pubsub.sh` (both `BASE_TOPICS` and `BASE_WORKER_TOPICS`) and to `gcp-setup.sh`'s own bootstrap copy of the list. Plus the **Cloud Run-targeted singleton** prod->QA raw-feed mirror — ONE job, TWO triggers: `sync-prod-to-qa-feeds-eod` (21:15 IST Mon-Fri) and `sync-prod-to-qa-feeds-daily` (00:45 IST) (MONEY-1156 / MONEY-1194). Both upserted on the **prod pass only** (prod is the producer; direction is hard-coded); a QA pass skips them **without** deleting, which would cut QA's feeds. Skipped while their Cloud Run job is absent — reported as PENDING (never as FAILED: nothing errored, a prerequisite is missing) and **each pending entry now carries its own provisioning command**. The summary block is shared, and it used to print one hardcoded `./scripts/ops/setup-sync-job.sh` for every entry: correct for the three `sync-prod-to-qa*` triggers, and wrong for the six EDGAR bulk ones, which are provisioned by `setup-us-fundamentals-jobs.sh`. Following it rebuilt an unrelated job, created none of `edgar-bulk-{submissions,companyfacts,ftd}-prod`, and the re-run it told you to do reported the same six as pending — forever, with no error anywhere. MONEY-1194 also adds `ENV_SCHEDULE_OVERRIDES` — a per-env schedule override for the rare job that CONSUMES what the other env PRODUCES. Today that is `bhavcopy-sync-daily`: prod archives at 21:00, the mirror copies at 21:15, QA replays at **21:30**, all on the same IST calendar day. Do not unify them, and do not move QA past midnight (`resolveTargetDay` resolves to TODAY, so QA would ask for an unpublished file). Shards are ONE YEAR each, ten of them, sized from a MEASURED 9.5s per filing (the 5.7 MB attachment download dominates, not `PDF_PACE_MS`) = ~14.8h per shard against the 24h cap; a 3-year shard measured ~38h and a KILLED task cannot chain, so the sequence would stall silently. Trigger only the head (2024) — Shards CHAIN automatically: a completed shard triggers the next via the Cloud Run Admin API (`jobs:run` with an arg override, authenticated from the metadata server). Guarded — it refuses to chain after a 429, after `CONSECUTIVE_FAILURE_BREAKER`, when filings were offered and NONE stored, or from an interactive run, and each refusal is NAMED in the log. Termination is structural: `BSE_SHARD_PLAN` is a fixed array walked forward only, so a chain is at most `length - 1` links and an ad-hoc window matches nothing and never chains. **MONEY-1258** adds `setup-bse-backfill-job.sh`, provisioning the Cloud Run job `bse-backfill-{env}` for the LONG BSE historical walk. Measured from the live 2025 run (2.49 filings per symbol-year over 2,255 companies), 2015-2025 is ~61,800 filings = **~30.6h**, which EXCEEDS Cloud Run's 24h task cap — so it runs in SHARDS (`--from-year=2015 --to-year=2018`, etc.), never as one `--from-year=2015` execution. `--max-retries=0`, 2Gi (a whole PDF is buffered in memory; measured max attachment 23.7 MB), no Scheduler trigger ever. `--apply` needs no TTY there: Cloud Run sets `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` and the job execution IS the confirmation — locally the prompt still applies. An interrupted shard is safe to re-run (stored rows are `skip-same` after one indexed read). Budget ~300 GB across all shards. `run-market-data-job.sh bseresults` deliberately stays LOCAL for the short interactive shard. **MONEY-1259** adds three EXTRA parse passes (`stock-document-parse-{morning,midday,evening}`, 07:00 / 13:00 / 19:00 IST) and a daily screener-only document run (`stock-document-sync-screener-daily`, 08:00 IST). All four are in `PROD_ONLY_JOBS`; the ORIGINAL `stock-document-parse-daily` deliberately stays on BOTH envs, so QA keeps writing `document_financial_extracts` itself (that pairing is why the collection is now a QA_DIRECT entry in `prod-only-sync-coverage.test.ts` — delete the exemption if the QA trigger ever goes). Parse throughput comes from MORE RUNS, never a bigger batch or budget: the run is capped by `RUN_TIME_BUDGET_MS` (210s) plus one 240s in-flight model call against the 600s ack deadline, and overrunning REDELIVERS the run and pays Gemini twice. 6h spacing exceeds the worst chain (20 x ~600s), so passes cannot overlap. The screener run uses the new `sources:["screener"]` message field rather than `docTypes`, because `annual_report` and `credit_rating` belong to BOTH `NSE_ANNOUNCEMENT_DOC_TYPES` and `SCREENER_PAGE_DOC_TYPES` — a docTypes-only payload would also switch on the per-symbol NSE fan-out that `annual-reports-sync-daily` already carries. **MONEY-1235 — `xbrl-projection-daily` carries a TEMPORARY payload `{"periodTypes":["ANNUAL"]}` instead of `{}`, and it must be reverted.** The companion fix stops the writer stamping `projectedAt` on CREATE-suppressed filings (correct — a suppressed filing was never decided about), so unstamped QUARTERLY rows re-enter every nightly page. Prod holds 71,450 QUARTERLY filings against 18,381 ANNUAL and the selection is symbol-major, so the two INTERLEAVE: an unscoped 500-row page is mostly rows that cannot be written while `createMissing` is off, and the ANNUAL work the switchover gates on advances at a fraction of its rate, nightly, forever. `periodTypes` is a real column filter applied at SELECTION (`xbrl-projection-writer.ts`, commit `510ea336`), not a post-verdict skip. Revert to `{}` — and delete the TEMPORARY comment block above the definition — once the quarterly drain in `docs/reference/xbrl-switchover-runbook.md` step 5 completes; `packages/core/__tests__/market-data/xbrl-projection-wired.test.ts` pins the payload, so the revert deliberately turns that test red until its assertion is updated with it. The payload narrows and can never ARM: `XBRL_PROJECTION_APPLY` on the service is the only switch that turns writes on. **MONEY-1379 — a NAMED allow-list of jobs may retry a failed publish.** `upsert_scheduler` sets `--max-retry-attempts` from an allow-list of EXACT job names — today only the four `news-rss-sync` spellings; everything else gets an EXPLICIT `0` (absent and 0 must not be the same state — MONEY-1214). An earlier version derived it from the schedule's MINUTE field, and that is wrong in both directions: frequency says nothing about what a second run COSTS, and the sub-hourly set includes `us-filing-insight-sweep` (a PAID Gemini call per owed document, still owed when the retry sweeps), `tiingo-news-poll` (a metered vendor), `edgar-detect-minutely` (one shared per-IP SEC budget) and `newsletter-publish-check` (sends EMAIL). `alert-evaluator` is NOT on the list despite being one of the two jobs that failed: its cooldown is a read-then-write, so two OVERLAPPING runs both notify a user, and at one tick a minute the retry buys about sixty seconds against a duplicate alert. Measured 2026-09-11: `news-rss-sync-market-hours-prod` and `alert-evaluator-market-hours-prod` each hit DEADLINE_EXCEEDED twice in 30 days on Cloud Scheduler's OWN Publish RPC, and with `retryCount` absent each failure was one silently skipped tick. It is an allow-list because a retry RE-PUBLISHES — a new messageId the dedupe middleware cannot pair with the first — so in the ambiguous case (the publish landed, only the response timed out) it is a genuine second run, CONCURRENT with the first. The flag is passed on BOTH the create and the update branch, but **a scheduler's config is pinned at setup time**, so the live jobs keep whatever they were created with until this script is re-run per environment — a fix in the provisioner reaches nothing on its own. Pinned by `packages/core/__tests__/market-data/scheduler-retry.test.ts`, which runs the derivation itself under a real `bash` rather than re-implementing it.                 | After editing the scheduler definitions. Once `setup-sync-job.sh` has provisioned the feeds Cloud Run job — until then that schedule is reported pending, not created.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

gold or private), READER on `lake_bronze_prod` (the ledger, read to skip tables mid-load) and project `bigquery.jobUser`. `--skip-build` is refused until `sync-prod-to-qa-lake` exists, because | `build-qa-sample-universe.ts` + `qa-sample-universe.json` | **MONEY-980** — generates the CHECKED-IN symbol manifest that `sync-prod-to-qa.sh --tier=sample` bounds every collection by. `node --import tsx scripts/ops/build-qa-sample-universe.ts --env=prod --apply` — **`--env=prod` is not optional**: with no `--env` it falls back to the local `.env`, which in this repo points at **QA**, and a manifest built from QA's 1.07M-row `daily_prices` instead of prod's 10.66M is a different, smaller, silently wrong universe. Dry-run by default; bare `--apply` writes. Emits `us.equities` 502 (S&P 500) / `us.etfs` 100 / `us.delisted` 20 / `us.funds` 100 / `in.equities` 500 / `in.etfs` 100 / `in.schemes` 100 / `isins` 600. **It is a TEST FIXTURE, not a prod data dependency** — nothing in a production code path may read it, which is what keeps a scraped constituent list out of licensing questions. Every list records its `method` (derived vs static-fallback) and its VALIDATION hit count against the collection it bounds, so an empty slice cannot pass as a successful run; an UNDECLARED zero aborts the generator. Three things it does that look optional and are not: dual listings collapse to one company (2,226 of 5,185 ISINs quote on both NSE and BSE, so an untreated top-500 was ~250 companies counted twice); fund lists intersect the NAV-BEARING population (only 1,816 of 14,225 schemes have any NAV history, and `mutual_fund_masters` has **no AUM column** to rank by); and Wikipedia's `BRK.B`/`BF.B` are normalised to Tiingo's `BRK-B`/`BF-B`, without which those two matched ZERO rows while the other 500 matched fine. `us.funds` is pinned around six MEASURED events — SWPPX x6, DODGX x16, FCNTX/FSPTX x10, plus VFIAX/FXAIX as never-split CONTROLS — so QA exercises both the sparse `splitFactor` path and the case where it must stay absent. | `sync-prod-to-qa.sh`                                      | Copy SCRAPED / REFERENCE data **PROD -> QA** (direction hard-coded; there is no way to flip it). `--tier=reference` (default) drop+restores an ALLOWLIST of **50** rate-limited-source Mongo collections (counted 2026-08-31; it read "39" before this recount) (Screener/Tickertape/Kuvera/AI-extracted, plus `macro_series` from MONEY-1106's prod-only RBI scrape and `bond_master` / `bond_eod` / `mwpl_daily` from MONEY-1204's prod-only `feed-parse` job) + rsyncs the scraped-PDF GCS prefix; the count read "13" until 2026-08-13, "19" until 2026-08-16 and "39" until 2026-08-31, having been stale for several additions each time — **the array in the script is the authority**, and `prod-only-sync-coverage.test.ts` is what actually enforces that a new prod-only writer's table is in it; a collection that is EMPTY in prod is SKIPPED so it can never wipe QA. `--tier=feeds` (MONEY-1085) rsyncs the archived raw exchange files (`feeds/`) and touches **no** Mongo collection — plus, since **MONEY-1373**, a SECOND rsync pair over `candles/`, the candle warehouse's gzipped-JSONL COLD SHARDS, with **no `--exclude` at all**. That prefix was never mirrored, and the gap is silent in the worst direction: the Mongo `candles` table IS a REFERENCE*COLLECTION, so QA receives the hot window by `mongorestore` and then answers every COLD read `absent` — the shape of "no data in that range", not of an error. Pre-existing for D1; the M1/H1 harvest widens it to everything behind the 45-day intraday hot window. It is a second PAIR inside the feeds tier and **not** a `--tier=candles` (no such tier exists; the script exits 1 on one): the same QA nightly chain consumes both on the same clock, so they belong on the same job and the same two triggers. The feeds exclusion is deliberately NOT reused — `--exclude` is matched RELATIVE to SOURCE, so the same regex means `candles/TIINGO/ws/` there, silently dropping any shard whose path matched, and rsync exits 0 either way — **minus `TIINGO/ws/`** (MONEY-1309), the raw Tiingo websocket ticks, which QA must never receive: they are not a replay input (the bars derived from them land in `candles`, a REFERENCE_COLLECTION QA already gets by `mongorestore`), they are the largest thing under the prefix, and prod deletes them at 30 days via `gcs-lifecycle.json`. The exclusion is `--exclude='^TIINGO/ws/'`, built ONCE into a bash array and expanded at **every** rsync site including the `--dry-run` echo, so the printed command cannot drift from the issued one (the MONEY-1157 stale-copy class this file already paid for). `gcloud storage rsync --exclude` semantics were MEASURED on SDK 554.0.0 rather than read off the help text: the regex is matched with Python `re.match` (START-ANCHORED) against the path RELATIVE to SOURCE — so `^feeds/TIINGO/ws/` matches nothing, `ws/` excludes nothing, and `TIINGO/ws/` correctly leaves `xTIINGO/ws/` alone. `packages/core/__tests__/ops/tiingo-realtime-infra.test.ts` asserts that this regex and the lifecycle prefix reconstruct each other exactly — one rule, two files. **The image BAKES this script**, so the change reaches the live `sync-prod-to-qa-feeds` job only after a founder re-runs `setup-sync-job.sh`; it runs **twice** a weekday (21:15 + 00:45 IST) because the single 00:45 slot reasoned only about the PRODUCER and landed 3h45m after QA's consumer asked, so QA replayed 0% and scraped NSE+BSE every weekday (MONEY-1194). **`--tier=sample` (MONEY-980)** — a BOUNDED QA rather than a mirror: the reference tier copies **12,753,000 rows** (measured against prod 2026-08-28) and 85% of that is four tables nothing in QA reads (`stock_flags` 7,597,566, `mf_nav_history` 3,193,427, `slb_positions` 747,614, `derivative_summary` 434,832). The sample tier copies a per-collection slice bounded by a checked-in symbol manifest (`qa-sample-universe.json`) and a history window — `--universe=N --years=N`, default 500/2. Five strategies because one rule does not fit 50 collections: `symbol`, `isin`, `scheme` (funds are keyed on `schemeCode`, so a symbol filter returns ZERO), `recent` (market-wide rows with no symbol dimension at all), and `all` (small, or DISJOINT by construction — an IPO is not yet listed, so it can never appear in a top-500-by-mcap list). **The load-bearing guard:** `mongorestore --drop` drops BEFORE restoring, so a query matching zero rows does not copy nothing, it DELETES QA's table — the whole-collection tier can never do this because its empty-guard catches an empty PROD, but a sampled one can, because prod is full and the sample simply misses. So the SAMPLED count is taken first and a zero skips. A collection in the allowlist but missing from `SAMPLE_PLAN` is refused, not copied whole. The plan lives in `packages/core/src/ops/qa-sample-plan.ts` (pure + unit-tested, and in `packages/core` rather than `scripts/` because the repo root has no `"type": "module"`, so `tsx` loads `scripts/**` as CommonJS and an ESM test cannot import a named export from there); `scripts/ops/qa-sample-plan.ts` is the CLI. Mongo only — the GCS half is the scraped-PDF mirror with no cheap symbol filter. Also `--collections-only`, `--gcs-only`, `--dry-run`, `--yes`. URIs come from Secret Manager; never pass a DB URL. MONEY-1157: `--tier` with no value now prints a usage error instead of dying silently under `set -e`, and the real-run "MongoDB sync SKIPPED" line names the tier rather than always citing `--gcs-only`. **MONEY-1238 — the exit code now means something.** There was no `exit` anywhere after the restore loop, so the script fell off the end and returned **0**; it is the sync image's `ENTRYPOINT`, so `sync-prod-to-qa-daily` reported SUCCEEDED with reference collections missing from QA. It now exits **1** when any collection failed to copy, when the post-restore re-link failed, or when the re-link ran and rows still dangle (`relink` vs `relink-orphans-remain` — different causes, different fixes). Two more corrections in the same pass: the re-link is no longer gated on `[ ${#FAILED[@]} -eq 0 ]` (a PARTIAL restore needs it MORE than a clean one — that gate skipped the repair exactly when rows had just been replaced with prod's ObjectIds), and it no longer shells out to `npx tsx repair-instrument-links.ts`, which had never executed once: `google/cloud-sdk:slim` has no Node and the image COPYs only the `.sh`. The repair is now an embedded **mongosh** program inside this script — self-contained precisely so it cannot be half-shipped again. `repair-instrument-links.ts` stays as the by-hand remedy; `sync-relink-required.test.ts` pins BOTH to the same list derived from `schema.prisma`, and `sync-prod-to-qa-exit-code.spawntest.ts` proves the exit codes by running the script against stub binaries. Also refuses to start when `DATABASE_URL_PROD` and `DATABASE_URL_QA` resolve to the SAME database. **MONEY-1519 — `candles`, the one reference collection QA also WRITES (`tiingo-realtime-qa`), is restored UNIQUE-KEY-FIRST.** The script drops it, creates prod's unique index(es) read from PROD's own `listIndexes`, then restores WITHOUT `--drop`, so the key holds during the restore and after a kill. A bar QA's writer landed first is refused on arrival and counted in mongorestore's `failures`; before this it detonated the unique build 30 minutes later and left 29.6M rows with only `\_id*`, every night since 2026-09-01. A failed index build (or an unreadable prod spec) is recorded as `INDEX_BUILD_FAILED`in`qa_sync_outcomes`— distinct from`FAILED`, because the data is not the problem — and flagged by `--status`. `UNIQUE_FIRST_COLLECTIONS` is the list; a collection that grows a live QA writer belongs in it. **`--tier=lake`(MONEY-1610)** copies every BASE TABLE present in both`lake_silver_prod`and`lake_silver_qa`with free, in-region WRITE_TRUNCATE copy jobs; a PLAN unless`--apply`. It refuses a destination that is `\*\_prod`, bronze, private or anything but `lake_silver_qa`; skips (exit 0, `WARN SKIPPED_LOADING`) any table holding a live LOADING claim in `lake_bronze_prod.partition_load`, so QA keeps last night's copy of a table prod is mid-loading; refuses per table on partition/cluster drift, QA-ahead columns or a same-name type/mode conflict; and fails on a row-count mismatch against the job's `copiedRows`or on any planned table the copy loop did not reach. A LOADING claim older than the lease is copied with`WARN STALE_LOADING`(a crashed loader may hold a deleted month), unless a later ACCEPTED, or a later REJECTED that landed rows, shows the month was loaded since. The skip covers only tables of the ledger-claimed loaders;`identity-sync`and`corp-action-lake-sink` take no claim, so run neither near 02:30 IST. | Ad-hoc to refresh QA. Normally runs itself via the three Cloud Run jobs below. Always `--dry-run` first — the reference tier DROPS QA collections.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | **MONEY-1478 — the feeds tier now mirrors a THIRD pair, the D1 chart tiles, and it is the first mirrored prefix in a DIFFERENT BUCKET**: `gs://stockbook-lake-prod/tiles/` -> `gs://stockbook-lake-qa/tiles/` (the tile lake is `stockbook-lake-*`, not `stockbook-content-*`). A tile is keyed `{market}/d1/{listingId}/` — a symbol, an exchange and a date, no ObjectId — so it is PORTABLE and QA takes prod's OUTPUT rather than recomputing it, the same argument as `candles`. No `--exclude` array: the flag is relative to its own SOURCE, so the feeds regex would mean `tiles/TIINGO/ws/` here. The exhaustive-prefix guard in `edgar-monitor-and-scheduler.spawntest.ts` was WIDENED rather than worked around — its content-bucket regex cannot see a lake-bucket assignment, so without a second explicit list a whole new mirrored prefix would have started copying with the deepEqual still green. **MONEY-1488 — every VISITED collection now writes an outcome row into QA's `qa_sync_outcomes` (`_id` = the collection name), and `--status` prints the whole table with ages.** Six DISTINCT states, because collapsing any pair loses the only signal that says what to do: `RESTORED`, `SKIPPED_EMPTY` (the empty-guard — QA untouched), `SKIPPED_SAMPLE_MISS`, `COUNT_FAILED` (failed BEFORE starting — QA untouched), `FAILED`, and `IN_FLIGHT`. **`IN_FLIGHT` is the whole mechanism**: it is stamped BEFORE the dump and never overwritten, because a process killed at the 3600s task timeout cannot write "I was killed" — so the intent goes down first and the ABSENCE of an end stamp is the signal. `FAILED` and `IN_FLIGHT` are the two that mean `mongorestore --drop` may have dropped QA's copy and not finished replacing it, which is the precondition MONEY-1486's delta bound has to read (a killed restore leaves a SCATTERED subset, so QA's live `max()` advances past rows that never landed, permanently). The collection is NOT in `REFERENCE_COLLECTIONS` and must never be — it records what QA received, so prod's copy of it is a fiction. No Prisma model either: nothing in the product reads it and the image has no Node. **MONEY-1487 — the loop ORDER is now derived from that record, least-recently-VISITED first, ties by manifest position.** The array grew 21 -> 54 when the US backbone landed and the US block sits above the India one, so its LINE NUMBER decided what ever got synced: 34 collections had not synced since 2026-08-31. VISITED and not SUCCEEDED, deliberately — `candles` has failed every run since 2026-09-01 after burning 25-29 min of a 60-min budget, and a success-keyed order would park it at the head for ever and starve the other 53. `REFERENCE_COLLECTIONS` itself is unchanged: only the walk moves. A short or unreadable ordering answer falls back to manifest order LOUDLY and never fails the copy. The rule's executable form is `packages/core/src/ops/qa-sync-outcomes.ts`; the image has no Node so it cannot be imported there, and `sync-prod-to-qa-exit-code.spawntest.ts` drives the REAL script against canned facts and compares the two orders. **Also MONEY-1487: `relink_required` now runs a `listIndexes` check over all 54 collections** (zero extra spawns — the connection is already open) and exits **3** when one holds rows with only `_id_`: `mongorestore --drop` rebuilds indexes LAST, so a killed run leaves a collection enforcing nothing, observed live 2026-09-19 on `stock_documents` and `shareholding_patterns`. \*\*And the empty-guard's `CNT=$(mongosh … | tr …)`was a BARE assignment from a pipeline** — under`set -eo pipefail`that takes the pipeline's non-zero status and`set -e`kills the script, so the documented`[ -z "$CNT" ]`fallback on the very next line was unreachable and one flaky count aborted the whole run mid-list. Guarded with`if ! CNT=$(…)`, and an unreadable count is now `COUNT_FAILED` rather than silently indistinguishable from an empty prod. |

Build context must be the repo root. **mongosh is LOAD-BEARING** as of MONEY-1238, not just for the empty-guard row count: it is the runtime for the sync script's embedded post-restore re-link. Node is deliberately still absent — adding it would mean the monorepo's node_modules (1.8 GB), a generated Prisma client and its platform-matched query engine, and `@prisma/client` cannot be bundled away (it is `external` in every bundler config here). | `setup-xbrl-backfill-job.sh`                              | **MONEY-1220 step E** — deploy the Cloud Run job `xbrl-backfill-{env}` (`dist/xbrl-backfill.js`), the ONLY source of ANNUAL financial history. Env is **positional and required**. Pins the image read from the live `stockbook-workers-{env}` SERVICE at setup time and **prints the tag, the commit inside it and the digest** — that SHA is what the job's own first `[build-provenance]` line must echo back, and it is the only check here that can actually go red. `--max-retries=0` (a retry re-walks the same window straight back into the NSE host a stop-loss just backed away from), 1Gi, 24h task timeout (`--task-timeout=` to shorten; **1h would kill every FY shard about a fifth of the way in** — `xbrl-backfill.ts` budgets up to 4h of downloads PER monthly window). `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE` is **per-env: archive on prod, replay on QA** — the scrape-once contract, and the one setting that differs from the feed jobs, which is exactly why this job may have only one owner (`--set-env-vars` REPLACES the env list, so a second script deploying it means last-writer-wins with no error). **No Scheduler trigger, ever**: the nightly `xbrl-sync` worker already captures new filings, and a nightly re-walk of eight years is the fastest way to get our egress IP throttled off nseindia.com. **DD_VERSION is deliberately NOT passed** — it is baked into the image by `workers/Dockerfile` from the same `$COMMIT_SHA` that forms the tag, so setting it here would make the reported SHA a property of the JOB SPEC and let a stale job report a fresh commit. Prints the FY-aligned shard list on every run. **Preflight:** the resolved image must contain the `xbrl-backfill` tsup entry, else the container dies with `Cannot find module /app/dist/xbrl-backfill.js`. | `setup-xbrl-reparse-job.sh`                               | **MONEY-1252** — deploy the Cloud Run job `xbrl-reparse-{env}` (`dist/xbrl-reparse.js`), which re-parses every stored `xbrl_filings` row from the XML **already in GCS** and rewrites BOTH rows the document produces (`storeFiling` + `storeAnnualCompanion`). Env is **positional and required**. Exists because MONEY-1250 taught the parser `bankBalanceOther` and `currentInvestments` AFTER all ~96,000 filings were stored, so their `facts.values` lack both and the projection's `totalCash` publishes about a quarter of a company's cash (81.5% disagreement at the F3 gate). **Makes ZERO exchange requests** — bytes come from `FeedArchiveStore.get`, never `getFeedAsset`/`fetchXml`, which fall back to NSE on a miss; ~2,044 of 96,056 rows have no archived object and are counted and skipped. **Non-destructive relative to the switchover**: it writes `xbrl_filings` only and has zero `stockFinancials` references, so it does NOT open the one-way door `xbrl-projection-drain` opens — but it DOES clear `projectedAt` on rows whose facts moved, which is what makes the nightly projection pick them up. `--max-retries=0`, **2Gi** (a keyset page plus a document plus both parsed contexts plus the incumbent's whole `facts` JSON), 24h task timeout against the job's own 20h `--budget-sec` so a run ends on its OWN budget and prints a resume cursor rather than being killed silently. Image resolved from the live `stockbook-workers-{env}` SERVICE and **fails closed** on an empty resolution — and the stale-pin risk here is the starkest in the repo, because a pre-MONEY-1250 image does not crash: it re-parses all 96,056 rows with the old concept table and reports a clean run that changed nothing. **No Scheduler trigger, ever**: this is a ONE-SHOT repair, and once complete it would change nothing every night — indistinguishable from a broken selector. **Preflight:** the resolved image must contain the `xbrl-reparse` tsup entry, else the container dies with `Cannot find module /app/dist/xbrl-reparse.js`. | `setup-xbrl-projection-drain-job.sh`                      | **MONEY-1235** — deploy the Cloud Run job `xbrl-projection-drain-{env}` (`dist/xbrl-projection-drain.js`), which pages `processXbrlProjectionWrite` until the unprojected backlog in `xbrl_filings` is gone. Env is **positional and required**. Exists because the nightly `xbrl-projection` push worker **cannot** drain it: its limit is 500 (sized for Pub/Sub's un-raisable 600s ack deadline) and its scheduler payload is deliberately scoped `{"periodTypes":["ANNUAL"]}`, so 71,450 quarterly filings would take ~143 nights — and **no Cloud Run job ran the projection at all** before this one (`xbrl-backfill.ts` calls only `processXbrlSync` and has zero `stockFinancials` references). **This job opens the one-way door**, unlike its non-destructive sibling: `StockFinancials` has no `source` in its unique key, so the first XBRL write to a period destroys the incumbent value with nothing left to reconcile against — hence `XBRL_PROJECTION_APPLY` ships **present and `"false"`** (absent and `"false"` must never be the same state; that is how `MF_METADATA_PROVIDER` disabled its job silently for months, MONEY-1214) and `--arm` is a separate, deliberate re-run. A write needs **both** the env flag AND `--apply`: a flag can never arm a run the environment has not armed. `--max-retries=0`, **2Gi** (the writer holds a whole page of `facts` JSON plus one `ScaleObservation` per row — bigger than the backfill's one-document footprint), 24h task timeout. Image resolved from the live `stockbook-workers-{env}` SERVICE and **fails closed** on an empty resolution. **No Scheduler trigger, ever**: it is an irreversible write path, and once drained it would select nothing every night — indistinguishable from a broken selector. **Preflight:** the resolved image must contain the `xbrl-projection-drain` tsup entry, else the container dies with `Cannot find module /app/dist/xbrl-projection-drain.js`. Step 4a, 2Gi, **no Scheduler trigger** and none wanted: it is dry-run by default and `--apply`-gated) and `xbrl-backfill` (MONEY-1114; step 4b **DELEGATES** to `setup-xbrl-backfill-job.sh` and passes its own resolved `$IMAGE` through, so one provisioning run pins every job in the env to the same image and there is exactly one place the XBRL spec lives) — off the EXISTING `stockbook-workers-{env}` image (resolved from the live service, so the jobs can never drift from what that env runs; env is **positional and required** — `setup-feed-backfill-job.sh {qa\|prod}`, matching `setup-infra.sh`; a bare call prints usage and exits 1 rather than defaulting to prod. `--image=` to pin, `--dry-run` to print). `feed-backfill-prod` / `feed-parse-prod` provisioned 2026-08-09+ and battle-used for the P3 backfill. | `setup-tiingo-backfill-job.sh`                            | **MONEY-1263 / MONEY-1264** — deploy the **three Tiingo US backfill Cloud Run jobs** in one env: `tiingo-us-eod-backfill-{env}` (`dist/tiingo-us-backfill.js`), `tiingo-us-corp-actions-backfill-{env}` (`dist/tiingo-us-corp-actions-backfill.js`) and `tiingo-us-news-seed-{env}` (`dist/tiingo-us-news-seed.js`). Env is **positional and required**; `--only=eod\|corp\|news` to deploy one, `--image=` to pin, `--dry-run` to print. Image resolved from the live `stockbook-workers-{env}` SERVICE and **fails closed** on an empty resolution — an empty `--image` is read by `gcloud` as "keep whatever is there", which silently preserves the stale pin this script exists to move. **Three jobs, not one parameterised job**: `gcloud run jobs execute --args=` REPLACES the container's args, so an entrypoint carried in `--args` is dropped by every parameterised execution — hence the script path goes in `--command` and `--args=""` clears the list (there is no `--clear-args` on `jobs deploy`). **Timeouts are derived per walk, not copied**: EOD 24h (Cloud Run's max — 72,685 listings, LATENCY-dominated at ~10h, so the ~3.7h pacing figure is a floor), corp-actions 12h (~18,600 ex-date requests), news **1h on purpose** — a 90-request walk is done in ~2 min, so a still-running hour later IS the signal, and it costs nothing because all three checkpoint. `--max-retries=0`: a retry restarts a multi-hour walk straight back into the metered account that just cut it off, and Tiingo publishes **no remaining-quota header** (verified — a successful EOD call returns only `x-frame-options`), so a 429 is the only feedback channel. `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE` is **per-env: archive on prod, replay on QA**, and `GCP_PROJECT_ID` / `GCP_STORAGE_BUCKET` are set EXPLICITLY — a job spec does NOT inherit the service's env block, and without them the walk reports success having stored no bytes (measured on the BSE job: 59 rows with an empty `gcsPath`, MONEY-1258). `TIINGO_API_KEY` is a secret; `TIINGO_PLAN=COMMERCIAL` is a plain var, set explicitly so "absent" and "explicitly COMMERCIAL" are not one state (MONEY-1214). **No Scheduler trigger, ever** — this is one-shot HISTORY; the nightly freshness leg is the `us-eod-sync` push worker. **RUN THEM IN SEQUENCE**: the EOD and corp-actions clients hold SEPARATE throttles against ONE account-wide quota, so two concurrent walks ask for 2x the ceiling and neither knows. **The argv dialect is the EQUALS form** (`--args=--apply,--from=1990-01-01`) and is the **INVERSE of `setup-bse-backfill-job.sh`'s** space form — do not copy that script's examples; `--symbols` additionally collides with `--args`' own comma split and needs gcloud's `^:^` separator escape. **Preflight:** the resolved image must contain all three `tiingo-*` tsup entries, else the container dies with `Cannot find module /app/dist/tiingo-us-backfill.js`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Once, to provision; again after any release that changes the three `scripts/jobs/tiingo-us-*.ts` scripts — re-running is what re-points the pinned image. **Probe first:** run the NEWS seed dry (~90 requests, ~2 min) before the 24h EOD walk — nothing in this repo has yet fetched `api.tiingo.com` from Cloud Run, and it exercises the token, the client, Prisma over the mounted secret and the GCS archive. Env is **positional and required**; `--only=harvest\|append`, `--harvest-tasks=N`, `--image=`, `--dry-run`. Image resolved from the live `stockbook-workers-{env}` SERVICE and **fails closed** on an empty resolution — gcloud reads an empty `--image` as "keep whatever is there", silently preserving the stale pin the script exists to move. **BOTH ARM FLAGS SHIP PRESENT AND `"false"`, ON THE JOB AND NOT IN THE CLOUDBUILD SERVICE SPECS**, for two measured reasons: a Cloud Run JOB does not inherit the SERVICE's env block (the `TIINGO_RT_EPHEMERAL_TTL_S` shape — a var that shipped in both specs and was read by nobody), and the two heredocs measure 9,196 / 9,174 characters against `cloudbuild-arg-limit.test.ts`'s 10,000 ceiling with a 700-character headroom floor, so two more entries each would fail that test. PRESENT-and-off rather than absent because absent and explicitly-off must not be one state (MONEY-1214, `MF_METADATA_PROVIDER`). **Arming is a deliberate `gcloud run jobs update … --update-env-vars=DATABENTO_HARVEST_APPLY=true`** — `--update-env-vars` MERGES, `--set-env-vars` REPLACES THE WHOLE BLOCK and would drop the bucket, project, DD_ENV and the budget in the same breath, leaving an ARMED job that archives nothing and reports success. `--max-retries=0`: a retry restarts a multi-hour walk against a PAID vendor billed on batch SUBMISSION, and both jobs resume from their own Mongo inventory, so re-running is a human decision. `DATABENTO_API_KEY` is a secret (an HTTP Basic credential); `DATABENTO_MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD=25` is a plain var because an operator must read the spend ceiling at a glance. **No `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE`, deliberately** — the archive lives under `databento/`, OUTSIDE `feeds/`, because `sync-prod-to-qa-feeds` rsyncs that whole prefix twice daily and this one is TB-scale (same call as `edgar/`). **No Scheduler trigger, ever**: the harvest buys HISTORY, which is one-shot, and the append is triggered by a harvest that left a batch job outstanding. **QA never spends** — both flags are `"false"` in BOTH envs and the SAME account key is mounted in each, because there is one bill; disarmed, the jobs call only the FREE `metadata.get_cost` / `metadata.get_dataset_range` and cannot reach a paid endpoint. `--harvest-tasks` defaults to **1**, unlike `setup-doc-ingest-backfill-job.sh`'s 4: one execution takes ONE manifest entry, so N tasks are N copies of it unless the job shards inside the entry — and the memory ceiling is PER SLOT. **Preflight:** the resolved image must contain both `databento-*` tsup entries, else the container dies with `Cannot find module /app/dist/databento-harvest.js`. **`lake-load-databento-{env}` (`--only=lake-load`) runs AS `lake-writer-{env}@`** (MONEY-1544 branch): the first four in-region runs on 2026-09-23 exited 1 with "Access Denied" on `trade_bar_h1` as `stockbook-api@`, which is granted nothing on BigQuery, and stranded 43 `LOADING` ledger claims. So it mounts NO `GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY_BASE64` (`googleCredentials()` resolves that key FIRST and would put `stockbook-api@` back) and NO `DATABENTO_API_KEY` (it reads the archive and buys nothing), `companyfacts.zip` 1,407,685,214 B (measured), against a Pub/Sub push ack deadline of **600s that cannot be raised**; the Form 4 walk is ~1.4M filings and is expected to span several 24h executions. `--max-retries=0` — all five checkpoint into `backfill_checkpoints`, so an interrupted run costs TIME and never WORK, while an auto-retry would restart a multi-hour walk straight back into a host that just applied SEC's ~10-minute fair-access block. **`--set-env-vars` is explicit and includes `SEC_EGRESS` (archive on prod, off on QA), `GCP_STORAGE_BUCKET`, `EDGAR_IDENTITY`, `EDGAR_LOCAL_DATA_DIR` and `EDGAR_PARSE_APPLY=false`** (present-and-off, so "absent" and "deliberately off" are different states — the three backfills arm per-execution with `--apply`, and without it they fetch, parse and COUNT but store nothing and checkpoint nothing) — a job spec does NOT inherit the service's env block, and edgartools mkdirs its data dir AT IMPORT against a read-only Cloud Run FS, so an unset `EDGAR_LOCAL_DATA_DIR` kills the container before the first log line. **`--set-secrets` includes `REDIS_URL`, which the Tiingo jobs do not need**: edgartools' own limiter is a per-PROCESS `InMemoryBucket`, so a backfill pacing to 9 req/s beside a detect service pacing to 9 req/s puts 18 on a 10 req/s ceiling with neither able to see the other — the shared Redis bucket is the only thing that makes the FLEET rate real, and it fails CLOSED. **Scheduler:** **three of the five ARE scheduled** — six triggers, by `setup-scheduler.sh`, on prod only. `edgar-bulk-submissions-daily-prod` 13:00 IST + `-retry-prod` 16:00 and `edgar-bulk-companyfacts-daily-prod` 13:20 + `-retry-prod` 16:20 — two passes each because SEC's documented 03:00 ET publish time and its measured 23:5x ET Last-Modified disagree by 3h, and an unchanged file exits 0 `NOT_YET_PUBLISHED` after one HEAD. `edgar-bulk-ftd-semimonthly-prod` `0 19 3,18 * *` + `edgar-bulk-ftd-retry-prod` `0 19 10,25 * *` — **four runs a month for a twice-monthly feed**, because there is no date-templated URL to aim at (the index page is the only discovery source and its 410 `cnsfails*.zip` links span four directories, two with a `_0` suffix, so a date-built URL is wrong for 201 of them). The job is checkpointed per period, so over-running costs one index page and re-fetches nothing, while under-running leaves the `SEC_FTD` tier a fortnight stale and every 13F holding of an affected issuer symbol-less for that long. 19:00 IST keeps it clear of the 13:00-16:20 band, where `submissions` (3h timeout) and `companyfacts` (6h) can still be streaming ~1.5 GB. The two BACKFILLS get **no trigger, ever** — one-shot history. It fills the `SEC_FTD` tier of `us_cusip_map`, whose coverage is **partial and rolling by construction** — a security appears only while it has an open fail — so its outcome reports a hit count (`cusipsResolved`/`cusipsSeen`), never a completion percentage. **Preflight:** the resolved image must contain `app/bulk/{submissions,companyfacts,xbrl_backfill,ownership_backfill,ftd}.py`, else the container exits with `No module named app.bulk.submissions`. **MONEY-1278 — the jobs now carry `DD_METRICS_TRANSPORT=http` and `DD_API_KEY`.** They have no `datadog-agent` sidecar (the SERVICE does), and DogStatsD is fire-and-forget UDP, so every counter was refused by a closed port and dropped in silence while `[job-outcome]` logged perfectly — a metric that never arrives reads NO DATA, not an alert. `app/store/outcome.py` POSTs to the v1 intake instead; without the key it no-ops. `tests/test_deploy_contract.py` pins both directions, so a service that GAINS `http` (bypassing its own healthy sidecar) fails too. **`--condition=None` on the bucket grants** (fix/MONEY-1544): the content bucket now also holds a CONDITIONAL binding (`setup-databento-jobs.sh`'s `databento/`-scoped read for `lake-writer@`), and gcloud refuses an unconditioned add to a policy holding conditions unless told the new binding is deliberately unconditioned.                      | Once, to provision; again after any release that changes the five entry points' arguments or the env they need — the service's own `cloudbuild.{qa,prod}.yaml` re-pins `--image` on every release, but only this script CREATES the jobs and owns their env/secret block. **Probe first:** `/health` on the service reports the Mongo ping, Redis ping, pinned edgartools version, resolved limiter mode and every `EDGAR_*` flag state — every piece the long walks depend on, for zero SEC requests. Then a single-CIK run (`--args=^:^--ciks=0000320193:--from-fy=2025:--to-fy=2026`, ~20 requests) before the 24h walk.                             |

| `databento-inventory.ts`                                  | **S0b / MONEY-1463** — READ-ONLY inventory of the Databento harvest. Reads every `DatabentoBatchJob` row, HEADs each file named in its `files` array against GCS **by the ledger's own `archiveKey`**, counts the `candles` rows carrying `source: DATABENTO`, and writes `docs/reference/databento-inventory-{YYYY-MM}.md` with a harvested/verified/loaded/bytes/consumer row per entry. **Never calls `listFiles`** — it fails open to `[]`, so a listing can never support an "it is missing" verdict; a test asserts the symbol is absent. FOUR presence states, not two: `getFileMetadata` returns `null` for an absent OBJECT but THROWS for a bad credential, so `UNKNOWN` reports as _unverified_ rather than _missing_ (an expired ADC token made all 66,046 files throw during development). Expiry is the EARLIER of the vendor's `tsExpiration` and `submittedAt + 30d`, because a re-purchase risk must never be under-stated. `--month=YYYY-MM`, `--skip-gcs`. ~3 min for a full pass at 32-way concurrency. Pure half in `@usemoney/core/ops/databento-inventory`. Measured 2026-09-14: **977 GiB / 1.4M objects (re-measure per run; the count moves with every ingest)**; `edgar/filings` 490 GiB (50%), `stocks/` 399 GiB (41%, **no lifecycle rule**). Commissioned to price a US-region move that was **cancelled the same evening** (plan v2.3 = single region); retained as the record v2.3 §1 cites, with a banner saying so. **The ONE place a listing is allowed** — the output is a size report, not a decision about presence — and it uses `streamObjectSummaries`, which THROWS rather than failing open to `[]` like `listFiles`, because a truncated listing renders as a smaller prefix. ONE walk of the whole bucket grouped client-side, so the total is a measured denominator; refuses to write if grouped != walked. Sub-prefixes are DISCOVERED (the brief named `edgar/13f`, which does not exist, and 4 of `feeds/TIINGO/`'s 7 children). | `setup-lake.sh`                                           | **S1 / MONEY-1465** — provision the lake for one env: bucket `stockbook-lake-{env}` (asia-south1, uniform access, public-access-prevention, versioning OFF, lifecycle `lake/stage/` 14 d + `rollback/` 45 d), the four datasets `lake_{bronze,silver,gold,private}_{env}` with **default table expiration OFF**, and SAs `lake-{writer,reader,private-writer}-{env}@`. IDEMPOTENT — every resource is read before it is created. Contract: `docs/reference/lake-layout.md`; `lake-infra-coverage.test.ts` fails when the two disagree. **Grants `stockbook-api@` nothing on BigQuery** (the web request path never queries it). Lifecycle is READ → MERGE → write-union with a FAILED READ FATAL, mirroring `setup-gcs-lifecycle.sh` — it does not call that script, whose `bucket_for()` is hardcoded to the content bucket. SAs are env-suffixed (deviating from the brief) because one project-level writer would accumulate `dataEditor` on both envs' datasets. The $50/mo BigQuery budget is a **founder step** — `billingbudgets.googleapis.com` is not enabled and budgets are a billing-account operation; the script prints the exact command. **Step 4c (MONEY-1533 / MONEY-1518) grants `lake-writer-{env}@` `objectCreator` + `objectViewer` on its OWN bucket and READS THE POLICY BACK**, refusing unless both unconditioned bindings hold. Measured 2026-09-23 the writer held nothing there, so every job running as it failed at its first GCS step (the `lake/stage/` uploads of `lake-replay-in` / `lake-replay-us` / `filing-registry-sync`, and `lake-release`'s EXPORT DATA, which BigQuery writes as the caller). No delete: `lake/stage/` is cleared by lifecycle, and a release's immutability becomes an IAM fact. If the first cut refuses on `storage.objects.delete`, swap to `objectUser` there, knowingly. | `setup-lake-jobs.sh`                                      | **MONEY-1518 (B7) / MONEY-1521** — the Cloud Run jobs that run AS `lake-writer-{env}@`, never `stockbook-api@` (and so mount NO `GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY_BASE64`, which would outrank the runtime SA): `lake-release-{env}` (MANUAL, no trigger; PLAN mode — no `--apply` in its args and `LAKE_RELEASE_APPLY` PRESENT and `"false"`, so a cut is a per-execution override the script prints) and `event-lake-sink-{env}` (HOURLY, `10 * * * *` IST, `--apply` in its deployed args, because the scheduler POSTs `:run` with no overrides and a dry-run sink would let nothing expire). `{qa\|prod} [--dry-run] [--image=IMG]`. Pins the image off the live `stockbook-workers-{env}` service and prints its SHA. VERIFIES `setup-lake.sh`'s step-4c bucket grant read-only and refuses without it; grants only `DD_API_KEY_{ENV}` (both) and `DATABASE_URL_{ENV}` (the sink) `secretAccessor`, plus `run.invoker` on the sink job. **MONEY-1609 — it also deploys the five MANUAL lake loads** (`lake-replay-in`, `lake-replay-us`, `filing-registry-sync`, `corp-action-lake-sink`, `identity-sync`) as `lake-writer-{env}@`, with no key, no default args and no scheduler: pass `--args` per execution, one table per execution (gcloud splits on commas). The replays get a CONDITIONED `objectViewer` on `stockbook-content-{env}`'s `feeds/` and `candles/`. Sizing: the replays 24 h / 4Gi, `filing-registry-sync` 6 h / 2Gi, `corp-action-lake-sink` 1 h / 1Gi, `identity-sync` 2 h / 2Gi. Inside a Cloud Run job `--env` must match the job's suffix — `pinJobEnv` refuses `identity-sync-prod --env qa`. | `udf-parity.ts`                                           | **A3 / MONEY-1468** — READ-ONLY parity between the live Yahoo-backed `/api/market/udf/history` and the tile-backed market-data service, 60 listings x 6 ranges, before the chart is flipped. **Yahoo is the BASELINE, not the authority**: on a closed Indian session bhavcopy wins by `mayWritePriceSource` (MONEY-1175), so such a disagreement is recorded as an _explained_ divergence and does not fail the run — a run that "fixed" tiles to match Yahoo would regress to the weaker source while reporting success. Tolerances are never tuned; every real failure is a wrong adjustment, a missing year tile or a timezone split. The sample is DERIVED from the database and the run REFUSES a short cohort, because a run over the listings that happened to be easy proves nothing about the ones that are not; an EMPTY run is likewise not a pass. Needs `UDF_PARITY_CLERK_TOKEN` (the web route is behind Clerk — without it every baseline 401s and the run reports total failure for an auth reason, which looks exactly like a broken tile build). | `setup-tile-build-job.sh`                                 | **A2 / MONEY-1467** — deploys the Cloud Run jobs `tile-build-d1-in-{env}` and `tile-build-d1-us-{env}` from the workers image. TWO jobs rather than one parameterised job because they follow DIFFERENT producers on DIFFERENT schedules (India after `bhavcopy-sync`, US after `us-eod-sync`); one job with a cron-supplied `--market` would make "which market am I" a property of the trigger, and a mis-set argument would silently build the wrong market. **BOTH environments**, unlike the metered jobs — it reads our own Mongo and spends nothing, and QA holds its own `daily_prices` (QA-DIRECT), so QA must build its own tiles or its charts have none. **REFUSES** when `gs://stockbook-lake-{env}` is absent: tiles live in the LAKE bucket (the `tiles/` prefix routes there), not the content bucket, and a job that ran without it would write nothing and report success. `LAKE_ENV` is set explicitly because a Cloud Run JOB does not inherit the service env block — without it `initTileStore()` registers no store. Re-run after EVERY workers deploy: **a job pins its image at provision time** and deploying the service does not move it. **MONEY-1541 — prod charts read these tiles (`MARKET_DATA_TILES=true` since 2026-09-17), so `tile-build-d1` now adjusts India exactly as the live chart does:** the chart's own action query (`adjustingActionsWhere` — both exchanges, every former symbol, the ISIN, with `source` + `details` so the OBSERVED gap and the DERIVED rules apply), and a Yahoo row only for the actions after its `createdAt` (the MONEY-1382 basis; Yahoo had already applied the rest). `ADJUSTMENT_POLICY_VERSION` 2 forces ONE deep rebuild of every listing — `mustReadDeepHistory` reads the whole history so closed years are rewritten too, not just the 5y window. **MONEY-1543:** that query now runs through `loadAdjustingActions`, which refuses a row whose ISIN issuer is known and is not the listing's (FOCUS, WORTHPERI, ZEAL, RAJPUTANA) and admits and counts one nobody can name; the `done` line prints both as `actions_refused=` and `actions_issuer_unknown=`. so building them there is redundant — and it was also broken: measured 2026-09-18 `tile-build-d1-us-qa` was provisioned 2Gi/1CPU/1task against the 8Gi/2CPU/4 this script specifies (a job PINS its config at provision time and the QA jobs predate the MONEY-1479 sizing), so it was OOM-killed nightly and `gs://stockbook-lake-qa/tiles/US/` had never existed. The override remains because a change to `tile-build-d1` still needs somewhere to be run for real before it reaches prod. |
| `market-data-smoke.sh`                                    | **A1 / MONEY-1466, extended by MONEY-1469/1470** — the POST-DEPLOY proof for one env, read-only (every request is a GET). **A4/A5 add the GATE checks**: `/quotes` refusing an anonymous caller (it shipped once with no auth at all, accepting `market=US`, which resolves to the live Tiingo socket keyspace — a licence breach that answers 200), the US licence refusing `market=US` without the `us` claim, India quotes actually SERVING (a check that only ever sees refusals cannot tell a working gate from a broken route), intraday refusing as a **200 `s:"error"`** rather than a 401 the widget cannot parse, daily bars staying public, and `X-Bar-Basis` being in `access-control-expose-headers` — without which the browser strips it cross-origin, the badge reads null, and that is byte-identical to correct daily behaviour while working perfectly in local dev. It MINTS its token from the same Secret Manager value the service verifies with, so a Vercel/GCP secret mismatch surfaces as a gate failure rather than a mysteriously dead chart. Asserts, rather than prints for eyeballing: the service answers at all (**this is where a missing `allUsers`→`run.invoker` shows as 403** — the ALB sends no identity token and `services replace` never touches IAM, so the whole LB ladder can provision green and refuse every request); Cloud CDN actually **serving from the edge**, retried, because the FIRST request is always a MISS and so a single `curl -I` proves nothing; each route's `Cache-Control` — `/udf/history`'s graded against the IST WALL CLOCK rather than accepted as either legal value, because a check that accepts both cannot see an inverted session detector, and where `no-store` now means the chart is BLANK (MONEY-1475: the route emits a CDN value only when it HAS bars, and an unconfigured tile store answers `no_data` for every symbol because `readTile` yields `absent` rather than `failed`, so nothing else goes red for it); `/udf/time` in **seconds** (a 12-digit answer is milliseconds, which puts the chart ~55,000 years ahead); the 501s returning `{s:"error"}` and never `{s:"no_data"}`; the request id **absent** from cached responses and echoed on `no-store` ones; and that the **run.app origin refuses direct traffic**, which is the only check that proves ingress is still restricted once `allUsers` can invoke. Every failure it looks for is SILENT in the product — TradingView renders an empty chart, never an error. A check that could not run is **SKIPPED and still fails the run**: "not measured" must not read as "verified" (MONEY-1238). **DOES cover the Datadog span** — check 8 queries the spans API for the traffic checks 1-6 just generated, which is the one silent failure no HTTP response can reveal: the dd-trace ESM loader fails quietly, traffic served, zero spans, no error. This line said it merely printed a link for eyeballing; that was stale.
