| ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `feed-backfill.ts` | Walk each `report-registry` family **backwards** from today to its `startDate`, pulling every trading day through `getFeedAsset()` into `gs://<bucket>/feeds/`. Checkpoints every day to `FeedBackfillProgress` (`@@unique([family, date])`) so a killed run resumes instead of restarting. Ends a range on the **K-miss rule** (`DEFAULT_K_MISS=20` consecutive misses _below the known floor_ — not just 20 misses, because early archives are sparse and non-monotonic). Per-host backoff + stop-loss on repeated throttling. `--families=`, `--from=`, `--to=`, `--rps=`, `--dry-run`. **`--special-sessions` (MONEY-1613)** walks ONLY the weekend special-session calendar (`packages/core/src/market-data/india-special-sessions.ts`, 93 candidate dates) for the families the four India lake tables read; a file is archived only when its own date column is the requested day (another day's file → MISSING `[session-date]`, unproven → ERROR, retried). PROD-only: it throws outside `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE=archive` unless `--dry-run`. Run it as `--args="--special-sessions"`; the default families are every India family except `bse-cm-legacy`, which has no date column and can never prove a weekend session (BSE cash weekend sessions 2006–2016 stay a gap). A checkpoint is DONE only when the object was STORED (an archive write that fails or times out is a retried `transport` ERROR). Residual: the four delivery months (2019-10, 2020-11, 2024-01, 2024-05) resolve only if NSE serves the weekend's own key md5-identical to the next-weekday stale copy; the own-DATE1 fallback is not built. `feed-parse-nightly` is listing-driven, so it writes every archived weekend file into Mongo `daily_prices`, derivatives and index closes the same night.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Ad-hoc, **sharded one family per execution**. A bare run plans every family and will exceed the 24h task timeout. Needs founder go-ahead: it is hours of exchange traffic on the same egress the nightly sync depends on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `feed-parse.ts`    | Read what the walk archived out of GCS and write `DailyPrice` (`BHAVCOPY_HIST`) + `DerivativeEOD`. 30 families wired in its `PARSERS` table, spanning the legacy and UDiFF eras on both exchanges. **MONEY-1101 (P7)** added the two BSE legacy cash families (`bse-cm-legacy` 2006→2024 with NO ISIN column, `bse-cm-isin-legacy` 2017→2024 with one), which are also what unblocks `bse-gross-delivery`: SCBSEALL is UPDATE-ONLY, so every pre-2024 BSE delivery row was counted `no-price-row` for want of a `DailyPrice` to attach to. Their parser returns **`null`, not `[]`, on an unrecognised header** — the file is then left un-checkpointed and retried instead of being recorded as an empty trading day (BSE moved a column mid-2017, so this format demonstrably drifts). Idempotent via a pre-filter query per file (**not** `skipDuplicates`, which Prisma types as `never` on MongoDB). Exits **1** when `totalFilesSeen === 0` — a GCS auth failure otherwise reads as "0 files, parse complete" — and **1** again when files were read but **zero rows** parsed across every family (MONEY-1169: thousands of envelope-corrupted files each parsing to nothing looked exactly like success). **MONEY-1204 (P14 14b.1/14b.2)** added the debt and market-wide-OI families: `nse-wdm-list` + `nse-corporate-bond-report` -> **one** `BondMaster` (joined on ISIN, the only identifier both files print), `nse-cbm-trades` -> `BondEOD` (the price series the ~12k DEBT rows in `InstrumentMapping` have never had, since the cash bhavcopy does not carry debt at all), and `nse-combine-oi-deleq` -> `MwplDaily`. Three rules those writers add and the next debt family must keep. (1) **`BondMaster` has no date in its key**, so an older file re-walked after a newer one would roll the master back with no error: a row is left alone when the stored `asOf` is strictly newer, counted as `OLDER-THAN-STORED`. (2) The two master files have **disjoint columns** — wdmlist has no Issuer/Face Value/Credit Rating column at all — so the update path passes `undefined` for every incoming null and a file can never blank a field it does not publish (MONEY-1093's `mapToUpdate` rule). (3) The archive key is a legitimate date **only** for `BondMaster.asOf`, and only because wdmlist prints no date in any column; `BondEOD.date` and `MwplDaily.date` come from the row's own cell and their writers do not take `day` as a parameter at all. `writeBondEod` also **measures** how many of its ISINs have no master row and shouts `NO-MASTER-ROW-FOR-ANY-ROW` on a total miss — it never drops such a row, because a price with no master row is still a price and a silent zero join is the MONEY-1085 shape. `MwplDaily.mwpl` / `.utilisationPct` are written as the literal null on purpose: only the numerator has a source (`mwpl_cli` 404s with zero archived objects, and `oi_cli_limit` publishes a per-CLIENT limit, a different quantity). All three collections are in `REFERENCE_COLLECTIONS` in `sync-prod-to-qa.sh` in the same commit as the writer, because `feed-parse-nightly` is prod-only and QA would otherwise never see them (MONEY-1198). **MONEY-1238** — a file that put **not one row in front of the database** is now recorded `ZERO`, never `DONE`. The status comes from `decideParseStatus` (`parsed - unaccounted`) instead of the parser's row count, so rows a writer dropped before any query ever ran (`unresolved-scrip`, `no-price-row`, `dropped-unresolved`, `empty-slug`, `dropped-undrawable`) can no longer stamp a file complete — `ZERO` is retried, `DONE` is skipped forever and used to need a hand-purge of `FeedBackfillProgress`. Deliberately NOT counted as unaccounted: rows the database compared and declined (`skipped` source-precedence, `older-than-stored`) and rows dropped by policy (`fo` pre-2018 retention), because those are decisions a retry cannot change and counting them would re-parse complete days nightly forever. The BSE scrip-master join is now a `{ ok, reason }` result rather than a memoised `null`: an EMPTY `feeds/BSE/scrip-master/` listing is read as a STORAGE failure (`listFiles` returns `[]` for that and for an empty prefix alike, and says so in its own comment), so the BSE families **throw `FeedPrerequisiteUnavailable`** — un-checkpointed, retried, run exits **1** — instead of resolving nothing and being checkpointed complete. Deliberately not `UnreadableFeedFile`: that status is skipped on resume, which is the same poisoning under a different label. The run also emits a `reportJobOutcome` (`selected` = files opened, `written` = rows that reached the database), which the `feed-parse | 36h                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | daily` zero-write monitor had sat in NO DATA waiting for since MONEY-1220. | After a backfill shard lands. GCS -> Mongo only, no exchange traffic, so this one is safe to run wide. |
| `feed-repair.ts`   | **MONEY-1169** — strip the multipart upload envelope that the broken `uploadFile` path stored as object content (every `feeds/` + `stocks/` object for ~3 months). Recovers the intact payload in place — **zero exchange traffic** — restores contentType/cacheControl from the envelope, and verifies magic bytes (PK/%PDF/JSON/not-HTML) before replacing anything; unverifiable objects are left alone and reported. **Dry-run by default**, `--apply` to write; idempotent (clean objects skipped, safe to re-run mid-way). Writes go through the FIXED resumable+CRC32C `uploadFile`, so running it doubles as a live test of the fix. `--prefixes=`, `--bucket=`, `--concurrency=`. Exits 1 on `scanned=0` or any failure. **`--audit` (MONEY-1204 / 14a.1) is a STRICTLY READ-ONLY second mode**: it classifies every object from a RANGED read of its first 512 bytes plus the declared size — no full download, no write — and prints a per-family `ok / empty / envelope / html / invalid` table. `--audit` IGNORES `--apply`, because a read-only mode a stray flag can upgrade to a writing one is not read-only. `empty` and `envelope` are deliberately SEPARATE verdicts: an envelope is repairable in place from bytes already held, a zero-byte object can only be re-fetched (and for a 2005 file may be unrecoverable), so collapsing them turns a re-fetchable gap into a permanent one. Classifier is the pure `packages/storage/src/archive-audit.ts`, co-located with `isMultipartEnvelope` so there is exactly one implementation of "is this corrupt". Exits 1 on `scanned=0` or a head-read error; a FINDING exits 0 so it can run on a schedule with the table as the signal. **MONEY-1321 M14 — the `tooLarge` warning was telling the operator the wrong number.** It read "`--audit` counts the same population as `envelope-too-large`". It does not: `tooLarge` is incremented from `size > --max-bytes` alone, BEFORE any download, so it counts every oversized object under the prefix — a 200 MB annual report included — while `envelope-too-large` is a strict SUBSET the classifier reaches only after confirming a MONEY-1169 envelope over an INTACT payload. Reading them as one population sends the operator to re-download tens of GB to repair a small fraction of it. The warning now says so and names the audit's count as the one to size the decision by.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Once on **PROD** (`feeds/` + `stocks/`) after the MONEY-1169 fix deploys. Re-run safe. **Do NOT run it against QA** — `stocks/` is mirrored from prod by `sync-prod-to-qa.sh --gcs-only`, and prod is already repaired, so a resync overwrites QA's corrupt objects with known-good bytes. Repairing QA separately re-derives an answer we already have, and QA's `stock_documents` rows are prod's rows anyway (they come down in the reference tier), so the objects must match prod's or the paths resolve to the wrong bytes. |
| `fo-summary.ts`    | **MONEY-1100 P4.4/P4.7** — derive `DerivativeSummary` (futures OI + day-over-day change, basis vs cash, PCR OI/volume, max pain, rollover %, OI-buildup class) from our own `DerivativeEOD` rows, one row per (underlying, exchange, trading day). Math in `computeDerivativeSummary` (@usemoney/core, unit-tested); this is the I/O harness. Underlying cash close via `InstrumentMapping`→`DailyPrice` (stocks) or `INDEX_UNDERLYING_SLUG`→`IndexClose` (indices); misses are counted, never guessed. Default = latest EOD day (the nightly increment); `--date=ISO` one day; `--backfill` every day missing summaries, ascending, with a rolling prev-day cache so each day is read once. Idempotent per day (delete-then-createMany under the unique). No-ops loudly on QA (no `DerivativeEOD` there yet).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Nightly on prod via `fo-summary-nightly` (23:30 IST, after `feed-parse-nightly`); `--backfill` once after the P4 deploy. ---

**`corp-actions-backfill.ts`** (MONEY-1176) — one-off history backfill for
`corporate_actions`. Builds nothing new: `corporate-actions-sync` already
fetches the whole market from NSE+BSE, already chunks to `<=180` days, already
parses ratios. It had only ever RUN over a recent window (5,878 of 5,898 rows
sat in the current year), and the processor's own header even names this job.
Driven from a script rather than by setting `CORP_ACTIONS_SYNC_DAYS` on the live
worker, because that env var is one forgotten rollback away from making the
DAILY job re-pull twenty years every night. **Probed for a row cap first** —
the sibling `historicalOR` deals endpoint hard-caps at 70 rows and silently
turns a backfill into a biased sample; this one does not (7d:39 → 365d:2,645).
Persists only STRUCTURAL types by default: `OTHER` is 81% of volume and, checked
across 5,561 sampled rows, is entirely bond servicing with zero splits hiding in
it. `--years`, `--chunk-days`, `--types=ALL`, `--skip-nse`, `--skip-bse`,
`--apply`. Ran on prod 2026-08-12: 5,472 created, qty-changing 59 → 3,276.
**MONEY-1189** — `DIVIDEND` is now IN the default type set (founder-confirmed):
the old exclusion was right for CHARTS but wrong for the ticket, whose own
acceptance test is "RELIANCE dividends >= 20 back to 2005" against a table that
held 2. Marks and adjustment cannot regress by construction — `MARKED_TYPES` and
`QTY_CHANGING_TYPES` both exclude `DIVIDEND` and the adjustment fingerprint
queries only qty-changing rows. `OTHER` stays out. The volume that made the
exclusion attractive is handled by `upsertCorporateActionsBulk` (one keyed read
per chunk + `createMany`, instead of a `findUnique` + write PER ROW; the
row-by-row path is kept for the small nightly). Second prod run 2026-08-12:
25,408 created, `DIVIDEND` 1,589 → 26,815, qty-changing only +122.
**MONEY-1189 follow-up** — the run now ENDS on a completeness verdict and exits
NON-ZERO when any window failed, printing each range plus a ready-to-paste heal
command. It previously printed `emptyChunks=0` while 37 NSE windows and 1 BSE
window had silently failed (both fetchers returned `[]` for "asked, never
answered" and "answered, nothing there" alike), leaving dips at 2013 and 2017
that read as macro. Three causes were fixed together: the NSE retry now
re-handshakes (a stale cookie makes NSE HANG, so all three attempts replayed the
same dead credential), `retry: 0` stops ky replaying the same request
underneath, and `normalizeActionType` learned NSE's pre-2010 abbreviations
(`Agm/Div-Rs.2/- Per Sh`) which had been fetched and then discarded as OTHER.
Prod re-walk 2026-08-13 (`--years=23 --nse-window-days=90`): DIVIDEND
30,387 → 42,759, the 2009/2013/2017/2020 dips filled (420→1,189, 721→1,852,
642→1,766, 755→1,769), ITC 2010→2005, INDIGO 2025→2016. qty-changing +681 —
real splits/bonuses inside the previously-failed windows, so adjusted charts for
those symbols legitimately re-derive. 7 BSE windows still failed (BSE was
rate-limiting that day) and are the printed heal command's job.
`--from-years=N` walks only windows STARTING on/before N years ago, so the deep
end can be retried without redoing the recent chunks. Needed because the NSE
bulk leg is the fragile one: it was ONE attempt at a flat 15s returning `[]` on
timeout — and `[]` is also what an empty window returns — so three timed-out
2006-07 windows read as NSE's known ~2020 history plateau rather than as
failures, and the dividend series silently stopped at 2008. It now retries 3x
with a GROWING timeout (30/45/60s), because a deep window is slow precisely
because it is big, so repeating a deadline already known to be too short is not
a retry.

### The projection DRAIN — `jobs/xbrl-projection-drain.ts` (MONEY-1235)

`xbrl-backfill.ts` fills `xbrl_filings` from NSE. **Nothing walked
`xbrl_filings` and filled `stock_financials`** — that script calls only
`processXbrlSync` and contains zero `stockFinancials` references, and no Cloud
Run job ran the projection at all. This is the missing runner, provisioned by
[`ops/setup-xbrl-projection-drain-job.sh`](ops/setup-xbrl-projection-drain-job.sh).

**Why the nightly worker cannot do it.** Measured on prod 2026-08-19:
`xbrl_filings` holds **90,188 unprojected** rows (71,450 QUARTERLY, 18,381
ANNUAL, 357 HALF_YEARLY) against **0 QUARTERLY** `stock_financials` rows. The
`xbrl-projection` push worker's limit is 500 — sized for Pub/Sub's un-raisable
600s ack deadline, where overrunning gets the message REDELIVERED and starts a
second concurrent copy — and its scheduler payload is deliberately scoped
`{"periodTypes":["ANNUAL"]}` so unstamped quarterly filings cannot starve annual
work. 71,450 / 500 is ~143 nights.

**The 600s ceiling does NOT apply here and must not be re-imposed.** This is a
Cloud Run JOB: no subscription, no message, no ack, `--task-timeout=86400`. The
binding constraints are memory (the writer holds a whole page of `facts` JSON)
and the run's own wall-clock budget, and both are explicit flags.

**Arming takes BOTH halves.** `XBRL_PROJECTION_APPLY=true` in the job's env AND
`--apply` on the execution. A flag can never arm a run the environment has not
armed — `StockFinancials` has no `source` in its unique key, so the first XBRL
write to a period destroys the incumbent value with nothing left to reconcile
against, and a one-way door must not be openable by an argument. The comparison
is against the literal string `"true"`, never `Boolean()`: `Boolean("false")` is
`true`, which would arm the job in the one configuration meant to keep it off.

**Termination is the part that had to be proven.** The cursor is
`XbrlFiling.projectedAt`, stamped by the WRITER on filings it decided about — so
a page advances only because the previous page stamped. The writer deliberately
stamps **nothing at all** on a dry run (a rehearsal must leave no trace, or the
first armed run skips everything it "decided"), and deliberately does not stamp
`notDecidedIds` — create-suppressed and no-instrument, neither of which is a
property of the filing. Each of those is a page that reads thousands of filings
and moves the cursor zero, so `while (filingsRead > 0)` would spin for the full
24h task timeout writing nothing. The loop stops on an empty page **and** on a
non-advancing one, measured by counting the scoped unprojected population before
and after each batch; all five stop reasons are proven in
`workers/__tests__/processors/xbrl-projection-drain-logic.test.ts`.

| stop          | meaning                                                                     | exit |
| ------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---- |
| `drained`     | no unprojected filings left in scope                                        | 0    |
| `dry-run`     | unarmed: ONE batch, nothing stamped — not a truncation                      | 0    |
| `no-advance`  | a page read filings and the population did not shrink                       | 1    |
| `max-batches` | the runaway guard (default 200)                                             | 1    |
| `budget`      | the 6h wall-clock budget, checked BETWEEN batches (it cannot interrupt one) | 1    |

`no-advance` on a QUARTERLY run almost always means `--create-missing` is off:
with zero incumbent quarterly rows every write is a CREATE, every CREATE is
suppressed, and a suppressed create is not a decision — so batch 1 stalls. That
is correct for an ANNUAL drain and wrong for a quarterly one.

**Flags** (dry run is the default; an unrecognised argument is FATAL, unlike
`xbrl-backfill.ts` — a mistyped `--apply` would otherwise run a silent dry run
and exit 0, indistinguishable from a deliberate rehearsal):
`--apply` · `--dry-run` · `--create-missing` ·
`--period-types=ANNUAL,QUARTERLY` · `--limit=N` (default 2000, max 5000) ·
`--symbols=A,B` · `--max-batches=N` · `--budget-sec=N`.

**Pilot before the full drain.** A CREATE is by construction a row the
reconciliation never compared, and a 100%-create run is also the exact signature
of a `periodLabel` join bug. Run scoped and dry first, then armed and scoped,
then READ THE ROWS against the filings — not the counters — before widening.

**Batches, arithmetic rather than guesswork.** ~`ceil(population / limit)`, plus
a little: a page that hits the limit DEFERS its trailing symbol
(`dropPartialTrailingSymbol`), costing about half a symbol's filings per batch.
71,450/2000 = 35.7 → ~36; 90,188/2000 = 45.1 → ~46; 71,450/5000 = 14.3 → ~15;
90,188/5000 = 18.0 → ~19.

Every non-zero exit resumes for **free** — `projectedAt` on the rows IS the
checkpoint, and the writer SKIPs a row whose values already match. There is
deliberately no checkpoint table, for the reason `xbrl-backfill.ts` gives at
length: the MONEY-1189 postmortem is a catalogue of checkpoints recording DONE
for work that never happened.

> **`scripts/jobs/xbrl-projection-drain-logic.mts` is the only `.mts` in the
> repo, and the extension is load-bearing.** `scripts/` inherits the root
> `package.json`, which declares no `"type"` (CommonJS); `workers/` declares
> `"type": "module"`. A test under `workers/__tests__/` is therefore ESM
> importing CJS, and Node has to recover the named exports by static analysis —
> measured on this file, it recovers **only `default`**, so every named import
> throws at instantiation. Nothing else in this repo imports a `scripts/` module
> from a workspace test, which is why the boundary had never been crossed.
> `.mts` is ESM regardless of the nearest `package.json`. The job ENTRY POINT
> deliberately stays `.ts`: `job-build-provenance.test.ts` parses tsup's entry
> map with `/"\.\.\/scripts\/jobs\/([\w-]+)\.ts"/`, so a `.mts` entry would
> drop silently out of the guard that checks every Cloud Run job reports its
> build. Import it as `./xbrl-projection-drain-logic.mjs` — the specifier both
> TypeScript (`moduleResolution: bundler`) and esbuild map back to `.mts`.

### The Tiingo backfills' argv contract (MONEY-1263)

`tiingo-us-backfill.ts`, `tiingo-us-corp-actions-backfill.ts` and
`tiingo-us-news-seed.ts` share one `parseTiingoCliArgs` (byte-identical, pinned
by `tiingo-script-decisions.test.ts`) that accepts **only** `--flag` or
`--flag=value`. The SPACE form is refused, and that strictness is load-bearing:
`--to 1999-12-31` used to fall back to the default and walk 1990-2026 instead of
1990-1999.

`gcloud run jobs execute --args=` is a **comma-separated list**
(`arg_parsers.ArgList`), and the equals form passes through that split unharmed:

```
--args=--apply,--from=1990-01-01,--to=1999-12-31
    -> ["--apply", "--from=1990-01-01", "--to=1999-12-31"]      ACCEPTED
```

**Do not copy `setup-bse-backfill-job.sh`'s dialect.** `backfill-bse-results.ts`
demands the SPACE form and REJECTS equals — the exact inverse. Fed to a Tiingo
script, `--args=--apply,--from-year,2024` dies on arg parsing. MONEY-1261 lost a
whole chain to this in the other direction, because two producers agreed with
each other and disagreed with the parser.

**`--symbols` collides with gcloud's own delimiter.** It takes a comma list, and
so does `--args`:

```
--args=--apply,--symbols=AAPL,MSFT
    -> ["--apply", "--symbols=AAPL", "MSFT"]                    REJECTED
--args=^:^--apply:--symbols=AAPL,MSFT
    -> ["--apply", "--symbols=AAPL,MSFT"]                       ACCEPTED
```

It fails loud — exit 1 before the first request — never a silently narrowed
symbol set.

### `jobs/tiingo-us-backfill.ts` — US EOD history from Tiingo (MONEY-980 / MONEY-1263)

```bash
# Cloud Run Job — the real walk. ~3.7h; a laptop cannot hold it open.
gcloud run jobs execute tiingo-us-eod-backfill-prod --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in            # dry run (default)

gcloud run jobs execute tiingo-us-eod-backfill-prod --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in --args=--apply

gcloud run jobs execute tiingo-us-eod-backfill-prod --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=--apply,--only-delisted,--limit=500

gcloud run jobs execute tiingo-us-eod-backfill-prod --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in --args=--apply,--retry-terminal

# --symbols needs the ^:^ delimiter — see the ARGV note under `jobs/`.
gcloud run jobs execute tiingo-us-eod-backfill-prod --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=^:^--apply:--symbols=AAPL,MSFT
```

Provisioned by `ops/setup-tiingo-backfill-job.sh {qa|prod}`. The local form
below still works and is genuinely useful for a **dry run** against a scratch
`DATABASE_URL` — the arg parser and the selection query are exercised without
spending quota — but it is NOT how the real walk is run:

```bash
node --import tsx scripts/jobs/tiingo-us-backfill.ts                      # dry run (default)
node --import tsx scripts/jobs/tiingo-us-backfill.ts --apply --symbols=AAPL,MSFT
```

Walks the 72,685-listing US universe (15,629 equities, 7,642 ETFs, 49,414
mutual funds) and pulls each symbol's ENTIRE history in ONE request — measured,
AAPL returns 11,517 bars back to 1980 in a single 2.4s call, so this is ~73k
requests rather than one per symbol-year (~3.7h at the Commercial plan's
20,000/hour, ~10 GB of the 100 GB monthly allowance).

`--apply` is a BARE FLAG. MONEY-1252: a wrapper forwarding `--flag=value` had
its `--apply` swallowed and a 54-minute "apply" run wrote nothing while exiting 0. Dry run is the default.

Checkpoints per symbol in `candle_backfill_checkpoints`, and the terminal
statuses are the point of the table: `EMPTY`, `NOT_FOUND` and `RECYCLED` all
produce ZERO rows, so without a checkpoint they are indistinguishable from
never-attempted and would be re-fetched every run forever. Measured examples —
`ENE` (Enron) 404s, `LEH` (Lehman) returns 200 with an empty series, and `DOW`
returns Dow **Inc.** (2019-) when asked about Dow **Chemical** (1972-2017),
which is refused rather than written under the wrong identity. `--retry-terminal`
re-attempts those.

Stops the whole run on a rate limit or an auth/entitlement failure rather than
grinding through the remainder collecting the same error; the checkpoint means
the next run resumes exactly where it stopped.

Run on PROD, where `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE=archive`. QA gets the same bytes from the
nightly feeds mirror instead of spending the quota twice.

Rate ceilings come from `TIINGO_PLAN` (`COMMERCIAL` default → 20k/hr, 150k/day,
100 GB; `REDISTRIBUTION` → 80k/hr, 1.2M/day, 1 TB). See MONEY-1262.

### `jobs/tiingo-us-corp-actions-backfill.ts` — US dividends + splits (MONEY-1263)

```bash
# Cloud Run Job. JOB=tiingo-us-corp-actions-backfill-prod
gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=--from=2020-01-01                                   # dry run (default)

gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=--apply,--from=1990-01-01,--to=1999-12-31

gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=--apply,--from=2020-01-01,--limit=20

gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=--apply,--from=2026-08-01,--retry-terminal
```

Provisioned by `ops/setup-tiingo-backfill-job.sh {qa|prod}`. **Never run this at
the same time as the EOD walk** — the two clients hold separate throttles
against one account-wide quota. Local form, for dry runs only:

```bash
node --import tsx scripts/jobs/tiingo-us-corp-actions-backfill.ts --from=2020-01-01
```

Fills `UsCorporateAction` (**not** `corporate_actions` — that table's readers
scope by SYMBOL and not by exchange, which is correct for India and false the
moment US rows share it: 214 active Indian symbols collide with a US ticker, so
a US split filed under `ACC` would mis-adjust a real ACC Cement cost basis).

**A DAY walk, not a symbol walk.** Measured: the market-wide route takes exactly
one `exDate` and REJECTS `startExDate`/`endExDate` — there is no market-wide
range query, and omitting `exDate` is a `400 {"detail":"Error: Must provide an
exDate."}`. That constraint turns out to be the better axis anyway: **~522
requests per YEAR** of history (261 weekdays x 2 routes) regardless of universe
size, against ~145,000 for a symbol walk over 72,685 listings — ~94 seconds
versus ~7.3 hours at the Commercial plan. It also reaches securities no master
still lists, which a symbol walk structurally cannot.

`--from` is **REQUIRED**; there is deliberately no default, because Tiingo's
history reaches 1962 and an implicit floor would launch a ~33,000-request walk
nobody asked for. `--to` defaults to **YESTERDAY**, not today: today's ex-dates
are still landing, and a partial day checkpointed `DONE` is never re-walked.
`--limit` counts DAYS.

`--apply` is a **BARE FLAG**. MONEY-1252: a wrapper forwarding `--flag=value`
had its `--apply` swallowed and a 54-minute "apply" run wrote nothing while
exiting 0. Dry run is the default.

Checkpoints per **(day, route)** in `candle_backfill_checkpoints` under
`jobKey = "tiingo-us-corp-actions"`, reusing that table with two aliased
columns — `symbol` holds the EX-DATE (the population is days) and `exchange`
holds `DISTRIBUTIONS` / `SPLITS`, so a run killed between a day's two fetches
does not redo the one that finished. The alias is documented at the top of the
script; the alternative was a second five-column model plus a schema migration.

Three measured facts the run depends on, none of them documented upstream:

- **An empty day answers `200` with the literal four-byte body `None`** — not an
  empty CSV, not `[]`. Verified on Sunday 2026-08-23 and on the 2026-01-01
  market holiday, both routes. Only the CSV serializer does this; the JSON form
  correctly returns `[]`. It is checkpointed `EMPTY` (terminal, so a holiday is
  never re-fetched) and never archived — 4 bytes is below the `FEED_SHAPES`
  floor, which is correct: there is nothing to replay.
- **`format=csv` is undocumented and saves ~74% of the bytes** (11,240 vs 42,809
  for the same 189 rows). Bandwidth is a metered limit on this plan.
- **The batch DUPLICATES tickers.** `splits?exDate=2026-08-25` returns two rows
  both `ticker: "reax"` with DIFFERENT `permaTicker`s — two real companies. The
  dedupe is on `(permaTicker, type, exDate)` and never on the ticker; the run
  reports `duplicatesDropped`, which is expected to be **0**.

`listingId` is resolved through a WINDOW test, not a ticker lookup: an action
whose ex-date falls inside a listing's `[start, end]` gets that `listingId`, and
anything outside gets `null` rather than a guess. That matters because
`supported_tickers.csv` has no `permaTicker` column, so the only available join
is the ticker — and `selectPrimaryListings` keeps only the SURVIVOR of a recycled
ticker, so a naive map would file Dow Chemical's 1990s dividends under Dow Inc.
Measured on a live dry run: **514 of 600 rows resolved (85.7%)**, the remainder
correctly refused. Costs one extra request per run (the master).

Stops the whole run on a rate limit or an auth/entitlement failure rather than
grinding through the remainder collecting the same error, and **exits non-zero**
when it does, so a wrapper reading only the exit code cannot mistake
"rate-limited after 3 of 250 days" for success.

Run on PROD, where `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE=archive`. **Do not run it at the same time
as `tiingo-us-backfill.ts`**: the two clients hold SEPARATE throttles against ONE
account-wide quota, so concurrent runs pace to up to 2x the ceiling each believes
it is respecting. The excess is bounded (~522 requests inside a 20,000/hour
budget) and the durable fix is one exported pacer — see the module header of
`tiingo-corp-actions.ts`.

Runtime utilities for fixing / augmenting reference data. Run as needed - not
part of any recurring pipeline.

Measured on prod 2026-09-05: **1,170 of 8,282 (14.1%)**. Classifies every CIK-less symbol by ticker SHAPE (`classifyUsTickerShape` in `packages/core/src/market-data/instruments/edgar-cik-logic.ts` — pure, and reporting-only) and prints counts + real examples: plain 770 / warrant 164 / unit 125 / right 53 / shareClass 30 / preferred 28, plus 33 exchange TEST symbols (`NTEST-P`, `ATEST-A`, `ZXYZ-A`) which are a cross-cutting flag rather than a shape and can never have a CIK. The `cik`-absent filter is spelled `OR: [{cik: null}, {cik: ""}, {cik: {isSet: false}}]` — on Prisma+Mongo `{cik: null}` alone matches only documents that CARRY the key, so it would return ZERO for exactly the rows this report exists to find (MONEY-1050/-1205/-1234/-1235/-1236). **NO GATE, deliberately:** whether SEC lists a given SPAC warrant is SEC's editorial fact about the market, not a measure of our ingest, so a threshold here would fire every time a quarter's IPOs list and be muted within a month. The thing to watch is the SHAPE, which is why the enricher now emits `unmatchedByExchange` on its outcome line. Flags: `--json`, `--examples=N` (default 8, capped 50). | `reclassify-instruments.ts`              | **MONEY-1093 / MONEY-1298** - retype mistyped cash instruments away from `EQUITY` (bonds/G-Secs the Zerodha dump types `EQ`, plus index pseudo-rows) and clear their bogus sector. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT — `--apply` required to write.** Classifies from the exchange's own settlement series via `classifyCashInstrument`, never from a symbol pattern. Has a READINESS GATE that refuses `--apply` while `series` is still the stamped default. `--env qa\|prod` (Secret Manager), `--limit`, `--keep-sector`. **EVERY `--apply` requires typing "yes"** and echoes the masked target host first — not just prod, because the documented `DATABASE_URL=... --apply` form bypasses an `--env`-keyed check. **`--apply` writes `reclassify-rollback-<ts>.json`** into `scripts/data/rollbacks/` (repo-relative, NOT the cwd — MONEY-1204; see that directory's README.md) BEFORE any write: clearing `sector`/`industry` destroys values that exist nowhere else, and a retyped row is excluded from re-enrichment (the enrichment pipeline queries `instrumentType: "EQUITY"` only), so that file is the recovery path — keep it. Retypes **DEBT + INDEX**; REIT/INVIT are also mistyped but retyping them would drop the ~7 real trusts' news/filings sync, which gate on `EQUITY` (INDEX has no such cost — nobody holds an index — and prod holds zero INDEX rows, so `getIndices` returns an empty list today). MONEY-1298 added three feed-supplied rules on top of the series column: `segmentCode == "INDICES"` (213 rows -> INDEX), the NSE series recovered from the trading symbol's `-XX` suffix (7,149 rows -> 6,172 DEBT / 876 EQUITY untouched), and BSE rows on the stamped default with no ISIN whose Kite `name` is the instrument code repeated (5,392 rows -> 5,288 DEBT / 104 abstain). The `name` test is load-bearing: without it that BSE rule types ~45 real companies (TEMPSENS INSTRUMENTS, NOVYRA PHARMACHEM, GLOBAL DEFENCE INDUSTRIES) DEBT, which removes them from the screener and clears their sector with no re-enrichment path back. Absence of an ISIN is a GATE, never a verdict — AUGMONT ENTERPRISES is a real newly-listed NSE company with no ISIN yet. The `--apply` WHERE now pins every decision input it can — `series`, `segmentCode` and ISIN-absence — so a row whose evidence moved between the plan and the human's "yes" is skipped and reported, not retyped on stale evidence.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

Needed because changing `YAHOO_TO_NSE_MAP` only affects FUTURE writes — the enrichment pipeline only fetches rows whose sector is UNSET, so a row already stored as "Financial Services" is permanently excluded from re-enrichment. Matches on the stored raw `industry` string, so NO network calls. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT.** The dry run prints EVERY distinct industry it matched plus the near-misses it rejected, because the only way to know a text matcher is right is for a human to read what it caught. `--apply` writes `resector-rollback-<ts>.json` into `scripts/data/rollbacks/` (repo-relative, NOT the cwd; see that directory's README.md) first and always prompts. `--env qa\|prod`, `--limit`, `--from SECTOR`. | `resector-noncanonical.ts`               | **MONEY-1302** - the GENERALISATION of `resector-real-estate.ts`: re-maps ANY off-taxonomy `InstrumentMapping.sector` value through `YAHOO_TO_NSE_MAP` instead of into one hard-coded target, and has no free-text matcher of its own — `toWritableNseSector` is the only decision, so this script and the enrichment writer can never disagree. Needed for the same reason as its sibling: a taxonomy edit only affects FUTURE writes, and enrichment only re-selects rows whose sector is null/empty. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT.** Two refusals carry most of the value. (1) A value the taxonomy does not resolve is REPORTED under UNMAPPED and left alone — the fix for that is a reviewed alias, not a heuristic here (`Other`, 29 prod rows, is the live example). (2) Only rows `classifyCashInstrument` positively confirms EQUITY/REIT/INVIT are written; abstentions are skipped, because 57 of the 101 off-taxonomy prod rows are DEBT wearing `instrumentType: "EQUITY"` (30 NHAI bonds under `Construction`, a Gujarat SDL and 25 debentures under `Other`) and re-sectoring a bond is unrecoverable. Measured dry run on prod 2026-08-30: 101 off-taxonomy -> 38 planned, 34 skipped as non-equity, 29 unmapped. `--apply` stages a rollback JSON in `scripts/data/rollbacks/` first and always prompts; it also refuses a non-TTY stdin, `--apply --limit`, unknown flags and the `--flag=value` form. `--env qa\|prod`, `--limit N`, `--only VALUE`, `--include-unclassified`. Also backfills the two new columns (`employeeTrustsPct`, `underlyingDrsPct`) that had nowhere to go before. Runs the REAL `parseShareholdingMaster` over `{data:[raw]}` rather than re-implementing the column rules — a second copy would drift from the writer on the next key correction. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT**, and the dry run is the deliverable: it prints an N/M completeness line per column (before -> after), which alias resolved each field, and the `unmapped` counter. | `probe-exchange-archives.ts`             | **MONEY-1096** (Data Engine P1) - reconnaissance, not remediation: pins which NSE/BSE archive files and API endpoints actually exist **from our egress**, and how far back, as the registry seed for P2/P3. **READ-ONLY — no Prisma, no GCS, no DB;** its only writes are `docs/reference/exchange-archive-probe.json` + `exchange-archive-ranges.md`. Safety is the design, because this is the same egress `bhavcopy-sync` uses nightly and an IP block would cost the platform its EOD prices: **1 rps globally**, a hard 60-request budget (overflow is recorded as `SKIPPED_BUDGET`, never silently dropped), and **stop-on-403 per host**. Imports the UA/Referer constants from `market-data/exchange-feed` rather than copying them, so the report always describes the client production actually is. Verdicts distinguish `PRESENT` from `HTML` — both exchanges answer missing files with **200 + an HTML page**, and scoring those as PRESENT is how a bogus floor date would enter the registry. `--dry-run` prints the plan and makes zero requests; `--only=<group>` filters, but **still overwrites both reports with only the groups it ran** — always run the whole thing to refresh the committed artifacts. **`--registry` (MONEY-1097)** switches modes entirely: instead of the P1 question set it probes every `REPORT_REGISTRY` row once against live and reports PRESENT/ABSENT, which is how the registry's `enabled` flag gets set from evidence instead of confidence. It writes its OWN pair of files (`exchange-registry-verification.{json,md}`) so it can never clobber the archive-range seed. Pair with `--max=80`, since 61 rows exceed the default 60-request budget; the 1 rps pacing and the 403 breaker are NOT adjustable. **`--only=bse-results` (MONEY-1255)** is the BSE results-XBRL group: it answers where a BSE company's results XBRL instance lives (`Result_Arch_ng/w?scrip_cd={SCRIP}` — per scrip, no date range, no paging) and keeps two DISPROVED leads in the probe deliberately, each with a control beside it, because both are 200s that look like answers: `GetCorXbrlDetails_ng` category 22 is literally named "Financial Results" and returns zero rows (category 7 returns 3,510 over the identical range, so the empty is real and not a param error), and `CorpXbrlGen.aspx` serves 200 `text/xml` with an `<xbrli:xbrl` root that is the _announcement_ taxonomy carrying no financial facts. Its instance probes follow the index's OWN links rather than reconstructing filenames, which is what surfaced BSE's own index defect: integrated-era NBFC filings are indexed under `NBFCUploadDocument/` and 404, while the files serve from the sibling `IFNBFCUploadDocument/`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `verify-chart-series.ts`                 | **MONEY-1175** - answers "is `DailyPrice` good enough to draw a correct chart at every timeframe?". **READ-ONLY.** The TradingView daily path is DB-backed (`udf/history` → `getHistoricalPrices`) and plots the raw `close` column, never `adjClose`, so a chart is exactly as correct as this table. Checks coverage, per-timeframe density vs a market calendar derived from the data itself, candle validity, duplicate dates, and — the important one — **price continuity where `source` FLIPS**. That last check is the split-adjustment test: `DailyPrice` is unique on `(instrumentId, date)` so two sources can never share a date and a same-date comparison is impossible; what IS observable is a jump at the seam. A jump ON a corporate-action ex-date is expected in a raw series, a jump at a source boundary with NO action means the two sources are on different price bases. `--symbols`, `--exchange`, `--jump-pct`, `--json=<path>` to record a baseline, `--compare=<path>` to diff a later run against it. Grouped by (source × era × class) so a defect concentrated in one source/era reads as a parser fix while a scattered one reads as bad prints. `--samples`. | `derive-corporate-actions.ts`            | **MONEY-1176** - recovers corporate actions the exchange feeds cannot reach, from the price series itself. NSE's corp-actions API plateaus around 2020 (measured: its leg stopped at 5,634 rows while BSE kept climbing) but `DailyPrice` reaches 1994-11-03. Trustworthy only BECAUSE of MONEY-1175: the series is uniformly RAW now, so a clean ratio jump is a real event rather than possibly the source flipping between raw bhavcopy and split-ADJUSTED Yahoo. A split moves price by an EXACT rational (arithmetic, not sentiment), so a candidate is accepted only when the observed ratio snaps to a plausible corporate ratio within 3% — RELIANCE's 2006 demerger (1/0.634 = 1.577) deliberately does NOT snap, because calling it a 1:2 bonus would rescale the whole pre-demerger history by a made-up number. **Does not guess the TYPE**: a factor of 2 is equally a 1:1 bonus or a 2:1 split, so rows are written `CAPITAL_ADJUSTMENT` with `details.observedRatio` and `source: "DERIVED"`, which ranks below every real feed in price-adjust's precedence. Volume corroboration is a SCORE, never a gate (illiquid names have noisy volume). Candidates within 3 days of a known feed action are discarded. Two guards exist because the FIRST production run got them wrong: a **Rs 20 price floor** (205 of 361 derived rows landed under Rs 20 and 55 under Rs 1 — 21STCENMGM produced seven "splits" in three years between Rs 0.05 and Rs 4.65, because 0.30 and 0.15 are 6 and 3 ticks so their ratio is EXACTLY 2 by arithmetic accident, and a 50% day is ordinary volatility at penny prices), and a **cap of 6 actions per instrument** which discards ALL of that instrument's candidates when exceeded (ANTGRAPHIC yielded 28 and BLUECHIP 15 — not companies with 28 splits, but series whose behaviour defeats the detector, so the individual candidates cannot be trusted either). **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT.** `--apply`, `--symbols`, `--min-rows`, `--min-confidence`, `--limit`.                                                                                                                                                                              |

They are "Payment of Interest" (3,383), "Redemption(Part) of NCD" (256) — debt servicing from BSE's bond universe, invisible to charts because they SHOULD be. | `backfill-financials-basis.ts`           | **MONEY-1300** - stamps `stock_financials.basis = "UNKNOWN"` onto the VENDOR rows. **NO NETWORK, NO RE-PARSE** - one raw `$set` of one column. Measured on prod 2026-08-31: **32,359 rows, `source: "TICKERTAPE"` on 100% of them, and `basis` ABSENT on all 32,359** - the column exists in the schema but a Mongo `db push` creates INDEXES, never FIELDS. **The value is a NAMED string, never null and never left absent**, which is the whole ticket: Prisma's Mongo connector matches `{ basis: null }` against an explicit JSON null only, so BOTH `{ basis: null }` and `{ basis: { not: null } }` return ZERO here, and an explicit null would still be invisible to `{ not: null }` and to a positive `in` list - it would move the rows from one unreadable state to another. `"UNKNOWN"` makes all three states separable by one ordinary equality: `UNKNOWN` = vendor row, basis not recorded; `CONSOLIDATED`/`STANDALONE` = the projection stamped it; ABSENT = a row nothing has touched yet. **Selection is raw `$runCommandRaw` with `$exists: false`** (the `ops/backfill-user-isactive.ts` pattern) - one filter document, `stampFilter()`, drives the census, the samples AND the write, so the plan an operator judges and the plan that executes cannot drift (the `backfill-syncbucket.ts` defect). **`NSE_XBRL` rows are refused** by a `$nin` against `XBRL_SOURCE` and counted in their own bucket - the projection owns their basis. Rows carrying an EXPLICIT null are out of `$exists: false`'s reach by construction, counted separately, and zero on prod. **IDEMPOTENT structurally** - the selection is "basis absent" and the write gives those rows a basis, so a second run matches and modifies zero; nothing to reset after an interrupted run. Never calls `mayOverwriteFinancials`, and that is not an omission: the write is gated on the cell being ABSENT, so there is nothing to displace, and the authority question is answered by the filter rather than after the fact. The dry run **prints the defect** - the raw `$exists:false` count beside `what a Prisma where can see`. Post-write verification goes through **Prisma**, not the raw predicate it selected with (MONEY-1236): the adversary is a write that lands `basis: null`, which would take the raw "still missing" count to zero and report success while nothing readable was written. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT**; `--apply` prompts, echoes the masked host and requires a TTY. `--env qa\|prod`, `--samples` (capped at 50 - a raw `find` returns only `cursor.firstBatch` and this script issues no `getMore`). Run it on **prod**: `stock_financials` is a REFERENCE collection in `sync-prod-to-qa.sh`, so QA receives the stamped rows and a QA run is undone by the next restore.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

Samples ONE file per month (a rename persists for months, so this cannot miss one while cutting ~9,000 downloads to ~420). The rule that keeps it honest: a rename is a **succession** (old stops when new starts), a variant is a **coexistence** — that one test rejects rights entitlements and series variants (`CITIUSINVT` vs `CITIUSINVT-IV`, same ISIN, same days) without a suffix blocklist that would rot. Also guards against symbol REUSE (never alias a ticker the exchange has reassigned to a live company) and single-sighting parse noise. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT.** A short seed list covers demergers whose successor got a NEW ISIN (`TATAMOTORS` → `TMCV`), which no amount of ISIN-grouping can recover. | `repair-fo-summary-gap.ts`               | **MONEY-1189** - re-derives the missing NSE F&O summaries. `derivative_summary` held BSE rows and NOTHING from NSE for **456 contiguous trading days** (2024-07-08 -> 2026-05-13): 2024-07-05 has 186 NSE rows, 2026-05-14 has 214, every day between has zero. Three completeness checks at the wrong granularity each reported success — `feed-parse` correctly parsed and checkpointed all 456 days; `fo-summary --backfill` selected targets as `eodDates - summaryDates` at **DAY** granularity, so a day already carrying its BSE half looked done and the NSE half was never derived; then `pruneOldEod` gated on `summarised.has(day)` — also day granularity — and deleted the raw rows step 2 had skipped. Step 3 is what made it permanent rather than merely late: the input was deleted on the strength of an assumption that the derivation had happened. Both granularity bugs are fixed in `fo-summary.ts`; this recovers the data. Re-parsing is unavoidable (EOD is pruned to ~3 months) but the archived bytes are intact, which is exactly what archive-first is for. Walks the gap in **monthly chunks** (456 days x ~40k contracts would be ~18M transient rows at once), each a full parse -> summarise -> **verify** -> prune cycle; a chunk that still has missing days keeps its raw rows and is NOT pruned. Purging a chunk's parse checkpoints is both how it re-parses AND how it stays confined to that month. **Measures the gap on every run**, so it is idempotent, self-terminating and resumable. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT.** `--apply`, `--from`, `--to`, `--only`.
