| `xbrl-backfill.ts`                       | **MONEY-1114 (P10 F2)** - walks NSE's results index month by month and lands each filing's XBRL into `XbrlFiling`, reusing the nightly `xbrl-sync` processor rather than reimplementing the write (a backfill with its own writer drifts from the nightly one, and then the two disagree about history). **`--from-year` defaults to 2018 and that is measured, not cautious**: a filing with no XBRL still carries a well-formed url ending in a bare dash, so a `startsWith('http')` check reports ~100% coverage back to 2012 - on the `.xml` suffix it is **0% through Feb-2018**, 89% May-2018, 97-98% from 2019. **NO checkpoint table, deliberately**: the processor skips a filing it already holds BEFORE downloading it, so the rows themselves are the checkpoint at exactly the granularity that matters, and the run is interruptible and idempotent for free (a coarser checkpoint recording DONE for work that had not happened is the MONEY-1189 failure). Handles NSE's **two indexes** - SEBI's integrated-filing regime split the API at the quarter ending Dec-2024, and the legacy endpoint stops receiving new filings there (2,402 records for Nov-2024, **31** for Nov-2025) - and the modern one's **silent 20-record default cap**, which returns the same 20 rows for a 6-day and a 46-day window. **MONEY-1200 - the two eras also serve two TAXONOMIES** (`in-bse-fin` vs SEBI's `in-capmkt`), and a wide window straddles the change, so the per-window line now reports `unknown-taxonomy` and `non-financial` (governance filings, which carry no numbers by design) alongside `unparseable`, and a run that ends with unknown taxonomies names them - the fix is one table entry in `xbrl-financials-logic.ts`. A failed window is labelled by CAUSE (`INDEX UNREADABLE` vs `PARSE HEALTH: ...`); before this, every failure read as an index problem. Exits **1** and NAMES the months when any window was unreadable or looked truncated, so a partial backfill cannot read as a complete one. `--dry-run` prints the window plan without touching the network. **MONEY-1204 (P0.2)** added two things. (1) `initFeedArchive()` + a `requireArchiveWired()` guard in `main()`, UNCONDITIONALLY: `xbrl-sync`'s download now goes through `getFeedAsset`, so every window this script walks is an archiving fetch — and `getFeedAsset` returns a perfectly good Response with no store registered, which would have meant fetching every filing from NSE, discarding the bytes, and exiting 0. `job-entrypoints-wire-archive.test.ts` could not have caught it either: it greps job sources for the literal `getFeedAsset` and the call is one module away. (2) `--archive-existing`, a SEPARATE pass for rows ingested BEFORE the archive existed. `processXbrlSync` skips a stored filing before the download — that skip is what makes the trailing window cheap and must not be weakened — so this walks `xbrl_filings` directly, probes GCS for each row's object, and re-fetches only the misses through the same `fetchXml` the worker uses. It writes nothing to the database. **Run it with `--dry-run` first**: that COUNTS the missing objects and stops, which is the number that decides whether this is a five-minute job or a multi-day one. Presence is probed by DOWNLOAD (`FeedArchiveStore` has `get`/`put` and no `exists`), so `--limit=N` and a 480 s wall-clock budget bound the pass; a run that stops early says so and reports its count as a FLOOR. The apply half refuses to run unless `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE=archive` — under `replay` every re-fetch would be served back or scraped and discarded while the pass counted it as re-fetched. |
| `xbrl-reconcile.ts`                      | **MONEY-1115 (P10 F3)** - **READ-ONLY**; writes nothing, ever. Answers whether `XbrlFiling` is good enough to become authoritative for `StockFinancials`, with evidence. Must run **BEFORE** the first XBRL write: `StockFinancials` has no `source` in its unique key, so once XBRL writes a period the Tickertape value for it is gone and there is no second copy left to compare against - which is also why `XbrlFiling` is a separate table rather than more rows in `StockFinancials`. Reports a **verdict ladder**, not a match percentage, because the four ways two financial figures differ need four different responses: `SCALE_MISMATCH` (a units bug - one occurrence implies a whole class of periods is wrong, so it **blocks** rather than counting toward a rate), `SIGN_MISMATCH` (discontinued operations or a loss convention - also systematic, also blocks), `DISAGREEMENT` (the only one a rate threshold suits), `MATCH`, `INCOMPARABLE`. INCOMPARABLE counts toward the **population**, so a source that mostly has no data cannot pass by comparing the handful it does have - the draining-denominator failure from MONEY-1196. Periods XBRL covers that Tickertape does not are reported as a **coverage gain** and excluded from the population, so breaking new ground cannot make the source look worse. Exits **1** when any field is blocked, and exits **1** on zero matched filings too - "0 disagreements" is what a successful run also prints. **MONEY-1235 — the gate now measures what the WRITER actually persists.** Two populations that used to be graded are now EXCLUDED and reported as their own buckets: filings the scale detector QUARANTINES (`judgeFilingScale`, the same call `xbrl-projection-writer` refuses on) and statements `planFinancialsWrite` would SKIP (`STANDALONE_NO_DISPLACE` — standalone-only is 29.5% of winner periods, and the writer will no longer overwrite a consolidated incumbent with one). Comparisons are TAGGED, never dropped, so recall and "how much did this remove" stay computable. Each per-field line now leads with the **surviving comparable count** (the old `match/compared` headline WAS the draining-denominator reading), states the excluded rows' own disagreement rate beside the survivors' — so a basis-mismatch hypothesis that does not hold gets SAID rather than inferred from a rate that failed to move — and prints **"READY RESTS ON THE EXCLUSION"** when a field's blockers were the excluded ones. Passes NO threshold overrides to `judgeSwitchoverReadiness`. `--symbols`, `--limit`, `--verbose`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | **MONEY-1239 — TRANSLATED is a REPORTED bucket, and the nulling trade is counted.** The gate blocked while our projection was CORRECT: adjudicated against screener.in as a third source, 3MINDIA reconciles 12/12 to the rupee against screener's CONSOLIDATED while the vendor matches STANDALONE. The two sides differ on exactly one line item — OTHER INCOME — which the vendor folds into `revenue` (their column is TOTAL INCOME) and into `operatingIncome` (EBIT INCLUDING other income). A DECLARED TRANSLATION (`ours + otherIncome`, both terms read off THE SAME FILING, never a fitted constant) now applies to those two fields ONLY: `netIncome` is deliberately excluded because both sides mean profit attributable to owners (p50 0.01%, 86.9% within 1%), so a translation there could only fire on a genuinely wrong pair. TRANSLATED is COUNTED AND PRINTED per field and never folded into `matched`, and each field prints the disagreement rate BOTH ways — with translations as matches (**the rate the verdict uses**) and with them demoted — judged by the SAME `judgeSwitchoverReadiness` with the SAME defaults and labelled "Reported, NOT applied": the threshold decision is the founder's. An absent `otherIncome` makes the pair `translationUnavailable` — a distinct value from a failed translation, still counted as a disagreement, because absence of evidence is not a match. Ordering is unchanged: SCALE > SIGN > TRANSLATION > DISAGREE. Separately a named **INCUMBENT-NULLING** block counts (symbol, period, field) pairs where arming replaces a POPULATED incumbent with NULL — `operatingIncome` needs `operatingProfitBeforeProvisions`, present in 76/741 banking and 0/206 NBFC ANNUAL filings, so ~90% of banks and 100% of NBFCs project none, and `StockRatios.operatingMargin` derives from it. Those pairs score INCOMPARABLE, which is outside the rate's denominator BY DESIGN, so the ladder structurally cannot see the loss; the count prints unconditionally (zero included) and is echoed into the VERDICT line. It does NOT block — it is a trade to accept before the one-way door, not a defect. **MONEY-1255 (D3) — `EXCLUDED_SYMBOLS`, the one exclusion here that does NOT measure the writer.** Every other exclusion removes a row because the WRITE WILL NOT HAPPEN; these rows WILL be written, so removing them makes the gate more permissive rather than more accurate, and it is printed above the ladder with that sentence. MODIRUBBER and OPTIEMUS only — they carry the only two surviving SIGN_MISMATCHes (and MODIRUBBER one of seven SCALE_MISMATCHes). CENTUM and DVL were on this list and are now in `xbrl-ratios-validate.ts`: they removed ZERO comparisons here, which is exactly what the print-every-symbol-including-the-zeros rule exists to surface. `nullsIncumbent` is keyed on the WRITER's skip, never on this list.                                         |
| `xbrl-ratios-validate.ts`                | **MONEY-1204 / 14a.4** — the RATIOS half of the XBRL switchover gate (`xbrl-reconcile.ts` covers `StockFinancials`; this covers `StockRatios`). **READ-ONLY by construction** — only `findMany`/`findFirst`, so `--apply` is meaningless. Gate: **>=95% of NON-BANK ratios within 5%**. **Compares ANNUAL filings only, and that is load-bearing:** `StockRatios` has NO period dimension (one row per symbol) and Tickertape publishes TTM, while `deriveRatios` deliberately does not annualise a quarterly filing — so a quarterly-vs-TTM comparison shows a systematic ~75% "disagreement" that is a PERIOD mismatch, not a data-quality one, and would block the switchover for the wrong reason. Symbols with no annual filing are counted apart, never folded into the rate. Banks/NBFCs/insurers are excluded (their ratios are DISCLOSED, and a bank's debt-to-equity is not comparable to an industrial's). SCALE and SIGN mismatches block outright rather than diluting into a rate — they are systematic. Exits 1 when the gate fails AND when nothing was comparable, because "we compared nothing" must never read as "everything matched". **MONEY-1235 — THREE verdicts, not two.** The ladder moved into `@usemoney/core/market-data` (`judgeRatioReadiness` / `judgeRatiosGate`, pure and unit-tested beside `judgeSwitchoverReadiness`); this script feeds it and prints the result. `UNVERIFIABLE` now covers the case where the VENDOR has no data — measured on prod, `operatingMargin` is 0 of 2,135 rows and only `roe` (2,038) has real coverage — which the old ladder BLOCKED on while simultaneously printing that the absence was "not evidence in either direction", making the arming precondition unsatisfiable by construction. Vendor coverage is checked FIRST, because a "scale mismatch" computed against 3 vendor rows is noise wearing the costume of a systematic error. Thin because THEY lack data does not block; thin because WE cannot derive what they could still does. A run where nothing blocked and nothing was READY FAILS — that would be a green verdict describing a comparison that never happened. `--symbols=`, `--limit=`, `--verbose`. Run via `./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh xbrlratios prod`. Runbook: `docs/reference/xbrl-switchover-runbook.md`. **MONEY-1255 (D3) — two SYMBOLS are excluded from every rate, and the report says loudly that this is not a declaration.** CENTUM (roe xbrl=-0.652 vs tickertape=+0.833) and DVL (-1.151 vs +4.684) carry the only two surviving `roe` SIGN_MISMATCHes, both from the attributable-vs-profitForPeriod ambiguity (`xbrl-projection.ts:1069`). They were originally declared in `xbrl-reconcile.ts`, where they removed NOTHING — their defect is here. Declaring the RATIO instead was rejected: `roe` is the only ratio with real vendor coverage (2,038 of 2,135 rows against 0/86/85/86), so `DECLARED_DIVERGENCE` on it would leave the gate passing on four UNVERIFIABLE lines. The block prints BEFORE the ladder, prints a row for every declared symbol INCLUDING one that removed nothing (the shared `tallySymbolExclusions`, seeded from the DECLARATION and not from the rows) plus a REMOVED NOTHING warning, and leaves the offending pairs in worst-offenders — labelled `[NOT GRADED — symbol excluded]`, sorted last — because those numbers are the evidence the exclusion rests on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Before arming the `StockRatios` switchover. | `xbrl-vs-pdf-ratios.ts`                  | **MONEY-1401 B6** — **READ-ONLY**, `--env {qa The VENDOR-FREE tie-break. Tickertape is dead (frozen 2026-03-21, 403 from our egress), so a `stock*ratios`disagreement can be neither inspected nor fixed. We hold TWO independent derivations of the SAME primary document —`xbrl_filings`and our own PDF parse in`document_financial_extracts`— overlapping on 4,624 (symbol, year) cells across 1,178 symbols, and BOTH sides run the SAME`deriveRatios`(via`extractToRatioInputs`), CLOSING equity on both, so a disagreement is about the NUMBERS and not the formula. Prints n / agree<=1% / agree<=5% / p50 xbrl÷pdf and the 10 worst per column, plus a THREE-WAY tickertape÷xbrl and tickertape÷pdf wherever a vendor row exists. **The interpretation rule is in the header and is the point of the script**: XBRL≈PDF and both ≠ vendor → a DECLARED divergence; XBRL ≠ PDF → one of OUR parsers is wrong, fix it and declare nothing. Measured on prod 2026-09-13 it returned the SECOND verdict: `roe`and`netMargin` agree between our two parses on only 72%/68% of cells within 1%, and the three-way says the PDF side is the wrong one (BELRISE roe xbrl 13.101 / pdf 1.310 — exactly 10x — / tickertape 14.067), consistent with UNKNOWN_SCALE being 96.6% of rejected extract rows. **`roce`is marked`roce\*`and is WEAK**:`deriveRatios`builds EBIT from`profitBeforeTax`+`financeCosts`, which the extraction prompt never asks for, so the PDF side falls back to `operatingIncomeExOtherIncome + otherIncome`— filled on 341 of 24,273 rows (1.4%;`revenue`/`operatingIncome`/`ebitda`on ZERO) — and n=15. An empty or thin column here is a FINDING, not a pass; do not declare a divergence on 15 cells. Resolves`DATABASE_URL*{QA                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | PROD}`from Secret Manager itself and NEVER prints it; imports the core barrel type-only and lazily, so no client can be pinned to a database`--env` did not name. | Before declaring any ratio a divergence; after `xbrl-ratios-validate.ts` blocks. Read-only, safe on prod. |
| `doc-financials-reconcile.ts`            | **MONEY-1255** — the document half of the financials gate (`xbrl-reconcile.ts` covers XBRL; this covers `document_financial_extracts`). **READ-ONLY by default**; with `--apply` it writes ONE column, `DocumentFinancialExtract.reconcileVerdict`, and nothing else, ever. It must run BEFORE any projection write — `StockFinancials` has no `source` in its unique key, so the comparison cannot be made afterwards — and the projection worker enforces that by refusing an ungraded extract (`requireGraded`, not overridable from a message). Same verdict ladder and the same `judgeSwitchoverReadiness` / `judgeFieldGateOutcome` as the XBRL gate, with **no threshold overrides**. **The field list and BOTH Prisma `select`s are derived from ONE constant** (`DOC_ALL_FIELDS`), because a hand-maintained select is the more dangerous copy: a missing column comes back `undefined`, which reads as "the source has no value" and printed `comparable 0 of 4516` for `totalDebt` while the table held 9,861 vendor values (MONEY-1250). `revenue` is graded as the DERIVED Schedule III total (`revenueFromOperations + otherIncome`), never as the extract's raw column, because that is what the writer writes. **INCOMPARABLE is broken down by CAUSE on every field line** — ours-blank (a coverage GAP, and the pairs where arming NULLs a populated cell) / theirs-blank (a coverage WIN) / both-blank — because it is outside the disagreement rate by design (MONEY-1196) and has hidden two broken pipes (MONEY-1234). **THE CEILING** is three nested counts, `comparable` of `joinable` of `examined`, and the run **exits 1** when the floor fails, so "0 disagreements over 0 comparisons" can never be reported as success: `NO_EXTRACTS` (the extractor has not run), `NO_JOIN` (pairs examined and not one addressed an existing row — the period-LABEL bug signature, where every write would be a CREATE; assert the innocent reading explicitly with `--expect-no-incumbents`), `BELOW_FLOOR` (<20 comparable; forgiven on a `--symbols`/`--limit` run, which cannot authorise anything). Prints `DOC_UNOWNED_VENDOR_COLUMNS` and `DOC_DECLARED_CONVENTIONS` verbatim, an unconditional INCUMBENT-NULLING count including the zero, and the roll-up distribution the projection gates on. `--symbols=`, `--limit=` (use an EVEN limit — one period occupies up to two extract rows, MONEY-1207), `--min-comparable=`, `--expect-no-incumbents`, `--verbose`, `--apply`. **If wired into `scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh` it MUST declare `APPLY_FLAG="--apply"`** — the wrapper consumes `--apply` and re-emits only its own declaration, so an empty one swallows the flag and the run reports everything and writes nothing, fail-safe and silent (41,924 rows read, 24,165 reported changed, NOTHING written, exit 0, 54 minutes). The MODE line printed first is the only in-script defence. Run before arming `DOC_FINANCIALS_APPLY`; founder-run against the prod DB.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

| `snapshot-vendor-fundamentals.ts`        | **MONEY-1401 B3** - SNAPSHOT the vendor rows of `stock_ratios` + `stock_financials` to `*_vendor_snapshot` siblings, and RESTORE them. **The reversibility the XBRL switchover is blocked on.** `stock_ratios` is `@@unique([symbol, exchange])` and `stock_financials` is `@@unique([symbol, exchange, statementType, periodType, period])` - NEITHER carries `source`, so the first XBRL write does not sit beside the incumbent Tickertape value, it ERASES it and the reconcile gate has nothing left to compare against. That is the one-way door `XBRL_PROJECTION_APPLY` has been blocked behind since 2026-08-30; with a snapshot in hand, arming it becomes a one-night experiment instead of a decision that has to be provably right in advance. Prod 2026-09-13: **2,135 ratio rows (100% `source: "TICKERTAPE"`) and 32,359 financial rows**. **DRY-RUN BY DEFAULT in BOTH directions**; `--apply` requires a TTY and typing "yes", and echoes the masked target host first - not just `--env prod`, because the documented `DATABASE_URL=... --apply` form leaves `--env` null and would bypass an `--env`-keyed check entirely. **The snapshot is IDEMPOTENT**: each document gets the deterministic `_id` `<snapshotDay>                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | <naturalKey>`and a re-run DELETES that day before inserting, so running twice on one day REPLACES that day and can never append a second copy - a bug that tried to would collide on`\_id` rather than silently double the collection. **RESTORE NEVER DELETES**: a row that appeared after the snapshot is left alone, because putting back what was taken and discarding everything written since are different promises. **RESTORE REFUSES TO GUESS WHICH SNAPSHOT** - with more than one day stored it names them all and exits rather than defaulting to the newest, since a second snapshot taken AFTER the projection ran holds XBRL rows and "newest" would restore exactly what you are undoing. The classification (`planSnapshot`/`planRestore`/ the per-collection natural key) lives in the sibling`snapshot-vendor-fundamentals-logic.ts`, which imports NOTHING - no prisma, no mongodb, no fs, no clock - so the decision that governs 34,494 production rows is assertable without a connection (`packages/core/**tests**/market-data/vendor-fundamentals-snapshot.test.ts`). Reads and restore-writes go through Prisma; the snapshot collections are not in the schema, so those are raw `$runCommandRaw`, keyset-paged on the string `\_id`because a raw`find`returns only`cursor.firstBatch`and this script issues no`getMore`. Run it on **prod**: both tables are REFERENCE collections in `sync-prod-to-qa.sh`, so a QA snapshot is undone by the next restore down.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

### `jobs/tiingo-us-news-seed.ts` — US news seed (MONEY-1264)

```bash
# Cloud Run Job. JOB=tiingo-us-news-seed-prod
gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in            # dry run (default)

gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in --args=--apply

gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=--apply,--from=2026-08-01,--to=2026-08-07
```

Day-walks the whole window Tiingo will serve — one request per calendar day —
archiving each day's raw JSON and writing `StockNews` for the symbols we carry.
Checkpointed per DATE, so a run killed halfway resumes where it stopped.

**Why a day-walk and not one range request**, all three measured: history is
**3 months** and asking for more does not fail (a pre-floor `startDate` returns
200 with rows from the floor, so one wide request would look like a successful
two-year backfill and deliver three months); the **offset ceiling is 10,000**,
past which every page returns zero rows — indistinguishable from "no more news";
and a **dated archive key needs a date**, which is what lets QA replay the seed
instead of spending the quota again.

`--to` defaults to **YESTERDAY**, not today: a day walked before it is over gets
a TERMINAL checkpoint, and on a feed whose history floor moves forward daily
that turns "stale" into "unfillable".

**This is the cheapest probe of the whole Tiingo path** — ~90 requests, ~2
minutes, ~16s of pacing at COMMERCIAL. Run it dry FIRST, before the multi-hour
EOD walk: it exercises the token, the client, Prisma over the mounted secret and
the GCS archive from inside Cloud Run. Nothing in this repo had fetched
`api.tiingo.com` from Cloud Run before MONEY-1263, and `api.tickertape.in`
answers 200 from a laptop and 403 from there (MONEY-1220).

At 90 requests a laptop CAN finish this one. Prefer the job anyway: the archive
credentials and `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE=archive` live there, and a laptop run seeds
rows without archiving the bytes QA replays.

### Every lake job cannot end silently (MONEY-1608 / MONEY-1609)

All nine lake jobs — the five `lake-*` plus `identity-sync`,
`corp-action-lake-sink`, `event-lake-sink` and `filing-registry-sync` — run
`main()` under
`guardCompletion` (`packages/core/src/ops/job-completion.ts`). If the event loop
empties with `main()` still pending, the job prints `ENDED WITHOUT COMPLETING`
and exits **3**, never 0: `lake-replay-us` exited 0 after 8 hours with 0 months
loaded, because an upload stream settled neither way and an awaited promise
holds no handle. Ledger DML and the silver month DELETE retry BigQuery's "Could
not serialize access … concurrent update" (`DML_CONFLICT_ATTEMPTS` = 6, jittered
backoff).

`lake-replay-us`, `lake-replay-in`, `lake-load-databento` and
`filing-registry-sync` upload their stage files under `STAGE_UPLOAD_POLICY` (a
5-minute deadline, 3 retries at 15 / 60 / 240 s) — the GCS SDK never retries an
upload that carries no generation precondition. **Retries are TRANSIENT-ONLY**
(`isTransientError`: network codes, HTTP 408 / 429 / 5xx, our own deadline); a
permanent error fails at once. **Stage writes are create-only**
(`ifGenerationMatch: 0`), because `lake-writer@` cannot overwrite: a refused
re-attempt is accepted only when the existing object has the same size and
CRC32C, and thrown otherwise. `lake-replay-us` also retries each symbol's reads
3 times; a full US replay is ≈ 8 h of walk plus 3–4 h of loads. `lake-replay-in` pins
`GCP_STORAGE_BUCKET` from `--env`. **Run them as the Cloud Run jobs**
(`setup-lake-jobs.sh`), never from a laptop: Workspace re-auth (`invalid_rapt`)
killed every local run twice on 2026-09-23.

### The lake ledger backfills (MONEY-1472, data-platform B1)

Two one-shot Cloud Run Jobs that fill `lake_bronze_{env}.source_object` from
inventories that already exist. Neither is scheduled: a cron would re-HEAD the
same objects nightly to rewrite rows that are already there.

**NEITHER LISTS GCS, and that is the point.** `listFiles` fails open to `[]`, so
a reader that asked the bucket "what is there?" records an EMPTY inventory on any
credentials blip — which in a LEDGER is worse than no row at all, because a
coverage query reads it as an answer.

### `jobs/identity-sync.ts` — security identity, SCD-2 (MONEY-1498, B3)

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/identity-sync.ts --env qa --seed            # dry run
npx tsx scripts/jobs/identity-sync.ts --env qa --seed --apply    # first fill
npx tsx scripts/jobs/identity-sync.ts --env prod --apply         # nightly diff
npx tsx scripts/jobs/identity-sync.ts --env prod --rebase-history          # plan
npx tsx scripts/jobs/identity-sync.ts --env prod --rebase-history --apply  # MONEY-1544
```

Fills `lake_silver_{env}.listing`, `.symbol_history` and `.source_instrument_map`,
and the `lake_gold_{env}.instrument_current` VIEW reads the first of them. DDL is
`packages/lake/ddl/003_silver_identity.sql` + `005_gold_instrument_current.sql`,
applied by `ops/lake-apply-ddl.ts`.

**A CLOUD RUN JOB, never a Pub/Sub handler.** It walks 74k US rows plus ~27k
India rows against a 600s ack deadline that is Pub/Sub's maximum; past it the
message is REDELIVERED and a second copy runs against the same table.

**`--seed` IS ONLY A LABEL.** It is deliberately NOT a truncate: the seed reads
the open rows and diffs exactly like the nightly, so re-running it over a
populated table is a no-op rather than a rewrite that discards every version
boundary already recorded. First run and thousandth take one path — the one that
is exercised every night.

**IT CLOSES ROWS, IT NEVER UPDATES ONE IN PLACE.** An identity change (symbol,
venue, market, isin, name, assetType, currency, status) sets `valid_to = today`
on the live row and INSERTS a successor starting today. Closes are written
BEFORE opens: the other order leaves two rows with `valid_to IS NULL` for one
listing, and a crash between the two statements makes that permanent — every
`WHERE valid_to IS NULL` join then double-counts, and `instrument_current` holds
both. Enrichment (sector, industry, cik10, cusip, permaTicker) IS written in
place, opens no version, and is refused by `assertEnrichIsSafe` if it ever
carries an identity field.

**ABSENCE FROM A SOURCE DOES NOT CLOSE A ROW.** A listing is closed only when a
source positively reports `DELISTED`. `listFiles` fails open to `[]` one layer
down and an empty read looks exactly like an empty market, so closing on absence
lets one bad fetch retire the universe behind a `valid_to` that asserts a
delisting which never happened. The other error — a stale ACTIVE row — is
corrected by the next good run.

**US HISTORY IS REAL; INDIA IS CURRENT-ONLY, AND THE ANSWER SAYS SO.**
`UsInstrument` carries start and end dates and holds DELISTED rows, so `DOW`
resolves to Dow Chemical for a 2017 as-of and Dow Inc. for a 2020 one.
`InstrumentMapping` is overwritten on every instrument-sync and **NSE publishes
no symbolchange/namechange family that this repo archives** —
`report-registry.ts` holds 26 families and none of them is one (verified
2026-09-22) — so India map rows are written with `valid_from` NULL and method
`SYMBOL_VENUE_CURRENT`, and `resolveListing` returns `dated: false`. A
fabricated `valid_from` would make a guess indistinguishable from knowledge.

**FIRST VERSIONS ARE SOURCE-DATED (MONEY-1544, identity/2).** Until this every
first version opened on the day the job ran (2026-09-22 on prod, all 98,249), so
a release `universe()` could answer nothing earlier. US: Tiingo's
`listingStartDate`; a delisted listing is ACTIVE through `listingEndDate` and
DELISTED from the day after. India: the listing's first bar in `price_bar_d1` —
truncated by the archive, NSE from 1994-11-03 and BSE from 2024-01-01 — else the
day it was first observed. A DELISTING on an existing listing is dated from the
source's end + 1 when that is strictly after the open version's `valid_from` and
not after the observed day; otherwise the observed day, with the fallback named
(`changeDate`). India also reads INACTIVE `InstrumentMapping` rows and bar ids no
master row carries (MONEY-1602; HDFC Ltd is in no master row at all): each
becomes a listing from its bars, or is counted by reason — `ARCHIVE_NOT_CURRENT`,
`NO_BARS`, `OVERLAPS_ACTIVE`, `OVERLAPS_SIBLING` — and nothing is admitted until
the venue's archive is CURRENT: 0 old-spelling ids AND its newest session within
10 days of today. "Still trading" means a last print within 365 days of the
venue's newest session; an open listing is never closed on a quiet night. `--rebase-history` re-dates the rows written
before this, touches `listing` only, and a second run plans nothing. **Order:**
`lake-apply-ddl.ts --apply` (the 005 view) → `--rebase-history --apply` → the
nightly `--apply`. That rebases US NOW; India is held back per venue until the
run logs `0 bar ids still on the old spelling — CURRENT`, then rebase again and
run the nightly. Until MONEY-1614 the rebase never received those
verdicts and held back EVERY India listing even on a CURRENT archive (prod,
2026-09-23: both venues CURRENT, `India held back 23939`); the heads are now a
required `rebase()` argument, so dropping them is a type error. After each rebase this must return no rows (the job also checks
it and throws):

```sql
SELECT listing_id FROM `stock-book-483718.lake_silver_prod.listing`
GROUP BY listing_id HAVING COUNTIF(valid_to IS NULL) != 1
```

**`listing_id` comes from `buildListingId`** (`tiingo-logic.ts`), the same
function the candle writer uses. Built two ways and disagreeing by one
character, it splits one security's history in two — visible only as a chart
that goes blank before a date.

**`UsFiler` is NOT queried**, though the spec names it: `UsInstrument.cik`
already holds the CIK, so reading it again would add a second spelling of one
fact and nothing else.

### `jobs/lake-replay-in.ts` — India exchange archive -> `lake_silver` (MONEY-1497, B2-IN)

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-replay-in.ts --env qa --from 2024-01-01 --to 2024-01-31
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-replay-in.ts --env qa --from 2024-01-01 --to 2024-01-31 --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-replay-in.ts --env prod --from 1994-11-03 --to 2026-09-22 \
    --tables=price_bar_d1 --apply
```

Fills four silver tables from bytes already in `feeds/`: `price_bar_d1`
(`market='IN'`), `derivatives_eod_in`, `delivery_daily` and `index_eod`. DDL is
`packages/lake/ddl/006_silver_derivatives_in.sql` (the first two of those tables
come from `002_silver_price_bars.sql`; 006 also `ALTER`s `isin` onto
`price_bar_d1`). **DRY RUN by default** — `--apply` is what writes an object, a
row or a ledger entry. No exchange traffic, no Mongo write: `DailyPrice` stays
the hot/live table and `mayWritePriceSource` still governs it.

**A CLOUD RUN JOB, and NOT a flag on `feed-parse`.** The brief asked for the
sink inside that job; two measured properties of it make a replay there load
almost nothing while reporting a clean run. (1) `feed-parse` is LISTING-DRIVEN
(`listFiles("feeds/{exchange}/{feed}/")`), and `listFiles` fails open to `[]`.
This job COMPUTES every key from the registry's own `buildUrl` +
`buildFeedArchiveKey` and fetches it, so a 404 is a real absence. (2)
`feed-parse` filters its work by `FeedBackfillProgress`, which already records
nearly all of history as `DONE` — so a sink inside it would never SEE the
history it was added to replay, and bypassing that filter for one sink would
give the replay and the nightly two different paths through one job.

**IDEMPOTENCY IS THE B1 LEDGER, not `FeedBackfillProgress`.** That table holds
ONE status per (family, date), so a Mongo-success / BigQuery-failure would stamp
`DONE` and resume would skip it for ever. The claim here is
(dataset, table, `IN/YYYY-MM`, source_hash, parser_version).

**THE UNIT IS A MONTH, AND THE BRIEF'S OWN KEY COULD NOT WORK.** It specified
`(table, month, source_hash = object generation)` — but a month holds ~21
day-files with ~21 generations, so that claims one month 21 times under 21
hashes, `acceptPartition` supersedes the other 20, and `decideClaim` re-LOADS a
SUPERSEDED row. The hash is therefore taken over EVERY contributing day-file
(sorted; generations, not names, so MONEY-1169's in-place repairs are visible).

**IT DELETES THE MONTH BEFORE IT LOADS IT.** `bq load` appends, and two things
make a second append real: a crash between the load and the accept, and a
repaired archive object changing the month's hash. Delete-then-load leaves a
GAP on a crash (which the un-ACCEPTED claim makes visible); the other order
leaves a MIXTURE, which nothing can detect.

**COVERAGE IS "did every session we read produce rows", NOT the trading
calendar, and NOT files.** `NSE_HOLIDAYS_BY_FY` covers recent financial years
only and this walk starts in 1994, so counting weekdays would make every month
containing a holiday read as partial. `expectedSessions` is the number of
distinct DATES a file was read and parsed for; `actualSessions` is distinct
session dates in the mapped rows — which catches the MONEY-1169 shape exactly
(thousands of files parsing to nothing, reported as success). `decideMonth`
takes `ReadonlySet<string>` on both sides rather than two counts, because
counting FILES was the first cut and it was wrong in the worst direction: from
2024 onward two families publish every session of three of the four tables, so a
healthy 21-session month reads 42 files, `21 >= 42` is false, and
`acceptPartition` would have thrown on every month the job exists to load. A
gate that refuses health is worse than no gate. What counting dates masks — one
family going dark while its sibling covers the same sessions — is REPORTED as
`zeroRows` in the run log rather than gated, because it must not block loading
what the healthy family did produce. The calendar question is a third question
and belongs to the coverage REPORT.

**A ROW WHOSE OWN DATE LEAVES THE MONTH IS QUARANTINED `WRONG_MONTH`.** The
archive key says which day a file is; the parser reads the day off the file's
OWN column, and the two can disagree — `parseSecBhavdataFull` counts exactly
that as `dateMismatch`, and `dateFromArchiveKey` recovers a date from five
filename grammars including a two-digit year. A stray row is not merely
misfiled: `replaceMonth` deletes ONE month, so a row in an adjacent month sits
outside every later reload's predicate and can only accumulate.

**THE DELETE IS SCOPED BY `source`, NOT `parser_version`.** `parser_version` is
in the ledger key precisely so a parser FIX re-runs over unchanged bytes — so
the rows a reload must remove are, by definition, the ones carrying the PREVIOUS
version. A predicate naming the NEW one removes nothing and the month ends up
holding both parses with nothing on either row to say which is current.
`sourceFor(table)` single-sources the value for both the write and the delete.

**`price_bar_d1` and `delivery_daily` are at v2 (`in-bhavcopy-v2` /
`in-delivery-v2`, MONEY-1522)**: their `listing_id` is now
`buildListingId(sym, exch, null)` = `{symbol}:{exchange}:unknown`, core's one
builder, handed to the mappers rather than re-spelled. `derivatives_eod_in` and
`index_eod` carry no `listing_id` and stay v1. A full-window re-run replaces the
v1 rows, because the delete is scoped by source, not version — which only holds
with MONEY-1538 deployed: until then the month DELETE bound its dates as NULL,
removed nothing, and every re-load appended.

**A REFUSED MONTH NO LONGER ENDS THE RUN (MONEY-1612).** It is marked REJECTED in
`partition_load` (with expected/actual sessions), a `COVERAGE_REFUSED` quarantine
row names the missing session dates, the log prints `REFUSED <table> <month>
partial: a/b sessions present — missing: <dates>`, and the walk continues; the
job exits 1 at the end listing every refused month (also in
`pipeline_run.notes`). Loaded rows for a refused month stay in silver; the next
run reloads it. Any other error still aborts immediately. A dry run prints
`would REFUSE … missing: …` per month. `lake-replay-us` follows the same rule.

**A STALE DELIVERY COPY ON A PROVEN HOLIDAY IS NOT A SESSION (MONEY-1612).** On NSE
holidays the archive holds a STALE COPY of the previous session's
`sec_bhavdata_full`. A zero-row file is a stale copy only when (a) every DATE1
it holds is a weekday EARLIER than its key, possibly last month, AND (b) it is
byte-identical — md5, or crc32c when md5 is absent — to the file archived under
that date's own key (a memoised `getFileMetadata`). A stale copy is a holiday —
not expected — only when the NSE cash bhavcopy for that date is also absent from
the archive; the absent bhavcopy keys are hashed into the month (generation
`"absent"`), so archiving one later re-judges the month (and flipping a price
family's `backfillable` flag, or adding an NSE cash price family, reloads every
delivery holiday month once). Any other zero-row file holding another date
stays expected and refuses, named in the log: `GAP 2019-10-28 (file holds
weekend session 2019-10-27 — not walked)`, `GAP 2021-11-05 (file holds session
2021-11-04 — not held under its own key)`. Month lines show `stale=N foreign=N
expected=N`, then `holiday …` / `GAP …` lines. Known refusals until MONEY-1613
loads weekend and Muhurat sessions: 2019-10, 2020-11, 2021-11, 2024-01, 2024-05.

**SHARD A LONG REPLAY BY YEAR WITH `--from`/`--to`.** There is no `--tasks`
fan-out: the window IS the shard, so a Cloud Run Jobs execution with
`--tasks=30` passes each task its own year and every task claims its own months
in the ledger. Overlapping windows are safe (an ACCEPTED month is skipped); the
only thing that is not is two tasks claiming the SAME month, which the six-hour
LOADING lease in `decideClaim` already handles.

**TWO TABLES THE BRIEF NAMED ARE NOT WRITTEN, and neither is a gap this papers
over.** `nse-mto-delivery` is archived and has **no parser anywhere in this
repo** (it is not in `feed-parse`'s `PARSERS`), so `delivery_daily` is fed from
`nse-sec-bhavdata-full` alone and delivery history floors at 2019-10-01 rather
than 2002; the table is nonetheless built for MTO's shape (`series` NULLABLE,
because an MTO row carries no SERIES column). And there is **no CF-CA family** —
India corporate actions never touch the feed archive, they come from live NSE
and BSE APIs through `jobs/corp-actions-backfill.ts`, so a ledgered
`corporate_action` sink belongs to that job.

### `jobs/lake-replay-us.ts` — the US warehouse -> `lake_silver` (MONEY-1513, B2-US)

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-replay-us.ts --env prod --from 2024-03-01 --to 2024-03-31
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-replay-us.ts --env prod --from 2024-03-01 --to 2024-03-31 --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-replay-us.ts --env prod --from 1962-01-01 --to 2026-09-30 \
    --tables=price_bar_d1 --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-replay-us.ts --env prod --from 2015-03-01 --to 2015-03-31 \
    --symbols=AAPL,DOW        # a cheap, real dry run
```

**MONEY-1616 — recycled tickers.** Tiingo dates a SECURITY's history, not its
tenure on a ticker, so 437 master pairs overlap (Barrick's `B` from 1985 vs
Barnes to 2025) and 196,569 rows were quarantined AMBIGUOUS. A row is now
admitted as `LATEST_OCCUPANT` when its stored id is a covering candidate AND the
symbol's unique latest-ending window, unless the symbol is STITCHED (the
occupant's id before its own start, or a >28% adjacent-session price seam at
another window's boundary). Such symbols are read over their WHOLE history
whatever `--from/--to` says; out-of-window rows are skipped as before. The run
logs `LATEST_OCCUPANT rows=N; stitched (withheld) K: …`. Measured 2026-09-24:
174,785 admitted over 276 symbols; 21,702 stay AMBIGUOUS. `price-bar-v2` forces
the reload of every US month (India stamps its own `in-bhavcopy-v1`).

Fills `price_bar_d1` (`market='US'`) from the two tiers of the candle warehouse —
Mongo's D1 hot window plus the gzipped-JSONL cold shards under
`candles/US/D1/{symbol}/{year}` — and `fund_nav_d1` from `us_fund_nav`. DDL is
`packages/lake/ddl/002_silver_price_bars.sql`. **DRY RUN by default.** No vendor
traffic and no Mongo write: Tiingo is a paid metered API and these bytes are
already bought.

**B2 (MONEY-1496) SHIPPED THE MAPPING AND NOT THE JOB.** Measured on prod
2026-09-22, `price_bar_d1` held ONE group — `market=IN`, `source=BHAVCOPY_HIST`,
13,185,397 rows — and no US rows at all; `priceBarSourceHash` and
`priceBarPartitionKey` had zero non-test callers; `fund_nav_d1` appeared in no
source file outside a test asserting it is not an India table. The pure layer was
real and correct and nothing drove it.

**TWO WALKS, BECAUSE THE TWO SOURCES ARE SHAPED DIFFERENTLY.** `price_bar_d1` is
SYMBOL-MAJOR: the cold tier is keyed by symbol and YEAR, so a month-major walk
would re-read every year shard twelve times — ~5.5M GETs against ~464k over the
231,861 symbol-years this universe spans. It also makes the hot/cold
de-duplication trivially correct, because `dedupeBySession` then runs over ONE
listing's bars instead of a global key set the size of a year. `fund_nav_d1` is
MONTH-MAJOR: `us_fund_nav` has no cold tier and a month is ~42,500 rows.

**NOT `CandleRepository.getCandles`, for two reasons that are properties of this
job.** (1) It cannot return a shard's GENERATION, and `priceBarSourceHash` is
specified over generations rather than names precisely so a shard rewritten in
place produces a new month hash instead of being invisible to the ledger for
ever. (2) Its `writeBack` defaults TRUE and promotes every cold hit to
`tier: "LOADED"` — `CandleQuery`'s own doc names this case ("a one-shot
deep-history export whose 46 years would otherwise be pulled into a hot window
sized for 10"). A replay must not change what the store holds.

**THE COLD KEYS ARE COMPUTED, NEVER LISTED**, and `UsInstrument.coldYearsFrom/To`
is deliberately not used to narrow the plan. It would cut the walk from 231,861
symbol-years to 130,494 — and it is a STAMP written by the backfill that only
ever widens, so a shard `candle-evict` wrote after the stamp sits outside it. A
narrowing that can be stale drops a year of history silently.

**THE STORED `listing_id` IS WHAT GETS WRITTEN; `resolveListingForDate` IS THE
GATE.** A ticker is not a security (2,196 US tickers repeat; `DOW:NYSE` is Dow
Chemical to 2017 and Dow Inc. from 2019), and a cold shard is keyed by SYMBOL, so
it can hold bars for two occupants across its lifetime. The bar's own id is what
`Candle`'s index, the shards and `identity-sync`'s `listing` table already use,
so overwriting it with the resolver's answer would make the lake the only store
holding a third value; taking it on trust is the other failure. So a disagreement
is QUARANTINED. Measured on prod over March 2015 (7,731 symbols walked, 7,323
shards read, 408 absent, 160,140 bars in window): **132 `LISTING_MISMATCH` across
6 symbols** — `VR` carries `VR:NASDAQ:2022-04-28` on a 2015 bar, a listing that
did not exist for another seven years — and **835 `LISTING_AMBIGUOUS` across 19**
— `ESCR` has overlapping BATS and NASDAQ windows with different start dates. 0.60%
refused, nothing unresolved. Resolution happens at 04:00 ET of the session, not at
the bar's own timestamp: a D1 `ts` is UTC MIDNIGHT of the session, which is
earlier than the ET session open, so resolving at the raw value would put every
listing's FIRST session before its own window.

**BOTH TIERS ARE REQUIRED EVEN INSIDE THE HOT WINDOW**, and March 2024 is the
proof rather than the argument: a full-universe dry run reached 208,249 rows
against **184,904** that Mongo holds for the same window — 23,345 sessions exist
ONLY in a cold shard, because `candle-evict` has already archived part of 2024.
1,237 shards read, 9,675 absent, 0 unreadable; 830 rows refused (0.40%); 20
sessions seen and 20 written.

**ONE SYMBOL MUST NOT ABANDON THE OTHER TEN THOUSAND.** `mapWithConcurrency`
does not catch — its own doc says a caller needing per-item isolation must catch
inside its task — and the first full prod dry run died on `BC/PB`, a symbol
`buildCandleColdKey` correctly refuses because a `/` reshapes the key hierarchy
invisibly. Two outcomes now, and they are deliberately not the same one. A symbol
with NO COLD KEY is a named FLOOR: it has no cold tier under our scheme, its hot
leg still runs, and prod holds exactly three (`BC/PA`, `BC/PB`, `BC/PC`, all
DELISTED NYSE stocks with zero D1 candles between them). Any OTHER throw is
counted, named, and makes the walk THROW at the end — because a symbol missing
from the walk is missing from `sessionsSeen` too, so the coverage gate cannot see
the gap and would ACCEPT the month over it.

**THE WINDOW IS SNAPPED TO WHOLE MONTHS, and the run header says so.**
`replaceMonth` deletes a MONTH whatever was asked for, so `--from 2024-03-15
--to 2024-03-31 --apply` would delete the first fortnight of March and reload only
the second — a hole in a month the ledger reports ACCEPTED.

**ONE DELETE-AND-LOAD PER `source`.** `price_bar_d1.source` is NOT NULL and a
cluster key because it is what disambiguates a volume of 0 (structural, from a
quote-derived feed, versus nothing traded), so each bar carries its OWN source
rather than one stamped by the job. `replaceMonth` scopes its DELETE by source, so
a month holding two would have the second load delete the first's rows; the stage
object's name carries the source and the `bq load` wildcard is per-source.
`TIINGO` is a PREFIX of `TIINGO_RT`, so the dash separator is load-bearing.
Measured on prod: every D1 candle and every `UsFundNav` row is `TIINGO` today, so
the multi-source path is unexercised — it is built because the alternative,
assuming one source, is wrong in a way no row count reveals.

**COVERAGE IS THE SAME RULE AS INDIA'S, re-worded.** `decideUsMonth` delegates the
arithmetic to `decideMonth` — a rule stated twice can disagree with itself — and
replaces only the skip REASON, because "before the family's floor" sends a US
operator hunting a registry with no US entry. `expectedSessions` is the distinct
session dates OBSERVED in the source and `actualSessions` the dates present in the
rows that survived, for the same reason: `US_MARKET_HOLIDAYS` covers 2023-2028
only (`US_HOLIDAY_TABLE_YEARS`) and this walk reaches 1962, so counting weekdays
would make every month with a holiday read partial.

**`fund_nav_d1` HAS NO `market` COLUMN** — it keys on `perma_ticker`, which is not
derivable from the master — so `replaceMonth`'s `market` is `string | null` and
`usDeleteScope` decides per table. `null` is spelled explicitly rather than
optional: an omitted field would let a forgotten argument widen the delete across
both markets of the shared `price_bar_d1`.

**THE REACHABLE NAV UNIVERSE IS THE ACTIVE PARTITION, and that is a documented
FLOOR.** `UsFundNav` keys on `(permaTicker, date)` and the permaTicker enricher is
ACTIVE-ONLY by design, so the 22,739 delisted funds can never receive a NAV row —
from this job, the nightly, or the backfill. Measured on prod 2026-09-22:
`us_fund_nav` holds 5,176,672 rows over 27,134 permaTickers against 27,121 active
funds, and a March-2024 dry run reads 42,568 rows over 20 sessions with nothing
quarantined. Do not read a low ratio against the whole 49,860-fund master as a
defect.

**SHARD A LONG REPLAY BY YEAR WITH `--from`/`--to`**, exactly as `lake-replay-in`
does: the window IS the shard, overlapping windows are safe because an ACCEPTED
month is skipped, and two tasks claiming one month is what the six-hour LOADING
lease in `decideClaim` is for. `--symbols` narrows the CANDLE walk only and is
refused with a warning for `fund_nav_d1`, whose identifier space is different.

**A failing walk says so, and stops (MONEY-1608 review).** Every symbol failure
is logged as it happens, reads retry only TRANSIENT errors, and 50 consecutive
symbol failures (`MAX_CONSECUTIVE_SYMBOL_FAILURES`) abort the walk with
`WALK ABORTED` — a systemic fault, not bad luck. Stage writes are create-only;
see "Every lake job cannot end silently" above.

### `jobs/lake-parity-in.ts` — the switchover gate (MONEY-1497, B2-IN)

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-parity-in.ts --env qa
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-parity-in.ts --env prod --listings 30 --dates 10
```

READ-ONLY. Compares `lake_silver.price_bar_d1` (`market='IN'`) against Mongo
`DailyPrice`, sampling FROM THE LAKE so a listing the replay never wrote is
visible rather than silently skipped. **The comparison is EXACT and there is no
tolerance to tune** — both sides are the same archived bytes through the same
core parser, so a disagreement is a mapping bug rather than rounding. Exits 1 on
one.

Three buckets that are reported and are NOT failures: a session Mongo sourced
from `YAHOO` (a gap fill — the lake holds exchange truth or a gap, never Yahoo);
a row with no `InstrumentMapping` (the lake keys on
`{symbol}:{exchange}:unknown` and keeps rows `DailyPrice`'s ObjectId key drops);
and a session the lake has ahead of `DailyPrice`. Zero rows compared is a
FAILURE, not a clean run.

**`--env` pins BOTH sides (MONEY-1523).** It used to import the
`@usemoney/database` singleton, which captures the ambient `DATABASE_URL` at
import — the repo `.env` is QA — so `--env prod` compared the prod lake against
QA's `DailyPrice` and reported every difference between the two ENVIRONMENTS as
a mapping bug. It now resolves the URI through `pinJobEnv` and builds its own
client, the MONEY-1514 fix.

#### `jobs/ledger-backfill-databento.ts`

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/ledger-backfill-databento.ts --env prod                  # dry run
npx tsx scripts/jobs/ledger-backfill-databento.ts --env prod --apply --limit=500
npx tsx scripts/jobs/ledger-backfill-databento.ts --env prod --apply --no-head
```

Walks `DatabentoBatchJob.files` — a Mongo `Json` array, unqueryable, which is
what B1 exists to fix — and writes the ledger rows it implies. HEADs each object
for its `generation`, because `recordSourceObject` MERGEs on
`(object_uri, generation)` and with generation always null that degenerates to
`object_uri` alone; the first row that ever DOES carry one then becomes a second
ledger row for the same object, with a `state` that can disagree with the first.
`--no-head` skips that for a fast re-run.

**It selects on `{ ledgerBacked: { not: true } }`, never `{ equals: false }`.** A
Mongo `db push` creates indexes and not fields, so every pre-existing row has the
attribute ABSENT — a different value from `false`. `{ equals: false }` matches
NEITHER and would select zero rows while reporting success. That class has cost
this repo three tickets (MONEY-1050, 1234, 1235).

It stamps `ledgerBacked` on a file-less row ONLY if the row is TERMINAL. A
QUOTED / SUBMITTED / PROCESSING row legitimately has no files yet, and stamping
it would exclude that purchase's whole inventory from every future run, for ever,
while this one reports `empty=1` as a healthy outcome.

#### `jobs/lake-load-databento.ts` — Databento bars into the lake (MONEY-1505, B5)

```bash
# dry run: enumerate files, plan month partitions, write nothing
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-load-databento.ts --env prod --schema ohlcv-1m --year 2024

npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-load-databento.ts --env prod --schema ohlcv-1h --year 2024 --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-load-databento.ts --env prod --schema ohlcv-1m --year 2024 --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-load-databento.ts --env prod --schema spread   --year 2024 --apply

# one month only — the shape a nightly top-up uses after databento-append lands
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-load-databento.ts --env prod --schema ohlcv-1m \
  --year 2026 --month 2026-09 --apply

# or as the Cloud Run Job (setup-databento-jobs.sh --only=lake-load)
gcloud run jobs execute lake-load-databento-prod --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=--env,prod,--schema,ohlcv-1m,--year,2024,--apply
```

Loads the Databento bars ALREADY IN GCS into `lake_silver.trade_bar_m1` /
`trade_bar_h1`, and folds `bbo-1m` + `tbbo` into `spread_cost_daily`. **It spends
nothing** — there is no Databento client on any path out of it.

**`--year` is the DATA year (MONEY-1515)** — the ET session year of the rows.
It used to be the ARCHIVE PATH year, which is the harvest job's START year: the
whole H1 and M1 harvests sit under `…/2023/`, so 32 of the 43 bought months
(2023-03..2026-09) were reachable by no `--year`. Every run now lists the whole
inventory and plans months from the vendor range in each filename; months
outside the purchase are skipped BY NAME (`outside-purchase`), and an empty plan
refuses rather than exiting 0. Walk every year, **H1 before M1** (same path, a
quarter of the rows), then `spread` — expect H1 ≈ 38.8M and M1 ≈ 151.7M rows.
**Run it as the Cloud Run job, in-region** (~2–2.5 h for the lot), not from a
laptop. **It depends on MONEY-1538:** before that fix the purge bound its date
range as NULL and deleted nothing, so every re-load appended a second copy.

**`--schema ohlcv-1s` is REFUSED**, twice: once at the front door and once at the
table lookup. It is archive-only by founder decision, and an `undefined` table
renders as `lake_silver_prod.undefined`, which BigQuery answers "not found" and
an operator reads as a missing DDL migration.

**One claim per (timeframe, MONTH).** Per FILE would be 44,097 load jobs against
BigQuery's 1,500 per table per day; per YEAR would restart 43M rows after a crash
and re-load twelve months to store one appended session.

**Purge, then load, then accept — in that order.** A load job is atomic but the
pair (load, accept) is not, so every load is preceded by a delete of the range it
is about to write. That makes a re-run idempotent wherever the last one died, and
it removes a previous `parser_version`'s rows, which the ledger deliberately lets
back in.

**Run `lake-apply-ddl.ts` first.** `createDisposition: CREATE_NEVER` means a
missing table fails the load rather than inventing a schema.

#### `jobs/ledger-backfill-tiingo.ts`

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/ledger-backfill-tiingo.ts --env prod                     # dry run
npx tsx scripts/jobs/ledger-backfill-tiingo.ts --env prod --apply

# or as the Cloud Run Job (setup-tiingo-backfill-job.sh --only=ledger)
gcloud run jobs execute ledger-backfill-tiingo-prod --region=asia-south1 \
  --project=stock-book-483718 --account=founder@disdigital.in \
  --args=--env,prod,--apply
```

**IT LISTS THE ARCHIVE. That is the one thing to understand about it**, and it
is the opposite of what the sibling above does.

The first cut computed keys from `BackfillCheckpoint`, because B1 said "never by
listing". Its first prod dry run (2026-09-17) measured what that produced:

```
19,308 checkpoints -> 93 candidates -> 22 PRESENT, 71 MISSING
     28 objects actually in gs://stockbook-content-prod/feeds/TIINGO/
```

Wrong in both directions from one cause. **63 of the 71 MISSING were ex-dates in
the FUTURE** (to 2026-12-31) — and `MISSING` means "the archive PUT did not
land", in a table CLUSTERED ON `state`, so a coverage query would have read 71
losses that never happened. **6 REAL objects were never candidates** (splits for
2026-09-04, -07, -11, -15, -16, -17).

Because every one of those 19,308 checkpoints was written on **2026-08-29** by
one historical walk over ex-dates 1990–2026, while every archived object was
written **2026-08-31 onward** by the NIGHTLY corp-actions leg, which writes no
checkpoints at all. For those six dates the checkpoint honestly said "< 32
bytes, nothing there" — true on 2026-08-29, when the date was still in the
future — and the object arrived days later from a different producer. Two
causally unrelated sources, so no bound derived from one can describe the other.
`--archiving-since` bounded the damage and is now **gone**.

**"Never list" was a rule about `listFiles` FAILING OPEN to `[]`.** A ledger
that records an empty inventory on a credentials blip is worse than one with no
row, because a coverage query reads it as an answer. That is a property of that
function, not of listing — so this job uses **`streamObjectSummaries`**, which
already existed for exactly this reason, **THROWS**, and **PAGES** rather than
buffering a whole prefix into one array. Its listing carries generation, size,
contentType and creation time, replacing one HEAD per object.

An **empty feed is a REFUSAL**, and it is checked **per feed, not over the
union**: distributions going dark while splits still returns objects gives a
non-empty union, a SUCCESS run, and a ledger silently covering half the source.
That is MONEY-1255's per-source zero hidden inside an aggregate counter, one
level up.

**A run that rejected objects is FAILED, not SUCCESS.** Every reject reason
except `below-min-bytes` is a key this reader could not parse inside a prefix we
pinned — a grammar drift that would otherwise silently drop each night's new
objects while the all-or-nothing refusal stayed quiet. `endRun` is wrapped so it
fires even when the BigQuery write throws; without that, `finished_at` stays
NULL for ever, which the DDL defines as "STILL RUNNING or killed", and `runId`
mints a new id per attempt so nothing reconciles the orphan.

**`MISSING` IS NOT REACHABLE HERE, deliberately.** An absent object is either
"no corporate actions that ex-date, so the shape guard correctly stored nothing"
or "the PUT failed", and nothing distinguishes them. The Databento leg may write
MISSING because a vendor MANIFEST proves delivery; this one has no manifest, so
`PRESENT` is the strongest honest statement. `verifiedAt` stays null — these
feeds carry no vendor checksum.

`acquired_at` is the object's **creation time** — ARRIVAL, matching the only
other writer into that column (`toLedgerSourceObjects` computes
`downloadedAt ?? submittedAt ?? now`). One column in one
`PARTITION BY DATE(acquired_at)` table must not carry two different facts keyed
by `source`. The ex-date would be wrong twice over: the nightly leg fetches the
NEXT trading day, so the stamp would sit in the FUTURE of the PUT on every row,
and across 1990–2026 it would occupy ~9,600 of BigQuery's 10,000 partitions per
table for this leg alone.

**Both are PROD-ONLY and refuse `--env qa` without `--allow-qa`.** Databento is
a paid metered vendor whose rows QA receives by copy; `FEED_ARCHIVE_MODE` is off
outside prod, so prod is the only environment that ARCHIVES them. QA is NOT
empty, though — `setup-sync-job.sh` rsyncs all of `feeds/` prod→QA excluding
only `TIINGO/ws/` — so a QA run would ledger prod's objects under a QA bucket
name. That is what `--allow-qa` guards, and an earlier note claiming "a QA
listing is legitimately empty" was simply wrong.

**IAM:** both write BigQuery as `stockbook-api@` (the attached SA; the lake
client uses plain ADC). That principal holds `roles/bigquery.jobUser` at project
level plus dataset WRITER on **`lake_bronze_prod` only** — not on QA, and not on
silver/gold/private. Granted 2026-09-17 after the first provisioning found it
had no BigQuery role at all, which a DRY RUN cannot detect: every BigQuery call
sits behind `if (args.apply)`.

### The two event/registry lake jobs (MONEY-1507, data-platform C1)

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/event-lake-sink.ts       --env qa   # dry run
npx tsx scripts/jobs/event-lake-sink.ts       --env prod --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/event-lake-sink.ts       --env prod --reload           # census + plan
npx tsx scripts/jobs/event-lake-sink.ts       --env prod --reload --apply   # after a parser-version bump
npx tsx scripts/jobs/filing-registry-sync.ts  --env prod --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/filing-registry-sync.ts  --env prod --count   # the B6 gate
```

Both run as **`lake-writer-{env}@`**, never as `stockbook-api@` —
`docs/reference/lake-layout.md` §10 grants that account **nothing on BigQuery**,
which is also why neither of these is a Pub/Sub worker route. `LAKE_ENV` is set
from `--env` inside the scripts; a Cloud Run JOB does not inherit a service's
env block.

**`event-lake-sink`** drains the Mongo serving copy (`instrument_events`) into
`lake_silver.instrument_event` by WATERMARK, and the watermark is **OUR** clock:
`MAX(ingested_at)` in silver, minus a **2 h overlap**, selecting Mongo rows on
`updatedAt`. Not `observed_at` — that is the PRODUCER's clock, create-only since
MONEY-1531, because the 8-K insight sweep adds a summary hours later and
deliberately leaves it alone (we saw the filing when we saw it). A window anchored on it would
never re-read an enriched row, and silver would carry `summary: null` for ever
while Mongo held the sentence, with both tables looking entirely healthy.

The overlap covers the gap between two clocks on two machines: a row can be
written between this run reading its window and the same run stamping
`ingested_at`, and a strict `>` would drop it permanently and leave a gap no
count can see (`tiingo-news-poll`'s 30-minute `crawlDate` overlap is the same
argument). The run SKIPS the `(event_id, observed_at, enriched_at)` TRIPLES
silver already holds, so the re-read writes nothing. Three parts, because an
enrichment appends a second row with the same `event_id` **and** the same
`observed_at`; readers take the latest `(observed_at, ingested_at)`.

**It is the ONLY writer of `expiresAt` (MONEY-1521).** The serving copy's TTL
index skips a row with no `expiresAt`, so the stamp is the permission to delete:
a row is stamped only after the INSERT carrying it returns (or when silver
already holds its current key), by a RAW compare-and-set that never moves
`updatedAt`. Every UNSTAMPED row is re-read on every run however old, so a
killed run or a failed stamp heals itself. A row whose `observedAt` sits more
than 60 s after its own `ingestedAt` is REFUSED by name — the MONEY-1531 moved
clock — never written, never stamped. **`--reload`** rebuilds silver from the
whole serving copy after a parser-version bump: INSERT first, then a SCOPED
DELETE (`parser_version != current AND event_id IN` the current version's ids),
so a crash leaves a duplicate rather than a gap and an event the 90-day serving
copy no longer holds is never deleted from the lake. Run it only after
`data/repair-instrument-event-clocks.ts` has cleaned the serving copy.

**`filing-registry-sync`** MERGEs `us_filings` into
`lake_silver.filing_registry` on `accession`, which makes it idempotent and is
why it needs no checkpoint — a killed task is resumed by re-running. **Since
MONEY-1533 it is ATOMIC and costs one target scan per run:** rows go to
~100k-row NDJSON under `lake/stage/filing_registry/US/{runId}/`, one load job
into `filing_registry_stage_{runId}` (48 h expiry, dropped on success), then ONE
MERGE that is dry-run priced first, deduped on `stage_seq`. The old
per-2,000-row MERGE re-billed the whole target each statement — ~28.5 TiB for
prod's 18.77M rows; the first fill now bills ~4.7 GB and a `--since` top-up
~5.6 GB. `--count` must read MATCH afterwards. **It has NO
scheduler trigger**, the same posture as `databento-harvest`: a full walk is
millions of rows and a cron would repeat it nightly for no reason. `--since`
narrows on `ingestedAt`, **our** clock, not on `filedAt`: the detectors and the
nightly `submissions.zip` ledger both store filings whose FILING date is old, so
a `filedAt` window would silently miss every backfilled row.

`--count` prints both counts side by side and is **B6 lever 3's stated
precondition** ("registry row count == Mongo count"). Run it, read MATCH, and
only then delete from `us_filings`.

`event-lake-sink` is a Cloud Run job since MONEY-1518: tsup entry plus
`ops/setup-lake-jobs.sh`, which schedules it HOURLY (`10 * * * *` IST) with
`--apply` in its deployed args. `filing-registry-sync` is a Cloud Run job too
since MONEY-1609 — tsup entry plus `setup-lake-jobs.sh`, manual, no scheduler.

### `jobs/corp-action-lake-sink.ts` — corporate actions into silver (MONEY-1516a)

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/corp-action-lake-sink.ts --env prod           # dry run
npx tsx scripts/jobs/corp-action-lake-sink.ts --env prod --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/corp-action-lake-sink.ts --env prod --apply --reload   # after a parser-version bump
```

**Why it exists.** B4's `adjusted_bar_d1` computes its split and dividend
factors from the BAR's own `div_cash` / `split_factor` columns. Measured on prod
2026-09-22, **all 13,185,397 India bars carry NULL in both** — bhavcopy publishes
no corporate-action data — and `NULL > 0` is NULL rather than true, so every
`CASE` takes its `ELSE`: `split_factor_cum = 1`, `adj_close = close`, for every
row, reported as a success. `assert_latest_bar_is_unadjusted` cannot see it
(under CRSP back-adjustment the latest bar is unadjusted by construction).
Founder decision, same day: load `silver.corporate_action` and JOIN it in the
adjustment. This is the LOADER; the Dataform change is MONEY-1516 (b).

Same shape as `event-lake-sink` — a Mongo→silver drain by watermark, not an
archive replay, and not a Pub/Sub route for the IAM reason above. It builds its
own `PrismaClient` from `pinJobEnv(--env)` rather than importing the
`@usemoney/database` singleton (MONEY-1514): that singleton is constructed AT
IMPORT from the ambient `DATABASE_URL`, so `--env prod` from a shell carrying the
repo's QA `.env` would read QA Mongo into `lake_silver_prod` with every log line
saying `env=prod`.

**`listing_id` has ONE spelling since MONEY-1522: `buildListingId(sym, exch,
null)`, i.e. `{symbol}:{exchange}:unknown`** — the same builder `silver.listing`
and the v2 India bars use. Before it, the bars carried `{symbol}:{exchange}` and
the two id sets (16,898 vs 23,907 on prod) intersected in **nothing**, so a
loader emitting the other spelling wrote every row, reported success and joined
zero. The run still measures the overlap against the real bar ids and
**throws** when it is zero, so until the v2 bar replay has run it REFUSES —
correctly. An EMPTY bar set (or an empty candidate set) reports `NOT MEASURED`,
never `0%`: not-measured is not not-passed.

**Refused, named and counted:** `UNRESOLVABLE_LISTING` (a blank symbol or
exchange), `UNKNOWN_VENUE` (anything but NSE/BSE), `BAD_DATE` (no usable
`exDate` / `createdAt` / `updatedAt`). A row naming a security we hold no bars
for is **not** refused — India's `listing` is current-only (`InstrumentMapping`
where `isActive`), so a company delisted in 2010 has thirty years of bhavcopy
bars and no identity row, and refusing on identity membership would drop exactly
the splits whose charts have a cliff.

**Measured, prod dry run 2026-09-22:** 59,087 scanned, 59,087 mapped, **0
refused**, and **49,061 / 59,087 = 83.03%** name a `listing_id` `price_bar_d1`
holds. The 10,026 that do not are dominated by G-Secs (`815GS2026:NSE`) and BSE
`#`-suffixed rows — real actions for instruments nothing charts. Of the 4,105
qty-changing rows (`ratio != null`) **90.72%** resolve to a bar.

`ratio` and `cash` route through `numeric()` (MONEY-1512) and it is load-bearing
rather than tidy: `ratio` is a post:pre QUOTIENT and prod holds
`1.333333333333333`, fifteen decimals into a `NUMERIC(18, 6)` column, on a load
path that runs `maxBadRecords: 0` — that one row would fail the whole job.

**`observed_ratio` (MONEY-1528 B)** carries `details.observedRatio` — numbers
only, else NULL — through the same `numeric()`. `actionFactor` prefers the
OBSERVED gap over the snapped `ratio` for a capital adjustment, and silver held
only the snapped one, so gold divided a cliff by 10 where the chart divided by
9.93. On prod it is non-null on exactly the 156 DERIVED `CAPITAL_ADJUSTMENT`
rows. It is part of the dedupe key, for `ratio`'s reason.

**`--reload` appends FIRST and prunes LAST** (`appendThenPrune`, MONEY-1522
review). On a reload only CURRENT-version rows count as present
(`planCorpActionAppend`); then the DELETE removes an older row only where its
`source_record_key` already has a current-version row, and it runs even when
nothing was pending, so a reload re-run after a crash between the two steps
still prunes. A SECOND arm (MONEY-1522 code review) prunes older-version rows
whose Mongo row is GONE — the 1,772 MONEY-1530 re-anchor losers — by explicit
key, only when the run read the FULL table (count taken before the read, and the
read ≥ 0.9 × the older version's keys); otherwise it refuses by name
(`orphan-arm=refused`). Each arm is counted separately in the log. It used to be an unscoped `parser_version != @current` run first,
which a truncated Mongo read would have turned into an emptied table. **Order
for the v3 reload:** `lake-apply-ddl.ts --apply` (the `observed_ratio` column)
→ the v2 India bar replay (`lake-replay-in --tables=price_bar_d1`) → this job
`--apply --reload`.

**A THIRD arm runs on EVERY `--apply`, reload or not (MONEY-1607):** it deletes
CURRENT-version rows whose Mongo row is gone, so a retired or hand-deleted action
(`retire-derived-corporate-actions.ts`) stops adjusting gold without waiting for
a version bump. Same whole-read proof as the second arm — an empty read, a read
short of the count, or one under the 0.9 floor refuses by name. The keys are read
BEFORE Mongo is counted, the delete is by explicit key and current version only,
and it runs LAST; logged as `current-orphaned=`. Measured 2026-09-23 it has
nothing to do (0 of 0: silver is all v2); the v3 reload's OLDER-version orphan
arm owes 762. **A FOURTH arm, same runs, same proof:** a current-version row
whose Mongo row still EXISTS but now maps to another (market, `listing_id`,
action type, ex-date) partition is deleted by explicit `restatedKey`, never by
`_id` (the moved row shares it) — otherwise a retype like the subjects repair's
BONUS → OTHER would leave both rows adjusting gold. A same-partition correction
is kept (gold takes the later `observed_at`); an unmapped row is kept and
counted. Logged as `current-restated=`.

The table is **APPEND-ONLY** (its DDL: a correction is a new row with a later
`observed_at`, never an UPDATE, because the adjustment is computed at read time
from the full set). So the dedupe key carries the VALUES as well as the natural
