key — Mongo's own key is `(symbol, exchange, type, exDate)` and a revised ratio
arrives on the SAME row, so a four-part key would skip it and the correction
would never reach the lake. `observed_at` is deliberately NOT in the key: it
moves on every re-assert, including ones that changed nothing. **"Already
present" means the SAME Mongo row with the same values** (MONEY-1607 review):
`source_record_key` is in the present key, so a value-identical twin from
another `_id` is appended as its own row, never skipped and then orphaned by a
prune arm. Gold applies an event once regardless (`action_latest`); measured
2026-09-23, prod holds 0 twin groups.

`published_at` is `createdAt` (when the corporate-actions sync first recorded it
— the "what arrived today" question its partitioning is built for) and
`observed_at` is `updatedAt`. Neither is an announcement date; Mongo holds none.

A Cloud Run job since MONEY-1609 (tsup entry plus `setup-lake-jobs.sh`,
manual, no scheduler), mapped in `SETUP_SCRIPT_BY_ENTRYPOINT`.

### `jobs/embeddings-v2-backfill.ts` — the int8 embedding migration (MONEY-1499, B8)

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/embeddings-v2-backfill.ts --env=prod --verify
npx tsx scripts/jobs/embeddings-v2-backfill.ts --env=prod --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/embeddings-v2-backfill.ts --env=prod --census
npx tsx scripts/jobs/embeddings-v2-backfill.ts --env=prod --rename-v1 --apply
```

Reads `document_embeddings` in `_id` order from a checkpoint, quantizes each
768-dim float64 vector to int8 BinData, and inserts into
`document_embeddings_v2` — executing
`docs/reference/embeddings-decision-2026-09.md` §5. A JOB rather than a Pub/Sub
handler because 5.95M rows and ~67 GiB is hours, and past the 600 s ack deadline
Pub/Sub REDELIVERS: a second copy of the migration would run beside the first,
against the same cursor.

**It deletes nothing.** Not a row, not a collection, not an index. `--rename-v1`
RENAMES v1 to `document_embeddings_v1_retired`, which preserves everything.

`checkRenameReady` is SIX rungs, not three, and the last three exist because the
first three were satisfiable by states nobody intended (MONEY-1499 review):
`EMBEDDINGS_V2_RENAME_APPLY` armed; mode already `on`; a v1 count that can be
right; **v2 + REFUSALS accounting for every v1 row** (a refused row is one v1
holds and v2 never will, so without that term a complete migration is refused
for ever and then passes unremarked once live traffic grows v2 past v1); **zero
v1 rows past the backfill checkpoint** (in `on` mode v1 is FROZEN and v2 grows
on live traffic, so `v2 >= v1` goes trivially true — it proves nothing); and
**`--accept-refusals=N` matching the refusal count exactly**, so the rows that
will not exist after the rename cost a human a deliberate number.

**Run ONE MORE backfill pass after the cutover**, before arming the rename. A
row the DUAL phase wrote lands past the checkpoint even though it is already in
v2 — ObjectIds are time-ordered — so the checkpoint rung refuses until a final
pass over the now-frozen v1 reaches those rows, finds them present, and advances
the marker to the true end. The refusal names this, because "the gate refuses
the correct end-state" and "the gate found un-migrated rows" must not read
alike. The DROP after B0's
30-day window has no code in this repo. The rename is the one command that goes
through the MongoDB driver rather than Prisma — `renameCollection` may only be
run against the `admin` database and `$runCommandRaw` always targets the one in
the connection string.

**A row it cannot migrate goes to a MANIFEST**
(`document_embeddings_v2_refusals`) with a NAMED reason — `NON_FINITE`,
`ZERO_VECTOR`, `DIM_MISMATCH`, `MODEL_MISMATCH` — never a `continue`. And if
refusals pass 1% of a meaningful sample (10,000 rows, so the ratio is not judged
on noise) the run STOPS: B0 measured this corpus as 100% one model at 768 dims,
so bulk refusals mean the measurement missed something.

`--verify` prints v1/v2/refusal counts, the checkpoint, and **the leftover set
as a paste-able query** — B0 asks for the remainder to be QUERYABLE rather than
inferred, and "5,912,004 of 5,951,933" says nothing about WHICH rows are
missing. `--census` is B0 §6 criterion 5, the duplication and chunks-per-docId
scan that blew `embeddings-profile.ts`'s deliberate 120 s ceiling on the serving
cluster; it runs here, against the smaller v2, off the request path, and reports
three states so a `$group` that could not finish is never written down as "no
duplication".

Provisioned by `ops/setup-embeddings-v2-job.sh`; built by the
`embeddings-v2-backfill` entry in `workers/tsup.config.ts`.

## `data/` - Enrichment / fix utilities (ad-hoc)

This section used to document the three Tiingo backfills. They moved to
`jobs/` (MONEY-1263 / MONEY-1264) because they run for hours and are now Cloud
Run Jobs — see the sections above. `data/` still holds the ad-hoc enrichment and
probe utilities (`backfill-bse-results.ts`, `probe-exchange-archives.ts`,
`repair-stock-document-queue.ts`, …).

### The Databento manifest and its two local tools (MONEY-1373)

| File                                 | What it is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `data/databento-manifest.json`       | **The buy list, and the single source of truth for what a harvest execution may ask for.** Seven entries, each `{id, schema, symbolSets, start, end, load, splitSymbols, splitDuration, append, order, note}`, over `dataset: "EQUS.MINI"` with a `historyFloor` of `2023-03-28`. `order` is the sequence the runbook walks, and `load: false` on `ohlcv-1s-baseline` is the founder decision of 2026-09-06: ARCHIVE ONLY — TB-scale, and loading it is weeks of writes for a series no strategy reads yet, but the bytes are unpurchasable later at the included price, so it is downloaded LAST and kept. The `symbolSets` block (BASELINE / BENCHMARKS / TOP50) is DATA, not code, so widening a set never means touching a job.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `data/refresh-databento-manifest.ts` | Regenerates `symbolSets.BASELINE` / `TOP50` from SSGA's published SPY holdings workbook (one public XLSX; no DB, no spend), converting the vendor's dot form (`BRK.B`) to our master's dash form (`BRK-B`). **DRY RUN BY DEFAULT; `--apply` is a BARE flag** (MONEY-1252 — a wrapper that forwarded `--flag=value` had its `--apply` swallowed and a 54-minute "apply" run wrote nothing while exiting 0). It REFUSES to write a list outside **480..520** symbols, because the S&P 500 is ~503 after share classes and anything else means the workbook layout moved and the parse is reading the wrong column. Non-ticker rows are DROPPED **and reported**, never silently filtered — the As-of-03-Sep-2026 sheet carried two (`-` and `2602335D`), each of which would resolve to no listing and quarantine every bar it produced. The list is CHECKED IN rather than fetched at run time, for the reason every manifest here is: what we BUY should be a reviewable diff, not a list that changes under a job on the day State Street rebalances. |
| `data/databento-probe.ts`            | **Quotes the manifest and costs NOTHING.** `metadata.get_cost` and `metadata.get_dataset_range` are free on the Databento historical API and the probe never reaches `batch.submit_job`, so it is the cheapest possible check of the token, the symbology resolution, the manifest arithmetic and the budget ledger. Run it before arming anything. Prefer the wrapper — `run-market-data-job.sh dbprobe {env}` pulls `DATABASE_URL` from Secret Manager and passes the GCP env, instead of having either pasted in by hand.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

```bash
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh dbprobe prod          # quote every entry, spend nothing
node --import tsx scripts/data/refresh-databento-manifest.ts          # dry run: re-derive BASELINE / TOP50
node --import tsx scripts/data/refresh-databento-manifest.ts --apply  # WRITES the manifest
jq -r '.entries[] | "\(.order)  \(.id)"' scripts/data/databento-manifest.json
```

The harvest itself is NOT here — it is a Cloud Run job
(`jobs/databento-harvest.ts`), because one entry is hours to days of vendor wait
plus download. See the `jobs/` section above and
[`ops/setup-databento-jobs.sh`](ops/setup-databento-jobs.sh).

## `quant/` - Bulk research reads (PYTHON)

### `quant/read_candles.py` — bulk cold-shard reader for backtests (MONEY-1384 B0)

| File                     | What it is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `quant/read_candles.py`  | **`candles/{market}/{timeframe}/{symbol}/{year}` -> one parquet file.** READ-ONLY; it writes nothing but the `--out` file. Exists because `CandleRepository` is a SERVING-tier API — one GCS GET per symbol-year, a full gunzip plus line-by-line `JSON.parse`, and a cached path that writes every decoded shard into Redis TWICE with no size guard; 507 symbols x 4 years of M1 is ~2,028 GETs and ~72 GB of JSON through it. Reuses the SERVICE's codec (`services/us-fundamentals/app/sec/archive_logic.py`) rather than a second one, so the two cannot drift, and inherits its miss-vs-failure line: a 404 is a normal "never archived" and is COUNTED, while a transport error is counted separately and RAISES. `--adjusted` is a port of `computeAdjustedSeries` (CRSP, cumulative factors backward from the newest bar), grouped per `listingId` so a recycled ticker's actions never reach its predecessor. The `TIINGO_RT` exclusion covers **BOTH tiers** — RT bars genuinely reach cold shards (`candle-evict-logic.ts` `buildEvictWhere` archives WINDOW-tier intraday rows with NO source clause, and the RT socket subscribes up to 3,000 symbols against a ~507-symbol Databento manifest), so gating only the Mongo query left the default flag filling at quote-mid prices with a structural `volume: 0` while reporting 100% shard hits. `--include-rt` is still the only way in, and the refused rows are COUNTED (`excludedSource=`) rather than silently dropped. **`--to` is INCLUSIVE of the whole day** (a bare date covers to 23:59:59.999999Z); midnight dropped every intraday bar of the final session and read as "the vendor has not published yet". The summary also carries `dropped=` (lines the CODEC could not decode, from `ShardParse.dropped`) and `rejected=` (rows that decoded but are not bars) on both tiers, so a short-key rename cannot print a perfect health line over an empty frame. **The frame is per LISTING, not per symbol**: both occupants of a recycled ticker are returned under one `symbol`, `listingId` is in the merge dedup key, and a symbol resolving to more than one listing is counted (`multiListing=`) and warned about on stderr — group on `listingId`. Tests + mutation notes: `services/us-fundamentals/tests/test_read_candles.py`. |
| `quant/requirements.txt` | **MONEY-1518 (B7)** — the whole Python closure `lake.py` and `read_candles.py --release` need (`numpy`, `pandas`, `pyarrow`, `python-dateutil`, `six`), PINNED to the versions `services/us-fundamentals/constraints.txt` ships; `test_lake.py` fails when the two disagree, because the suite that proves these readers runs in that venv. `duckdb` is deliberately ABSENT — only `open_release()` uses it, imported lazily. The cold-shard path still needs the us-fundamentals venv for its codec. `python3.12 -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install -r scripts/quant/requirements.txt`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

```bash
# Python, not tsx. Runs on the us-fundamentals venv. pandas/pyarrow are declared
# in that service's `[project.optional-dependencies].dev` (pymongo is already a
# runtime dependency) and pinned in its constraints.txt; they were reaching the
# venv only transitively through edgartools until MONEY-1384, which made the
# whole test file an ImportError away from that pin moving.
# RAW bars from the cold shards. `--adjusted` NO LONGER WORKS HERE — see below.
services/us-fundamentals/.venv/bin/python scripts/quant/read_candles.py \
  --market US --timeframe M1 --symbols AAPL,MSFT \
  --from 2023-03-28 --to 2026-09-06 --out out.parquet --workers 16

# The whole universe, plus the hot window (needs DATABASE_URL for --hot).
services/us-fundamentals/.venv/bin/python scripts/quant/read_candles.py \
  --market US --timeframe M1 --symbols-file universe.txt \
  --from 2023-03-28 --to 2026-09-06 --out us_m1.parquet --hot
```

**`--adjusted` needs `--release` now (MONEY-1518).** This file's hand port of
`computeAdjustedSeries` is DELETED, not bypassed — one policy had three
implementations and now has two, the TypeScript and the SQL that
`packages/core/__tests__/market-data/adjustment-sql-parity.test.ts` executes
against it. `--adjusted` without `--release` raises rather than returning raw
bars under adjusted column names, which is wrong by a multiple and renders
perfectly:

```bash
LAKE_ENV=prod services/us-fundamentals/.venv/bin/python scripts/quant/read_candles.py \
  --market US --timeframe M1 --symbols AAPL,MSFT \
  --from 2023-03-28 --to 2026-09-06 --adjusted \
  --release 2026-09-22T00-00-00Z --out out.parquet
```

**`--release` is a DROP-IN:** the parquet it writes has the cold path's
columns, dtypes, row rules and `--from`/`--to` semantics, byte-compared against
it on one fixture by `test_read_candles.py`. `--hot`, `--include-rt` and
`--workers` describe the cold walk and are REFUSED with `--release` by name.
The cold path stays until a release covers its callers — none can be cut yet —
and it remains the only reader of the Mongo hot window and of `TIINGO_RT` bars.

### `quant/lake.py` — the RELEASE reader (MONEY-1518, B7)

Reads `gs://stockbook-lake-{env}/releases/{release_id}/` — immutable, versioned
parquet a backtest PINS, so the same id re-read in six months answers the same
question. Needs `LAKE_ENV`. Install its closure with
`pip install -r scripts/quant/requirements.txt` (pandas + pyarrow, pinned to the
`services/us-fundamentals` versions; `test_lake.py` holds the two in agreement).

**`LAKE_RELEASE_CACHE`** reads the same layout from local disk instead of GCS,
with the bucket name kept in the path so a prod and a QA copy of one id cannot
answer for each other:

```bash
gcloud storage rsync -r gs://stockbook-lake-prod/releases/2026-09-22T00-00-00Z \
  "$LAKE_RELEASE_CACHE/stockbook-lake-prod/releases/2026-09-22T00-00-00Z"
```

Then run `verify_release("2026-09-22T00-00-00Z")` once — size AND sha256 of every file against
the manifest, returning `MISSING` / `SIZE` / `SHA256` / `UNLISTED` lines (empty
is the only pass). A sync killed part-way leaves a cache that reads perfectly
and holds a fraction of the universe.

```python
from lake import bars, universe, manifest, open_release, latest_release

manifest("2026-09-22T00-00-00Z")                       # policy, universe rule, row counts, licence
bars(["AAPL"], "D1", "2023-01-01", "2026-09-01", release="2026-09-22T00-00-00Z")
universe("2019-06-30", release="2026-09-22T00-00-00Z", market="US")  # from listing windows
open_release("2026-09-22T00-00-00Z")                    # a duckdb connection, IF duckdb is installed
```

Four things worth knowing before using it:

- **`universe()` refuses an as-of before a source's coverage floor** (MONEY-1544):
  each `market`/`source` in scope has an earliest dated window, and before it the
  source cannot say what traded. India's NSE and BSE archives start on different
  days (1994-11-03 and 2024-01-01 on prod), so an India universe before 2024 is
  refused unless narrowed to `venue="NSE"`. A release cut before the
  `identity-sync --rebase-history` run starts every window on 2026-09-22 and
  refuses every earlier as-of.
- **Every entry point REQUIRES `release`.** There is no default and no implicit
  newest: a backtest run against March's release and re-run in September
  produces two different P&Ls from identical inputs with nothing to say why.
  `latest_release()` exists and is a separate, explicit call.
- **`duckdb` is NOT a dependency of this repo and `lake.py` does not add one.**
  `pyarrow` (pinned 25.0.1) carries `GcsFileSystem`, hive partition pruning and
  predicate pushdown, so `bars()` / `universe()` need nothing new.
  `open_release()` imports duckdb lazily and names the install if absent —
  a consumer-side choice (~40 MB wheel, MIT) bought for SQL ergonomics.
- **The adjustment is read, never recomputed.** `bars_d1` carries
  `price_factor` and `split_factor_cum`, so an intraday adjusted read joins
  them on the session date. Volume divides by the SPLIT factor alone.

### `jobs/lake-gold.ts` — build gold (MONEY-1611)

`--env qa|prod` (must match the job suffix) · `--actions NAME` (one bare name per
flag, repeatable) · `--include-deps` / `--include-dependents` (need --actions;
the latter brings the model's assertions) · `--full-refresh`. Exits 0 only when
the CLI exits 0 AND its transcript shows every requested action and at least
one action ran, none failed or skipped — CLI 3.0.0 drops an unknown name and
exits 0. Before building it reads `lake_bronze_{env}.partition_load` for a live
LOADING claim (the ledger's CLAIM_LEASE_MS) or any claim in the last 30 min on
gold's silver inputs. A plain scheduled run then skips with SKIPPED_LOADING and
exits 0 — the next slot builds. A run given --actions or --full-refresh exits 1
(`skipped_loading_targeted`) and prints "Retry after <UTC time>": rerun the same
--args after it, because the deployed args never will. An unreadable ledger
exits 1. No dry-run mode: the CLI's own dry run still writes metadata on
existing targets. The
CLI is `npm ci`'d from `tools/dataform-cli`'s lockfile; npm runs offline against
the core version cached in the image.

### `jobs/lake-release.ts` — cut a release (MONEY-1518, B7)

```bash
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-release.ts --env qa                    # PLAN, writes nothing
LAKE_RELEASE_APPLY=true npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-release.ts --env qa --apply
npx tsx scripts/jobs/lake-release.ts --env qa --verify 2026-09-22T00-00-00Z

# or as the Cloud Run job (setup-lake-jobs.sh) — arms THIS execution only
gcloud run jobs execute lake-release-prod --region=asia-south1 \
  --args="--env,prod,--apply" --update-env-vars=LAKE_RELEASE_APPLY=true
```

**The manifest records what the cut READ, not what the code expects.** Its
`universeRule` (MONEY-1602) states that membership comes from the `listing`
windows — US from Tiingo's dates, India from the first and last bar — and that
`symbol_history` supplies only labels, whose start dates no source knows. Every
file carries its `sha256` (streamed, one object at a time) beside the crc32c.
The adjustment policy is the distinct `adjustment_policy_version` of the
exported rows (`resolveAdjustmentPolicy`), and a cut REFUSES when the rows
disagree with each other or with `ADJUSTMENT_POLICY_VERSION` (`crsp-v3`) — an
immutable artifact naming a policy none of its rows carry could never be
corrected. Every INT64 / NUMERIC column leaves as FLOAT64
(`doubleReplacements`): a NUMERIC reaches pandas as `Decimal`, which `lake.py`'s
intraday adjustment cannot multiply.

`--apply` ASKS and `LAKE_RELEASE_APPLY=true` ARMS; a bare `--apply` continues as
a plan and says so. Immutability is BigQuery's — `EXPORT DATA ... overwrite =
false` fails on an existing object, which a read-then-write would race against a
retried job. `EXPORT DATA` and not `bq extract` because `gold.adjusted_bar_d1`
is a view and extract jobs refuse views; no row passes through Node and export
jobs are free of charge. `--verify` exits **2** on a disagreement (distinct from
1, "could not run") and reports `MISSING` / `UNLISTED` / `SIZE` / `CRC`
separately, because a partial cut and a missing file need opposite fixes.

**It refuses an empty dataset before writing the manifest**, and as of
2026-09-22 that refusal fires on prod: `trade_bar_m1` and `trade_bar_h1` hold
ZERO rows (MONEY-1514/1515 block their loader) and `lake_gold_prod` holds no
`adjusted_bar_d1` at all — Dataform has never run there. `listing` (98,249),
`symbol_history` (98,941), `corporate_action` (59,087) and `price_bar_d1`
(13,649,187) are ready.

## Repair scripts (MONEY-1093, MONEY-1205, MONEY-1207, MONEY-1255, MONEY-1300)

All of them are **dry-run by default** and prompt before any write, echoing the masked
host they are actually connected to. Run from the repo root:

```bash
yarn data:reclassify                    # dry run, ambient DATABASE_URL
yarn data:reclassify --env prod         # dry run against PROD
yarn data:reclassify --env prod --apply # WRITES (prompts; rollback file first)

yarn data:resector                      # dry run
yarn data:resector --env prod --apply   # WRITES (prompts; rollback file first)

# MONEY-1302 — the same shape, for ANY off-taxonomy sector value
yarn data:resector-noncanonical --env prod            # dry run (reports UNMAPPED + skipped bonds)
yarn data:resector-noncanonical --env prod --apply    # WRITES (prompts; rollback file first)

yarn data:repair-names                      # dry run (MONEY-1205)
yarn data:repair-names --env prod           # dry run against PROD
yarn data:repair-names --env prod --apply   # WRITES (prompts; rollback file first)

# MONEY-1207 - no yarn alias yet; invoke directly.
npx tsx scripts/data/reparse-shareholding-raw.ts --env prod          # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/reparse-shareholding-raw.ts --env prod --apply  # WRITES

# MONEY-1255 - the stock-document queue. Prefer the wrapper: it pulls
# DATABASE_URL from Secret Manager instead of having it pasted in by hand, and
# its `docqueue` case declares APPLY_FLAG="--apply".
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh docqueue prod           # dry run
./scripts/ops/run-market-data-job.sh docqueue prod --apply   # WRITES (prompts)

# MONEY-1300 - the stock_financials.basis stamp. No yarn alias yet; invoke
# directly. Dry run prints the census AND what a Prisma `where` can see (0).
npx tsx scripts/data/backfill-financials-basis.ts --env prod          # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/backfill-extract-validation-columns.ts           # dry run + reason census
npx tsx scripts/data/backfill-financials-basis.ts --env prod --apply  # WRITES

# MONEY-1401 B3 - the vendor-row snapshot the XBRL switchover is blocked on.
# No yarn alias yet; invoke directly. Dry runs in BOTH directions write nothing.
# market-data cutover flags (MONEY-1469/1470) — read-only unless --apply
npx tsx scripts/data/set-market-data-flags.ts --env qa                               # read all three
npx tsx scripts/data/set-market-data-flags.ts --env qa --quotes on                   # rehearse (before/after effect)
npx tsx scripts/data/set-market-data-flags.ts --env qa --quotes on --apply           # WRITES
npx tsx scripts/data/set-market-data-flags.ts --env prod --intraday PRO,PREMIUM,ULTIMATE --apply
npx tsx scripts/data/set-market-data-flags.ts --env prod --intraday off --apply      # the ROLLBACK

npx tsx scripts/data/snapshot-vendor-fundamentals.ts --env prod                      # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/snapshot-vendor-fundamentals.ts --env prod --apply              # WRITES the snapshot
npx tsx scripts/data/snapshot-vendor-fundamentals.ts --env prod --restore            # dry run: replace/insert/unchanged
npx tsx scripts/data/snapshot-vendor-fundamentals.ts --env prod --restore --apply    # WRITES the restore

# The corporate_actions repairs, in THIS order; restore in the reverse. `--env` is
# required and pinned (pinJobEnv shells out to gcloud: export
# CLOUDSDK_ACTIVE_CONFIG_NAME=stockbook). Not across 04:30 IST. Each takes the
# same four forms: dry run / --apply --yes / --restore (plan) / --restore --apply --yes.
#   1. reanchor-corporate-action-dates    MONEY-1530   DONE on prod 2026-09-23
#   2. repair-corporate-action-dividends  MONEY-1535   DONE on prod 2026-09-23
#   3. repair-corporate-action-bonus      MONEY-1542
#   4. repair-corporate-action-subjects   MONEY-1598 / 1599 / 1603 (one run, three modes)
#   5. retire-derived-corporate-actions   MONEY-1600
#   6. ONE `corp-action-lake-sink --env prod --apply --reload` (v3), and only once
#      the India in-bhavcopy v2 replay has finished: measured 2026-09-23 the join
#      covers 25.25%, because v2 bars exist only back to ~2006.
npx tsx scripts/data/reanchor-corporate-action-dates.ts --env=prod                            # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/reanchor-corporate-action-dates.ts --env=prod --apply --yes              # WRITES (backup first)
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-dividends.ts --env=prod                          # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-dividends.ts --env=prod --apply --yes            # WRITES (backup first)
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-bonus.ts --env=prod                              # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-bonus.ts --env=prod --apply --yes                # WRITES (backup first)
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-subjects.ts --env=prod                           # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-subjects.ts --env=prod --apply --yes             # WRITES (backup first)
npx tsx scripts/data/retire-derived-corporate-actions.ts --env=prod                           # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/retire-derived-corporate-actions.ts --env=prod --apply --yes             # WRITES (backup first)
npx tsx scripts/data/retire-derived-corporate-actions.ts --env=prod --restore --apply --yes   # undo, FIRST
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-subjects.ts --env=prod --restore --apply --yes    # undo, SECOND
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-bonus.ts --env=prod --restore --apply --yes       # undo, THIRD
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-corporate-action-dividends.ts --env=prod --restore --apply --yes  # undo, FOURTH
npx tsx scripts/data/reanchor-corporate-action-dates.ts --env=prod --restore --apply --yes    # undo, LAST

# MONEY-1531 - instrument_events clocks. After the workers deploy, before
# `event-lake-sink --env prod --reload --apply`. Backs up to
# instrument_events_money1531_backup first; exits 1 if writes were skipped or
# rows remain (old workers still deployed) — do not run the --reload then.
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-instrument-event-clocks.ts --env prod                          # dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-instrument-event-clocks.ts --env prod --apply --yes            # WRITES
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-instrument-event-clocks.ts --env prod --restore                # undo, dry run
npx tsx scripts/data/repair-instrument-event-clocks.ts --env prod --restore --apply --yes  # undo
```

> **`repair-stock-document-queue` is a repair, not the fix.** The two writers
> that produced those rows still run — `workers/src/processors/stock-document-sync.ts`
> weekly and `packages/research/src/data-sources/stock-document-fetcher.ts` from
> the seed script — so the fix is at the WRITE (`normalizeDocMimeType` /
> `initialProcessingStatus`, both applied there in the same change) and this
> script only clears what already accumulated. Run it AFTER that worker is
> deployed, or the next weekly sync rebuilds part of the pile. It is idempotent,
> so a second run after the deploy costs one read and reports nothing to do.

> **`reparse-shareholding-raw` has no upstream dependency and no deploy
> ordering** — unlike `repair-names` above, nothing overwrites what it writes.
> `ownership-sync` upserts `(symbol, quarter)` with the SAME parser this script
> runs, so once the corrected alias list is deployed the nightly job produces the
> identical values; running the script first simply repairs the 7,953 rows
> already stored, which the job would otherwise only refresh as it re-walks them
> (~7 days). Running it BEFORE the deploy is also safe and is strictly better
> than waiting: the columns it fills are null today.

> **`repair-names` MUST run AFTER the parser fix is deployed — MONEY-1205.**
> `ZerodhaInstrumentProvider.mapToUpdate` destructures away only `series` and
> `instrumentType`; **`name` stays in the spread and is rewritten on every
> nightly UPDATE**, so a repair applied against an un-fixed deployment is
> reverted by the 22:00 IST `instrument-sync` within 24 hours — silently. That
> is the MONEY-1093 class exactly. The script does not rely on you remembering:
> it REFUSES `--apply` when it can prove the fix is not live, by checking
> whether the most recent sync itself wrote still-quoted names (a fixed parser
> could not have produced them). Verified against prod on 2026-08-18, where it
> correctly refused — the previous night's sync had re-stamped 12,377 rows.
>
> Two side notes it prints, both benign. **Cross-exchange names:** 2,462 symbols
> exist quoted on one exchange and unquoted on the other, so after the strip some
> become identical across NSE/BSE — measured, that is only **+4** newly-identical
> symbols (241 -> 245), and nothing keys on name uniqueness (`@@unique` is
> `[symbol, exchange]` and `[yahooSymbol]`; `@@index([name])` is non-unique; no
> lookup resolves an instrument by name equality). **Caches:** Atlas Search
> re-indexes from the change stream automatically — no manual re-sync — but the
> 5-minute Redis caches on `/api/market/search` and `/api/market/autocomplete`
> and the per-lambda cache at `apps/web/lib/market/symbol-mapper.ts:135-136`
> serve stale names for a few minutes.

> **~~NSE debt is out of scope for `reclassify`.~~ FIXED by MONEY-1298.** The
> premise still holds and is worth keeping: the bhavcopy parser drops every NSE
> debt series (`GS/TB/SG`, `N*/Y*/Z*`) at parse time via `keepSeries`, nothing
> else supplies one, so the `series` COLUMN on NSE bonds sits at `"EQ"` forever
> and will never "accumulate". What changed is that the column is no longer the
> only place the series lives. NSE lists a non-main-board instrument as
> `<BASE>-<SERIES>` and `extractBaseSymbol` returns the trading symbol unchanged
> for NSE/BSE, so the series is sitting in `symbol`: `664UP30-SG`,
> `667GS2050-GS`, `364D280826-TB`, `965SCL25C-N0`. `classifyCashInstrument`
> recovers it — only when the stored series is the stamped default AND the row
> has no ISIN — and feeds it to the SAME tables, so 6,172 NSE rows are now
> decided instead of invisible. The 876 `-SM`/`-BE`/`-ST`/`-BZ`/`-IT`/`-SZ`/
> `-E1` rows come back EQUITY from the same tables and are left alone.

> **The real cash-equity universe is 5,610 symbols** (MONEY-1298, measured on
> prod 2026-08-30). 8,083 ISIN-bearing rows — 3,184 NSE + 4,899 BSE — against
> 20,850 rows typed `EQUITY`, so ~61% of that table was never equity. Read
> "ISIN coverage 8,083/20,850" as **100% of the real set**, not as a broken
> enricher. The dry run's classification table is the authority: on prod it now
> reads DEBT 13,901 · EQUITY 8,913 · INDEX 213 · UNKNOWN 191 · INVIT 31 ·
> ETF 17 · REIT 12 over 23,278 active EQUITY+DEBT rows, with 11,686 rows to
> change.

> **`INDEX` joined the retype set; `INDEX` is not a new type.** 213 rows carry
> Kite's own `segment: "INDICES"` — 136 NSE (`NIFTY 50`, `INDIA VIX`) and 77 BSE
> (`SENSEX`, `BANKEX`, `AUTO`) — and every one was typed `EQUITY`. The REIT/InvIT
> exclusion does not apply to them: nobody holds an index, there are no filings
> to drop, and prod holds **zero** `INDEX` rows today, so `getIndices` and
> `getInstrumentsBySymbolsAction` both return an empty list. This retype ADDS a
> surface rather than removing one.

> **DO NOT deactivate or delete the NSE suffixed twins.** 870 `SYM-SM`/`-BE`/
> `-ST`/`-BZ` rows sit beside a bare ISIN-bearing `SYM`, and they look like
> duplicates worth tidying. They are not tidy-able: 446 of them carry **13,917
> `daily_prices` rows**, 455 `latest_quotes` and 31 `stock_news` on the SUFFIXED
> `_id`. `DailyPrice.instrumentId` is a REQUIRED relation, so a dangling
> reference THROWS and blanks every result (see the `required_relation_orphans`
> note). Prices landed on BOTH rows — all 851 bare rows have history too. The
> classifier types both EQUITY and nothing here touches them; consolidating them
> is a separate ticket that has to move the price rows first.

Shared flags: `--env qa|prod` (DATABASE_URL from Secret Manager), `--limit N`
(positive integer — a malformed value is REFUSED rather than silently meaning
"every row"), `--apply`.

> **CACHE LAG after `resector`.** An earlier version of this note said to run the
> script BEFORE deploying the cache-key bump. That is not executable: the bumps
> live in `apps/web`/`packages/services` and web auto-deploys from `main`, so the
> new keys are live the moment the PR merges — before an operator can run
> anything. What actually happens: the first request after deploy fills the new
> key with the PRE-remediation universe, and it stays for the TTL. The
> `discover` namespace has a 24h TTL and no `deleteCached`, so if the deploy
> lands first the very next request fills the new key with the PRE-remediation
> universe and it sits there for a day — mobile keeps showing the old buckets
> while web refreshes in minutes, which reads as "mobile is broken". The key
> bump only buys anything if the data is already correct when the new key is
> first populated.

Read the dry-run output before applying. Both print what they matched and why:
`reclassify` lists classification reasons and sample rows; `resector` lists
**every distinct industry string it matched**, the near-misses it rejected, and
the ambiguous rows it deliberately did NOT move. That listing is the point —
these decide from free text, and on this ticket a free-text heuristic already
hit 2,236 wrong rows.

`--apply` writes `reclassify-rollback-<ts>.json` / `resector-rollback-<ts>.json`
before touching anything, into **`scripts/data/rollbacks/`** — repo-relative
(derived from `import.meta.url`), NOT the cwd, since MONEY-1204. The artefacts
stay gitignored; the directory's committed `README.md` records what each one
reverses and how to replay it. Keep them: `sector`/`industry` exist nowhere else
and a retyped row is excluded from re-enrichment, so that file is the only
recovery path — and `git clean -fdx` would delete it.

> **Why these are scripts and not worker jobs.** The repair for rows going
> FORWARD is already automated: `bhavcopy-sync` types a row DEBT/ETF from the
> exchange's own series the night it trades, and — for DEBT only — clears the
> bogus sector in the same write. A bond first seen tomorrow needs neither
> script.
>
> These exist for the EXISTING backlog and for the parts a Cloud Run job handles
> badly. `reclassify` still reaches rows the nightly job cannot (not traded since
> deploy, or already typed but carrying a sector that predates the change), and
> it writes a rollback snapshot the worker cannot — Cloud Run's disk is
> ephemeral. `resector` decides from FREE TEXT, and its whole design is a human
> reading the match list before anything moves. Neither can prompt from a job.

---

## US EDGAR gates (MONEY-979)

Three scripts, run **after a deploy**, to prove the SEC EDGAR branch did what it
claimed. Two of them read the database and one exercises the QA pipeline
end-to-end without touching sec.gov.

```bash
# 1. MEASURE — coverage, row counts, CUSIP tiers, detection latency
npx tsx scripts/data/edgar-coverage-report.ts               # table
npx tsx scripts/data/edgar-coverage-report.ts --json        # machine-readable
npx tsx scripts/data/edgar-coverage-report.ts --fail-under=95   # as a gate

# 2. ASSERT — the branch's stated acceptance, for AAPL / MSFT / BRK.B
npx tsx scripts/data/edgar-acceptance.ts
npx tsx scripts/data/edgar-acceptance.ts --json

# 3. PROVE THE PIPELINE — one synthetic filing through QA, zero SEC traffic
./scripts/ops/edgar-qa-synthetic-filing.sh qa --dry-run
./scripts/ops/edgar-qa-synthetic-filing.sh qa
```

**Which database do 1 and 2 read?** Both load the repo `.env` (`dotenv`, path
resolved from the SCRIPT and not from the cwd), which points at **QA** — so the
commands above work as written, from anywhere. Neither loaded it when they
shipped, and neither could run at all: `@usemoney/database` builds its
PrismaClient from `process.env`, `npx tsx` supplies nothing, and the copy-paste
command produced ten `prisma:error … Environment variable not found:
DATABASE_URL` lines and exit 2 — a release gate failing in a shape that reads
like an empty database rather than an absent one.

`dotenv.config` does not override an exported variable, so **gating a PROD
release means exporting `DATABASE_URL` first**:

```bash
DATABASE_URL="$(gcloud secrets versions access latest \
  --secret=DATABASE_URL_PROD --project=stock-book-483718)" \
  npx tsx scripts/data/edgar-coverage-report.ts --fail-under=95
```

`--json` writes JSON and nothing else to stdout (dotenv's v17 banner is
suppressed with `quiet: true`; it prints on **stdout**, so without that every
consumer's parse throws). `--help` needs no database at all — the Prisma import
is deferred into `main()` for exactly that reason.

### `data/edgar-coverage-report.ts` — the release gate

Read-only. Reports filers (total / `inUniverse` / `eligibleForCoverage`), rows
per collection, `us_fundamentals` per statement and period type, `us_filings`
per status and `parseClass`, the CUSIP map per resolution tier with the
unresolved share, and detection latency (`acceptedAt` → `detectedAt`).

**The gate's denominator is ELIGIBLE filers, and that is the point.** A gate
reading "95% of filers have 5+ years" fails every quarter a batch of SPACs
lists — a company that IPO'd last year cannot have five years of filings, so
that gate measures the MARKET rather than us. `UsFiler.eligibleForCoverage`
splits them: eligible filers are judged against the five-year bar; everyone else
is SATISFIED when they hold all the years they have and is counted separately.

**Two coverage numbers, because their DIFFERENCE is the diagnosis.** `gate`
needs income + balance sheet + cash flow for a fiscal year to count;
`gateIncomeOnly` needs only the income statement and is exactly what
`getFilerCoverage()` in `@usemoney/core/us-fundamentals` computes. A large gap
means the balance-sheet or cash-flow leg of `parse_financials` is not landing,
while a report built on income statements alone would call that universe fully
covered. The script also cross-checks its own income-only figure against the
seam's and prints a disagreement rather than reconciling it silently.

**An empty database prints zeros and exits 0, and does NOT claim 100%.**
`judgeCoverage([])` returns 100 deliberately — a gate over an empty population
is vacuously satisfied, and the alternatives are worse (0 fails a release for a
market condition; `NaN` fails silently in both directions, since `NaN >= 95` is
false and so is `NaN < 95`). But "vacuously satisfied" is not "95% covered", so
the script reads `eligible === 0` and reports the named state **NO_DATA** with
`pct: null`. With `--fail-under` on an empty database the gate is NOT EVALUATED
and the exit is 0, said out loud.

`--fail-under` accepts both `--fail-under=95` and `--fail-under 95`, and a value
that will not parse is a **hard error (exit 2)**, never a skipped gate: the
failure mode of a silently mis-parsed threshold is a script that prints a
percentage, exits 0 and is believed.

Exit codes: `0` measured, `1` gate FAILED, `2` the script failed.

### `data/edgar-acceptance.ts` — absence IS failure

Reads AAPL, MSFT and BRK.B **through the repository**
(`@usemoney/core/us-fundamentals`), never raw Prisma — the point is that the
SERVING SEAM works, and a direct query would prove the writer ran and nothing
about the layer users read through. Prints `accession` and `filedAt` on every
row.

Five legs, all required: the three statements (latest annual and latest
quarterly), the point-in-time property, insider transactions over 90 days, top
13F holders with the QoQ delta, and the filing ledger by form. 13D/G is reported
but **not** required — a mega-cap can legitimately have no 13D/G, since a 5%
stake in Apple is ~$170bn.

**The point-in-time probe walks backwards through the seam.** To assert "a date
between two filings returns the older one" you first need two filings of one
period, and finding them with a raw query would defeat the paragraph above. So
it uses `asOf` alone: read the current view, re-read as of `filedAt - 1 ms`
(which excludes that filing by construction, so a different accession for the
same `periodEnd` IS its predecessor), then read the MIDPOINT between the two
`filedAt`s and assert the older accession comes back. Both filings are printed.

Two point-in-time failures stay separately named, because they are two different
facts: `POINT_IN_TIME_RETURNED_NEWER` is a broken seam, and
`POINT_IN_TIME_NO_MULTI_FILING_PERIOD` is a database with no restated period to
test against. Both exit 1 — an acceptance script that could not demonstrate the
property must not report that it holds.

This is the script whose empty database is a **failure**, and the contrast with
the coverage report is deliberate: one MEASURES, one ASSERTS.

Exit codes: `0` everything present, `1` something missing (each named), `2` the
script failed.

### `ops/edgar-qa-synthetic-filing.sh` — the pipeline, with no SEC traffic

QA only, and it refuses prod. QA runs `SEC_EGRESS=off`, so it cannot fetch a
filing to test with; this script puts a committed fixture bundle
(`services/us-fundamentals/tests/fixtures/form4_jefferies.txt.gz`, sha256-checked
against the fixture manifest) at the exact key `SecClient.fetch_bundle` reads
FIRST — `edgar/filings/{cik10}/{accession}/submission.txt` — publishes one
`edgar-parse-ownership` message, polls `us_insider_transactions`, and prints the
measured latency.

**The zero-egress assertion has a verified ceiling.** "Grep the logs, find no
`SecEgressDisabled`, declare no network attempts" is the shape that hid the
Tickertape block for five months: a check whose pass is indistinguishable from
its never having run. So the script (a) reads `SEC_EGRESS` off the LIVE service
and refuses unless it is the literal string `off` — with egress allowed, a
clean grep means the fetch was PERMITTED; (b) bounds the log window to a
timestamp captured before the publish; and (c) requires a POSITIVE CONTROL —
some log lines in the window — before a zero-hit grep counts as a pass. Zero
lines is **INCONCLUSIVE**, never PASS.

It preflights `EDGAR_PARSE_APPLY` and refuses when it is not `true`, printing
the arming command rather than flipping a release flag as a side effect of a
test: with the flag off, the handler parses fully, counts honestly and writes
nothing, so the poll would time out against a service that is working perfectly.

`--dry-run` prints every command shell-quoted and touches nothing. There is no
destructive path in the script at all — the uploaded object is left in place,
which makes a re-run idempotent and costs one 14 KB object.

### What guards these

`packages/core/__tests__/us-fundamentals/coverage-gate.test.ts` drives the pure
decisions of both TypeScript scripts (`decideGate`, `parseArgs`, `percentile`,
`judgeAcceptance`) — which is why both files call `main()` only when they are
the process entry point. It also pins the TypeScript `judgeCoverage` and the
Python `judge_coverage` in `services/us-fundamentals/app/report/coverage.py` to
ONE shared contract, `services/us-fundamentals/tests/coverage_cases.json`, read
by both that suite and `services/us-fundamentals/tests/test_coverage_report.py`.
Two languages computing one gate is exactly how they drift.

---

## Running scripts safely

### TypeScript scripts (`.ts`)

```bash
# Use tsx (configured via scripts/tsconfig.json)
DATABASE_URL="mongodb+srv://..." npx tsx scripts/seed/seed-instruments.ts

# For GCP-managed envs, fetch URL from Secret Manager
DATABASE_URL="$(gcloud secrets versions access latest --secret=DATABASE_URL_QA --project=stock-book-483718)" \
  npx tsx scripts/migrations/migrate-resend-segments.ts
```

### Shell scripts (`.sh`)

```bash
./scripts/seed-env.sh qa
./scripts/ops/setup-infra.sh prod --skip-deploy
source scripts/ops/test-env.sh && yarn test:integration
```

---

## Conventions

- **Idempotency first**: migrations should detect already-applied state and
  no-op.
- **`--dry-run` for migrations**: always support a preview mode for anything
  that writes.
- **Rate-limit external APIs**: use `await sleep(125)` minimum for Resend (8
  req/sec safe under their 10/sec limit). Yahoo needs 2s between calls.
- **Never hardcode prod credentials**: fetch via
  `gcloud secrets versions access`.
- **Short logs with context prefix**: `[seed-docs]`, `[reconcile]`, etc. - see
  existing scripts for pattern.

---

## Test-runner flags — `--test-force-exit` is banned

`--test-force-exit` makes `node --test` exit the instant the top-level run
reports complete, **killing any test file still in flight**. It does not fail;
it just reports fewer tests, and CI goes green either way.

Measured (MONEY-1083) — same command, same shell, only the flag differing:

| workspace  | with the flag | without    |
| ---------- | ------------- | ---------- |
| `apps/web` | 15,552        | **15,844** |

292 tests silently dropped from one run of the largest suite, and _which_ 292
varied: three consecutive runs on an unchanged tree gave 15,691 / 15,712 /
15,797, every one reporting `# fail 0`. A green suite was therefore not
evidence that a given test had run — which undercut `yarn verify:push` and CI
alike.

The flag was originally there to stop orphaned handles wedging a run. That job
belongs to **`scripts/test-timeout.ts`**, the wall-clock watchdog every test
script routes through: it SIGKILLs the whole process group after
`TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 15 min). It is strictly better, because it kills a
genuinely stuck run instead of truncating a healthy one.

**If a suite starts hanging, fix the orphaning test** (clear the timer, close
the client) — do not reintroduce the flag. `apps/web/__tests__/meta/no-test-force-exit.test.ts`
fails the suite if it comes back.

---

## MONEY-1236 — three scripts that could not see their own target population

Prisma's MongoDB connector does **not** treat a missing field as null.
`{ field: null }` is an equality against an explicit JSON null, so it matches
ONLY documents that actually CARRY the key. `prisma db push` creates INDEXES,
never FIELDS, so a column added after rows already existed is _absent_ on every
one of them — not null. `isSet: false` is the only Prisma spelling that reaches
an absent key (it lowers to `$exists: false`); a bare `null`, `{ equals: null }`
and `{ not: null }` all do not.

Fifth occurrence of the class (MONEY-1050, 1205, 1234, 1235, 1236). Measured on
prod 2026-08-21:

| Field                             | Total | Absent | Explicit null | Set |
| --------------------------------- | ----- | ------ | ------------- | --- |
| `BrokerConnection.syncBucket`     | 9     | 1      | 0             | 8   |
| `Subscription.cancelledAt`        | 652   | 650    | 1             | 1   |
| `Subscription.proEntitlementType` | 652   | 651    | 0             | 1   |

### What changed

| Script                                   | Was                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Now                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `ops/backfill-syncbucket.ts`             | Selected and updated on `{ syncBucket: null }`, so every pre-MONEY-841 connection was invisible to its own backfill and stayed outside the sharded sync fan-out (`where.syncBucket = N` matches no absent field). Printed "Nothing to backfill" and exited 0. | One shared `NO_BUCKET_OR` predicate (`null` OR `isSet: false`) drives both the SELECT and the per-user UPDATE, so they cannot drift apart. Adds a raw `$exists`-semantics cross-check; exits **non-zero** if any connection is still unbucketed. |
| `migrations/fix-premium-enddate.ts`      | Selected on `cancelledAt: null` **and verified with the same filter**, so it printed "Nothing to fix." then "0 remaining" and exited 0 — a double false success over 650 invisible rows.                                                                      | `NEVER_CANCELLED_OR` for the selection. `endDate: { not: null }` is deliberately left alone — it means "has an endDate to clear", where excluding absent is the intent. Verification moved to `$runCommandRaw`.                                  |
| `migrations/backfill-pro-entitlement.ts` | Selected on `proEntitlementType: null` **and verified with the same filter**. On prod it could see exactly **zero** rows, since nothing carries an explicit null.                                                                                             | `NO_ENTITLEMENT_OR` for the selection, `$runCommandRaw` for the verification.                                                                                                                                                                    |

### The same class, INVERTED: a `$exists` query that matches too much

Every case above is a predicate that matched too FEW rows. `migrations/backfill-edgar-row-timestamps.ts`
caught the mirror image in its own dry run, and it is the more dangerous
direction because the failure is a WRITE rather than a no-op.

The script began with one fixed predicate for every collection:

```ts
const NEEDS_FILL = {
  $or: [{ ingestedAt: { $exists: false } }, { updatedAt: { $exists: false } }],
};
```

`$exists: false` is exactly right — and the models are not all the same. Seven
EDGAR models declare `ingestedAt`; the Tiingo models beside them declare
`createdAt` and have no `ingestedAt` at all. So for those, the first arm matched
**every row in the collection**: 2,948,700 `us_corporate_actions`, 74,155
`us_instruments`, 8,825 `us_news_articles`. All healthy. Applied, the script
would have stamped a fabricated `ingestedAt` onto 2.9M rows and created the
field it was checking for.

The fix is to derive the field list PER MODEL by parsing `schema.prisma` for
fields that are REQUIRED **and** carry a client-side default, so a model
contributes only the columns it actually declares. The general rule:

> A migration that walks N collections needs N predicates, not one. A predicate
> written against one model's schema and applied to another's is not a filter —
> it is a match-all wearing a filter's shape.

Two things made this visible rather than expensive, and both are cheap to keep:
**dry-run by default** (the counts were printed before anything was written),
and **printing per-collection counts rather than a total** — a single
`3,800,000 rows to fix` against an expected 785k would have looked like a
bigger win, not a bug.

### The durable half: verification must not share the selection's filter

A verify that re-runs the selection's predicate can only ever confirm the
selection's own blind spot, no matter how many times it runs. All three scripts
now count through `$runCommandRaw`, where the semantics are **inverted in our
favour** — raw Mongo's `{ field: null }` _does_ match a missing key. So the raw
predicate is precisely the bare `null` that was wrong on the Prisma side, and if
a Prisma OR ever regressed the two routes would **disagree** instead of agreeing
on a comfortable zero.

Two details worth copying:

- The `$facet` also re-counts the **whole collection** and compares it against
  Prisma's own total. `aggregate` against a collection name that does not exist
  returns an empty result, **not an error** — so a future `@@map()` would
  silently turn the verify into a hard-coded zero, which is the exact failure
  mode being fixed. On a mismatch it throws.
- The pre-flight disagreement check is **asymmetric on purpose**: only
  `raw > prisma` (rows exist that Prisma cannot see) aborts. A strict `!==`
  would risk halting a correct run over an unrelated nuance in the harmless
  direction, and an ops script that refuses to run is its own outage.

`migrations/stamp-welcome-email-backfill.ts` was written to this pattern from
the start — its verification re-counts the population with the `isSet`-aware
filter rather than trusting the write loop. It is the shape the other two have
now converged on.

### The class guard

`apps/web/__tests__/meta/prisma-null-vs-isset.test.ts` scans `apps/`,
`packages/`, `workers/` and `scripts/` and fails when a Prisma `where` compares
an **optional** scalar to a bare `null` without an adjacent `isSet` arm.
`{ not: null }` is deliberately never flagged (53 such filters exist and all are
correct), raw Mongo bodies are structurally out of scope, and a legitimate bare
null carries `// prisma-null-ok: <why>`. Sites that predate the guard sit in a
classified `KNOWN_UNFIXED` list that fails the build in **both** directions — a
new offender fails, and so does a listed entry that has since been fixed, so the
list can only shrink.

---

## `migrations/stamp-welcome-email-backfill.ts` (MONEY-1236)

Stamps `welcomeEmailSentAt` on existing users so nobody who already exists can
receive a **late** welcome email.

It is the zero-tail option and is deliberately **not required**: the route
(`apps/web/app/api/webhooks/clerk/route.ts`) now bounds the claim by account age
(`WELCOME_BACKFILL_MAX_AGE_DAYS`, 30 days), so that change is safe to deploy on
its own with no ordering dependency on this script. Run this only if you want
nobody who already exists to get a late welcome — not even the recent signups
the age gate would allow.

- **Dry run by default**; `--apply` writes.
- `--older-than-days=N` stamps only accounts older than N days. Pass the same N
  the route uses and the two agree exactly.
- Stamps each account's **own `createdAt`**, not `now()`. A watermark claiming
  "sent just now" for a January signup is a lie a later audit would have to
  unpick.
- Its dry run **prints the defect**: measured on prod 2026-08-21 it reports
  `unstamped (isSet:false OR null): 653` beside `what the BROKEN filter sees: 0`.
- Its verification re-counts the population with the `isSet`-aware filter rather
  than trusting the write loop — deliberately unlike the two migrations above
  before they were fixed, which verified with the same broken filter they
  selected with and so certified their own blind spot.

---

## MONEY-1300 — `data/backfill-financials-basis.ts`

Sixth occurrence of the class above (MONEY-1050, 1205, 1234, 1235, 1236), and
the first one where the fix is a **value**, not a filter.

`StockFinancials.basis` (CONSOLIDATED | STANDALONE) is written by the XBRL
projection on every create and update. Measured on prod 2026-08-31:

| Column  | Rows   | Absent | Explicit null | Set |
| ------- | ------ | ------ | ------------- | --- |
| `basis` | 32,359 | 32,359 | 0             | 0   |

All 32,359 carry `source: "TICKERTAPE"`. The column exists in the schema; a
Mongo `prisma db push` creates INDEXES, never FIELDS, so no row has the
attribute at all.

### Why the stamp is `"UNKNOWN"` and not `null`

Because `null` would fix nothing measurable. Three readers, one column:

| Reader                                        | absent | explicit `null` | `"UNKNOWN"` |
| --------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------- | ----------- |
| Prisma `{ basis: null }`                      | miss   | **hit**         | miss        |
| Prisma `{ basis: { not: null } }`             | miss   | **miss**        | hit         |
| Prisma `{ basis: { in: [CONSOLIDATED, …] } }` | miss   | **miss**        | hit         |

An explicit null moves the row from one unreadable state to another: it still
reads as "no basis" through `{ not: null }` and through the positive `in` list
the schema comment on `StockRatios.returnBasis` recommends. A NAMED value makes
all three states separable with one ordinary equality — `"UNKNOWN"` = vendor
row, basis not recorded; `CONSOLIDATED`/`STANDALONE` = the projection stamped
it; ABSENT = a row nothing has touched yet, which stays meaningful precisely
because the backfill never writes null.

### What it copies from the MONEY-1236 pattern, and what it adds

- **Selection is raw** — `$runCommandRaw` with `$exists: false`, the
  `ops/backfill-user-isactive.ts` pattern. Prisma cannot express this
  population: `{ isSet: false }` is the only spelling that reaches an absent
  key, and the raw operator says what it means to a reader arriving from the
  Prisma side.
- **One filter document** — `stampFilter()` drives the census, the samples and
  the write. A selection and an update that state the same rule twice is what
  made `ops/backfill-syncbucket.ts` print "Nothing to backfill" over a
  population it could not see.
- **Verification goes through the OTHER route** — but the direction is
  reversed from the three scripts above, and deliberately. They select with
  Prisma and verify with raw; this one selects with raw and verifies with
  **Prisma** (`count({ where: { basis: "UNKNOWN" } })`). The adversary is a
  write that lands `basis: null`: the raw "still missing" count would go to
  ZERO and report success, because the attribute would then EXIST. Only the
  Prisma equality notices that nothing readable was written.
- **The dry run prints the defect** — the raw `$exists:false` count beside
  `what a Prisma where can see`, which reads 0 against 32,359. Same move as
  `migrations/stamp-welcome-email-backfill.ts`.

### What it refuses

`NSE_XBRL` rows are excluded by a `$nin` against `XBRL_SOURCE` — imported, not
restated — and **counted in their own bucket** rather than folded into the
total. The projection owns those rows' basis; if one is missing, the projection
is what should supply it. Rows carrying an EXPLICIT null are outside
`$exists: false`'s reach by construction, counted separately, and zero on prod;
widening to reach them is a decision somebody makes, not a side effect of
swapping an operator.

It never calls `mayOverwriteFinancials`, and that is not an omission. The write
is gated on the cell being ABSENT, so there is nothing to displace, and it
touches no owned figure and never rewrites `source`. The authority question is
answered by the filter rather than after the fact.

### It is joined to step 1, by a type

`UNKNOWN_BASIS` is annotated with the READER's `StockFinancialsBasis`
(`@usemoney/core/market-data/financials-basis`, MONEY-1300 step 1), so a
vocabulary change there stops this writer COMPILING rather than letting it stamp
a value every reader would collapse to "Basis not stated" while believing it had
been told something. The test asserts the round trip
(`normalizeStockFinancialsBasis(UNKNOWN_BASIS) === "UNKNOWN"`), which also
records the ordering property: step 1 already collapses ABSENT and `"UNKNOWN"`
to one reader state, so this backfill changes what the COLUMN says without
changing what the page RENDERS — safe to run before or after that deploy.

**Idempotent structurally**, not by checkpoint: the selection is "basis absent"
and the write gives those rows a basis, so a second pass matches zero and
modifies zero. There is nothing to reset after an interrupted run.

Pinned by `packages/core/__tests__/market-data/financials-basis-backfill.test.ts`,
which evaluates the script's real filters against a fixture containing every
shape the column can be in, through one evaluator modelling raw Mongo semantics
and a second modelling Prisma's stricter ones — the two disagreeing on the same
fixture IS the defect, reproduced.

---

## `scripts/` is type-checked now (MONEY-1238)

`yarn type-check` used to be exactly `turbo run type-check`, and turbo reaches
only WORKSPACES (`apps/*`, `packages/*`, `workers`). This directory is none of
those, so **nothing type-checked it** — not the nightly Cloud Run jobs
(`jobs/feed-parse.ts`, `jobs/xbrl-backfill.ts`, `jobs/xbrl-projection-drain.ts`),
not the F3 reconcile whose verdict gates the XBRL switchover, not the one-shot
migrations that write to production. `build` cannot cover for it: tsup and Next
compile through esbuild, which strips types without checking them.

A `scripts/tsconfig.json` had existed the whole time, correct and `noEmit`, with
no caller. Config without a caller reads as coverage.

| Command                                   | What it does                                |
| ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| `yarn type-check`                         | workspaces (turbo) **&&** `scripts/`        |
| `yarn type-check:scripts`                 | `scripts/` alone                            |
| `… typecheck-scripts.ts --write-baseline` | re-record the allowance after fixing errors |

### The baseline is a RATCHET

Switching the check on surfaced **34 pre-existing errors across 14 files**,
several in migrations that have already run against production. A gate that is
red the day it lands is a gate somebody disables, so
`ops/typecheck-scripts-baseline.json` records what predates it, keyed on
`(file, TS error code)` — never on line numbers, which move on every edit above
them and would make the baseline red on unrelated changes.

Three rules, all enforced by exit code:

- a file **not** in the baseline may have **zero** errors;
- a file in the baseline may not exceed its recorded count for any code;
- a file with **fewer** errors than recorded also FAILS, telling you to lower
  the baseline. Without that arm the allowance never shrinks and a fixed file
  quietly re-earns it.

**Never add a new error to the baseline.** It records only what predates the
gate. `packages/core/__tests__/ops/scripts-typecheck-gate.test.ts` pins the
wiring (the root script, the tsconfig's `include`/`exclude`/`noEmit`, and that
no workspace file or XBRL script has acquired an allowance).

---

## `jobs/doc-ingest-backfill.ts` — the PDF archive, end to end (MONEY-1314)

Provisioned by **`ops/setup-doc-ingest-backfill-job.sh {qa|prod}`** as the Cloud
Run job `doc-ingest-backfill-{env}`.

> **NOT YET IN `workers/tsup.config.ts`.** At the time of writing there is no
> `doc-ingest-backfill` entry, so `dist/doc-ingest-backfill.js` is not in the
> workers image and the job cannot start. The setup script **refuses to
> provision** until the entry exists — a hard preflight, not the warning its
> siblings print — and names the line to add. The same script refuses while
> `@firecrawl/pdf-inspector` is undeclared in `workers/package.json`, because
> `scan-convert` is 309.8 GB of the work and cannot convert one document
> without it. Add both, let the release trigger rebuild, then provision.

### The five subcommands

The subcommand is the FIRST execution argument, so one job spec serves all five
(`--args="scan-convert,--apply"`).

| Subcommand     | Reads                                     | Writes                                                  |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| `scan-convert` | GCS PDF bytes                             | `${gcsPath}.md` sidecar + `convertStatus` / page stamps |
| `batch-build`  | the sidecars                              | one JSONL of `buildBatchRequestLine` lines (local file) |
| `batch-submit` | that JSONL                                | Files API upload + a batch job; rows stamped `BATCHED`  |
| `batch-ingest` | a finished batch's results                | `document_financial_extracts` + `parseStatus`           |
| `sweep`        | `FAILED_BATCH` / `FAILED_VALIDATION` rows | the same two, via the ONLINE path                       |

`batch-ingest --compare` is how the pilot is judged: it grades the batch's
answer against the extracts already on the row and writes a report. It **never**
writes to the database, which is why `--compare` and `--apply` together are an
argument error rather than a preference — an upsert would destroy the baseline
the comparison is measured against.

`batch-build --pilot-from-parsed=N` deals those N documents ACROSS SYMBOLS —
at most `--pilot-max-per-symbol` (default 2) from any one company, narrowable
with `--pilot-doc-types`. The 2026-09-02 pilot drew 25 documents from six
companies and its failure rates described those six rather than the corpus
(MONEY-1344). `scan-convert` takes `--shard=i/n` and `--concurrency=N`, both
defaulted from the Cloud Run task variables and `DOC_CONVERT_CONCURRENCY`
(MONEY-1339).

`batch-build --pilot-from-parsed=N` selects N documents that ALREADY hold an
`EXTRACTED` row, so the pilot has a known answer. The population is taken from
the extract table rather than from `parseStatus`: the status is a claim about
the rows, the rows are the evidence.

### Exit codes

| Code | Meaning                                                                                                |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `0`  | finished the work in scope — **including "there was none to do"**                                      |
| `1`  | refused to run: a bad or missing argument. Nothing ran.                                                |
| `2`  | something the run needs is absent or broke (no converter, no API key, a batch that is not `SUCCEEDED`) |
| `3`  | stopped on the wall clock or the batch cap; work remains. Re-run.                                      |

There is deliberately **no code for "nothing to do"**. Once the corpus is
converted, an empty queue is the normal state, and a non-zero exit for it trains
an operator to ignore the one signal that matters. What separates "nothing to
do" from "the selector is broken" is not the exit code — it is `selected`
beside `remaining` in the `SUMMARY` line, both counted with the same predicate.

Exit `3` resumes for **free**: the stamps on the rows are the cursor
(`scannedPageCount` for the conversion, `batchJobId` for the ingest). There is
no checkpoint table and none is wanted.

### Four rules this job is shaped by

- **Dry run is the default and `--apply` is forwarded, never swallowed.** The
  setup script has no `--apply` case at all — it is an EXECUTION argument. A
  wrapper that eats it is how `feed-repair` read 41,924 rows, wrote nothing and
  exited 0. The resolved state is printed on every run, whichever way it went.
- **Counters are writes, not attempts.** A write counter can only move inside
  `countedWrite()`, after the awaited operation resolves.
  `packages/core/__tests__/ops/doc-ingest-backfill-job-setup.test.ts` asserts
  the file contains exactly one such mutation.
- **The run is bounded by a wall clock** (`--budget-sec`, default 6 h), checked
  BETWEEN documents. Per-document cost is a GCS read plus a conversion
  dominated by a fixed ~150–200 ms and unpredictable from page count (a
  123-page image-based PDF ran 12.0 s), so a row budget is a guess.
- **Memory is 2Gi and 1Gi is a hard floor.** Peak RSS is 2.3–4.8× file size,
  measured, worst observed 618 MB for a 129.81 MB PDF; 109 archive objects
  exceed 40 MB. The setup script refuses a `--memory` below the floor, because
  a smaller task is OOM-killed mid-page and the kill takes the `SUMMARY` line
  with it.

### Order of operations

```bash
JOB=doc-ingest-backfill-prod; REGION=asia-south1

# 1. convert — resumable, run it as many times as you like
gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=$REGION \
  --args="scan-convert,--apply,--budget-sec=21600,--limit=250"

# 2. the pilot — 25 documents with a known answer
gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=$REGION --wait \
  --args="batch-build,--apply,--pilot-from-parsed=25,--out=/tmp/pilot.jsonl"
gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=$REGION --wait \
  --args="batch-submit,--apply,--file=/tmp/pilot.jsonl"
gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=$REGION --wait \
  --args="batch-ingest,--batch-job=batches/XXXX,--compare,--report=/tmp/report.json"

# 3. READ THE REPORT — the disagreements, against the filings. Then the tail.
# 4. the residue
gcloud run jobs execute $JOB --region=$REGION --args="sweep,--apply,--limit=200"
```

`/tmp` inside a Cloud Run task does not survive the execution, so a
multi-execution pilot needs the JSONL on GCS or the three steps run from one
long-lived execution.

Costs and the measured population are in
`docs/reference/doc-ingest-population.md`; the converter findings — including
the five documents that classify `TextBased` at full confidence and extract to
zero characters — are in `docs/reference/doc-convert-decision.md`.

## Maintenance rule

**When modifying `scripts/`:**

1. Add or rename a script → update the table above with purpose + notes.
2. Change a script's interface (CLI flags, required env vars) → update the
   table.
3. Retire a migration → move to `archive/` (or delete with git-log reference)
   and add a "Ran on prod YYYY-MM" status note.
4. Cross-references in `docs/reference/scripts-reference.md` and
   `docs/ops/PRODUCTION-CHECKLIST.md` may also need updating if the change is
   externally visible.

The enforcement is social, not automated. Keep this README honest.
